The subroutines generated under Location Counter 1 are, in general, routines which update 'pointer words'."}],"proposed_annotation":"[Append to Open Question 40, line 4203:]\n\n**Narrowed (90.02 mining, 2026-08-01):** Appendix 90.02 supplies no DO linkage at all — the string \"DO\" occurs nowhere in its 35 sections (J 90.02.00–90.02.33), which document generated code only for MOVE (MOVPAK), arithmetic scaling, comparison, GET/FILE, OPEN/CLOSE and STOP; and its eight classes of compiler-generated name (RS Result Storage, TS Temporary Storage, BL Base Locator, PI Positional Indicator, CP Constant Pool, GN Generated Name, SYS System Subroutine, IOC I/O Subroutine or Communication) include no return-linkage class, so a DO return cell can only be a GN-named word or a cell in the TS pool, never a category of its own (J 90.02.03). What the appendix does fix is the generic convention any DO code had to obey. Every generated call is a TSX tagged with index register 4, including calls to the compiler's *own* in-line code: \"TSX GN)019,4\" reaching the generated label \"GN)019 CLA PI)3\" (J 90.02.06), alongside \"TSX SYS)182,4\" and \"TSX IOC)9,4\"; parameters travel in IR1 (\"TXI SYS)243,1,84\", J 90.02.06). Returns are then written as displacements off that same linkage, stated explicitly once: \"If an error is detected during conversion, return is 1,4 to routine SYS)263 which prints an error message\" — the sequence being \"TSX SYS)261, 4 / TSX SYS)263, 6 / (Normal Return)\", so the error return lands at 1,4 and the normal return at 2,4 (J 90.02.29). MOVPAK's four entry points make the displacement arithmetic unambiguous: with two parameter words after the TSX \"the instructions beginning at 3,4 are executed\" (SYS)179, J 90.02.14), with one parameter word \"beginning at 2,4 are executed\" (SYS)180 and SYS)181, J 90.02.15), with none \"executed beginning at 1,4\" (SYS)182, J 90.02.15) — n,4 is the n-th word after the TSX, the 709/7090 convention in which IR4 holds the two's complement of the caller's location. (That last clause is *inference from the machine*; J never states TSX semantics, but it does confirm the compiler's complement discipline: \"by first loading an index register with the 2's complement of the location of the first word (the base) of the data block\", J 90.02.04.) IR4 is nevertheless not a register a DO could keep a return in across its body: the same appendix uses IR4 as the object-time data base register (\"LAC BL)2,4\" then \"CAL DATANAME,4\", J 90.02.04) and as scratch when building a complex Base Locator (\"PDX 0,4\", J 90.02.11) — so any return that must survive arbitrary generated code has to be planted in storage, for which 90.02 shows exactly the expected idiom, \"AXT 0, 4\" followed by \"SXA SYS)296, 4\" storing an index value into a named cell (J 90.02.33). Two further corroborations of a static per-procedure cell, both *inference by analogy*: the compiler already allocates static cells per procedure unit, Result Storage being section-qualified — \"Unique result storage cells are reserved for each section of the program as they are needed\", numbered from \"0 (main body of program)\" upward \"for each 'Begin Section' in the source program\" (J 90.02.03) — and every area the appendix reserves is a fixed block (RS) BSS N, TS) BSS N, PI) BSS N; J 90.02.03–05), with no stack, save-area chain, or dynamically allocated linkage area anywhere in it. Finally, where two linkages must be live at once the generated code takes a second index register rather than saving IR4 (\"TSX SYS)263, 6\" planted at 1,4 while IR4 still holds the outer caller's linkage, J 90.02.29). All of this is convention-level corroboration only; the DO sequence itself must be read off the 90.05 object listing.\n\n[Variant to append to §8.5.5, \"Nested and recursive DO\" (line 3882):]\n\n**Narrowed (90.02 mining, 2026-08-01):** the \"return address per procedure\" premise of the resolution is corroborated by the generated-code appendix, which shows a wholly static object-time storage model — every area it reserves is a fixed block (RS) BSS N, TS) BSS N, PI) BSS N; J 90.02.03–05), with no stack, save-area chain, or dynamically allocated linkage area, and Result Storage already allocated per procedure unit (\"Unique result storage cells are reserved for each section of the program as they are needed\", section numbers rising \"by 1 for each 'Begin Section'\", J 90.02.03). The generic call convention is TSX with index register 4, including to compiler-generated labels (\"TSX GN)019,4\", J 90.02.06), with returns expressed as displacements off it (\"return is 1,4 to routine SYS)263\", J 90.02.29); IR4 itself is reused as the data base register (J 90.02.04) and as scratch (J 90.02.11), so a pending return must be stored, not held. 90.02 never mentions DO, so the identity of the cell comes from the 90.05 object listing, not from here.\n\n[Optional one-sentence addition to §8.5.5, \"GO TO out of a DO-addressed (closed) procedure\" (line 3879):]\n\n**Narrowed (90.02 mining, 2026-08-01):** that a re-DO \"re-establishes linkage correctly\" is mechanically unproblematic under the appendix's static conventions — the return is a displacement off the IR4 linkage planted afresh by each TSX (J 90.02.06, J 90.02.29), and nothing in 90.02 is consumed or corrupted by an abandoned pending return; 90.02 itself is silent on DO.","remaining_open":"90.02 settles only the house style, not the DO sequence. Still open, and visible only in the 90.05 object/assembly listing (J 90.05, PDF pp. 198–216, against the source listing at PDF pp. 192–197): (1) whether a DO compiles to `TSX <procedure>,4` with the callee saving IR4 (e.g. `SXA <exit>,4` / `AXT`) into a cell owned by that procedure, or to an in-line `AXT`/`SXA` that plants the return address into the procedure's exit TRA before a plain TRA; (2) if a cell is used, which name class it falls in — a GN)nnn word, a TS)n cell, or an unnamed word inside the procedure's own code — since 90.02's storage-class list admits no return-cell category; (3) which index register actually carries it (IR4 per the universal convention, or another tag as in the IR6 usage at J 90.02.29), and whether the callee's exit is `TRA 1,4` or a TRA through a stored cell; (4) whether the cell is genuinely per-procedure or a single shared cell — which is what decides the question's real point, whether simultaneously active DOs of different procedures interact; (5) how the END sentence of a DO'd section is compiled versus the single-sentence DO case, and how DO … FOR's loop control (index increment and equality test) sits relative to the return; and (6) what the compiled DEPARTMENT.END / END.OF.MASTERS / SEARCH sites look like, including the DOs issued from inside IF arms and from GET's AT END phrase. Also unrecoverable from 90.02: whether any object-time check guards re-entry of an already-active procedure (nothing in 90.04's message list or 90.02 suggests one).","notes":"Method: read all 1,914 lines of /Users/jacklusher/development/comtran-compiler/comtran-manuals/J28-6169/90.02-generated-code.md (sections 90.02.00–90.02.33, PDF pp. 138–172). Silence on DO confirmed by exhaustive substring search, not by sampling: `grep -o -E '.{0,25}DO.{0,25}'` over the whole file (case-sensitive) and `grep -iE '\\bdo\\b'` (case-insensitive, word-bounded) both return zero hits; searches for \"go to\", \"goto\", \"perform\", \"alter\", \"recursi\", \"nested\", \"linkage\" likewise return nothing relevant (\"linkage\" never appears). Four load-bearing pages were re-verified character-for-character against the 150-dpi scans rather than trusted from the conversion: images/page-144.png (GN)019 example, header 90.02.06), page-153.png (SYS)179 \"3,4\", header 90.02.14), page-154.png (SYS)180/181 \"2,4\", SYS)182 \"1,4\", header 90.02.15), page-168.png (SYS)261 \"return is 1,4\", the unusual \"TSX SYS)263,6\" with tag 6, and \"(in IR1)\", header 90.02.29), plus page-141.png (the eight-symbol table and RS section-qualification, header 90.02.03). All match the transcription exactly, including \"TSX SYS)263,6\". Pointer check: the task's pointers (a)–(c) were all accurate as to section codes; nothing in them needed correction. Explicit inference labels used in the annotation: (i) that `n,4` = caller-location + n follows from 709/7090 TSX semantics, which J never states — J supplies only the corroborating complement discipline at 90.02.04 and the 1/2/3-word arithmetic across SYS)179–182; (ii) that IR6 at 90.02.29 is chosen to preserve a live IR4; (iii) that per-section Result Storage is an analogue, not evidence, for a per-procedure return cell; (iv) that the absence of any stack/save-area in 90.02 implies static linkage. No files were edited."}
{"question_id":"42","status":"narrowed","evidence":[{"claim":"The rule lives under the heading \"C. Conditional Statements\" (J 02.04.06), whose only construct-naming item is item 1, which names the IF statement.","citation":"J 02.04.06 (comtran-manuals/J28-6169/02-compiler.md lines 526-528; scan images/page-024.png)","verbatim_quote":"### C. Conditional Statements\n\n1. Comparisons may not be made between numeric and alphameric fields. If ABC is described as three position alphameric field (AAA) and DEF as a three position numeric field (999) the following IF statement represents an invalid comparison:"},{"claim":"The folding rule's own scope wording is operator-scoped / test-scoped, not construct-scoped: it names no construct at all. Only the illustrative example is an IF sentence. Verified character-for-character against the page scan.","citation":"J 02.04.07 (comtran-manuals/J28-6169/02-compiler.md lines 539-552; scan images/page-025.png)","verbatim_quote":"#### 2. Comparisons of fields of unequal length\n\na) Numeric fields are compared arithmetically without regard to length.\n\nb) Alphameric fields of unequal length are treated on the basis of the operator used in the comparison.\n\ni. In tests for an = or NOT = condition the fields will always be found to be unequal. In the following example where fields A and B are of alphameric type with unequal length the only code generated will be a transfer to D.\n\n```\nIF A = B THEN GO TO C OTHERWISE GO TO D.\n\nIF A NOT = B THEN GO TO D OTHERWISE GO TO C.\n```"},{"claim":"Rule 2.b.i is the only statement of the unequal-length alphameric equality rule in either manual. Exhaustive grep of comtran-manuals/J28-6169/02-compiler.md for \"unequal\" returns only lines 539/543/545 (this passage); F28-8043 contains no unequal-length comparison rule anywhere (grep of all F chunks for unequal/shorter/longer/truncat crossed with compar/equal/field returns only unrelated DO-loop and sample-program prose). The rule is a J-only addition.","citation":"J 02.04.07; F28-8043 (all chunks, negative search)"},{"claim":"J 02 never documents the conditional GO TO / WHEN construct at all — grep of 02-compiler.md for \"GO TO\" returns only the three lines of the 02.04.07 example, and \"WHEN\" appears only in the key-word list (02.03.02) and in the data-description clause BLANK WHEN ZERO. The conditional GO TO with WHEN is defined only in F. So 02.04.07's silence about WHEN reflects J's role as a clarification/amplification document, not a deliberate exclusion.","citation":"J 02.04-02.05 (negative search); F p. 48","verbatim_quote":"GO TO procedure.name.1 WHEN conditional expression 1,\n     procedure.name.2 WHEN conditional expression 2, ...\n     procedure.name.n WHEN conditional expression n"},{"claim":"F states the object-time semantics of the WHEN list, which is what a fold would have to preserve: alternatives are evaluated in order and the rest abandoned once one is true.","citation":"F p. 48","verbatim_quote":"When the conditional GO TO is executed at object time, each conditional expression is evaluated in turn until one is found to be true. Thereupon, control is transferred to the \"procedure.name\" associated with that conditional expression. Any remaining conditional expressions are left unevaluated. If none of the conditional expressions in the command is found to be true, control passes to the next clause or sentence in sequence."},{"claim":"Appendix 90.02 is scoped to the symbolic-listing format plus the IOC)/SYS) runtime library; it contains no per-verb or per-construct code templates and therefore cannot bear on comparison code shape.","citation":"J 90.02.00","verbatim_quote":"In addition, an assembly-like listing of the instructions generated by the compiler may be obtained by including the word LIST as one of the parameters on the $CMPLE card. This appendix explains the format and content of the instruction listing."},{"claim":"NEGATIVE RESULT (exhaustive): 90.02-generated-code.md (1914 lines) contains no mention of constant folding, compile-time evaluation of comparisons, or the shape of code generated for a conditional construct. Greps for fold / \"compile time\" / \"compile-time\" / unequal / truncat / OTHERWISE / THEN / branch / \"IF statement\" / \"GO TO\" return zero relevant hits; \"compar\" hits only SYS)161/SYS)162; \"WHEN\" hits only \"Blank When Zero\" and ordinary English \"whenever\"; \"transfer\" hits only the Base Locator prose; \"condition\" hits only SYS)131 (\"improper data condition\") and SYS)263 (\"GET error condition\").","citation":"J 90.02.00-90.02.33 (negative search across comtran-manuals/J28-6169/90.02-generated-code.md)"},{"claim":"Closest 90.02 item #1, SYS)162, is a run-time alphabetic-comparison subroutine with three returns; it says nothing about when the compiler emits it versus inline code, and nothing about folding. Its calling sequence carries independent LENGTH(1) and LENGTH(2), so the routine itself is not evidence that lengths are equalized at compile time.","citation":"J 90.02.12","verbatim_quote":"This subroutine performs an alphabetic comparison on two fields. OP is a CVR or NOP depending of the need to adjust the collating sequence before the comparison. If T(J) is 0, LOC(J) is the location of the field. If T(J) is not zero, the field is located by the 'pointer' word LOCATOR(J) and LOC(J) is the word displacement from the base. LENGTH(J) is the length of the field in characters and BYTE(J) is the beginning byte position of the field."},{"claim":"Closest 90.02 item #2, the phrase \"unknown at compile time\", belongs to SYS)242, a MOVPAK move routine, not to any comparison. It is irrelevant to the question.","citation":"J 90.02.25","verbatim_quote":"This MOVPAK subroutine moves alphabetic fields to alphabetic fields where some information was unknown at compile time."},{"claim":"NEGATIVE RESULT: the 90.05 sample program's only WHEN-clause equality test compares MASTER EMPLOYEE.NUMBER with DETAIL EMPLOYEE.NUMBER via the CALL synonyms M.EMP.NO / D.EMP.NO.","citation":"J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 195 (statements 187,00 and 192,00)","verbatim_quote":"        187,00   71461  GN)000CALL  (MASTER EMPLOYEE.NUMBER)   M.EMP.NO,\n                 71462                (DETAIL EMPLOYEE.NUMBER)   D.EMP.NO,\n\n...\n\n        192,00   71477  COMPARE.EMPLOYEE.NUMBERS.  GO TO CHECK.NEW.DEPT WHEN D.EMP.NO =\n                                         M.EMP.NO,  LOW.DETAIL WHEN D.EMP.NO LT M.EMP.NO."},{"claim":"Both operands of that WHEN equality test are non-format group fields of exactly 6 characters (DEPARTMENT AA = 2 plus EMPLOYEE AAAA = 4), hence EQUAL length: rule 2.b.i does not apply to it. MASTER EMPLOYEE.NUMBER is level 03 under DAT; DETAIL EMPLOYEE.NUMBER is level 02.","citation":"J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 192 (statements 1,00-5,00 and 23,00-26,00); typing rule J 02.05.06 / J 02.04.07 item 4","verbatim_quote":"          1,00   71175  MASTER                    1RECORD          L\n          2,00   71177    DAT                         2\n          3,00                  EMPLOYEE.NUM          3\n                 71200            BER\n          4,00   71201    DEPARTMENT                 4        AA\n          5,00   71202    EMPLOYEE                   4        AAAA\n\n...\n\n         23,00   71224  DETAIL                   01RECORD\n         24,00   71226    EMPLOYEE.NUMBER            02\n         25,00   71227    DEPARTMENT                 03       AA\n         26,00   71230    EMPLOYEE                   03       AAAA"},{"claim":"The sample's remaining equality/inequality tests are likewise outside rule 2.b.i: two are against the figurative constant HIGH.VALUE (governed by J 02.04.01-02), one compares two 2-character AA fields, and one is numeric against ZERO.","citation":"J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 196 (statements 197,00, 198,00, 200,00, 205,00)","verbatim_quote":"        197,00   71504  END.OF.MASTERS. IF D.EMP.NO = HIGH.VALUE THEN GO TO END.OF.RUN\n                                         OTHERWISE SET M.EMP.NO = HIGH.VALUE.\n        198,00   71507  END.OF.DETAILS. IF M.EMP.NO = HIGH.VALUE THEN GO TO END.OF.RUN\n                                         OTHERWISE SET D.EMP.NO = HIGH.VALUE, GO TO HIGH.DETAIL.\n\n...\n\n        200,00   71513  CHECK.NEW.DEPT. IF CURRENT.DEPT IS NOT EQUAL TO D.DEPT THEN\n                                         DO DEPARTMENT.END OTHERWISE GO TO COMPUTE.PAY.\n\n...\n\n        205,00                          IF M.BND.DED IS NOT EQUAL TO ZERO THEN DO BOND.ROUTINE"},{"claim":"CURRENT.DEPT is declared AA (2 characters) and D.DEPT is the synonym for DETAIL DEPARTMENT, also AA (2 characters) — equal length, so statement 200,00's NOT EQUAL TO is not subject to rule 2.b.i either.","citation":"J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 193 (statement 81,00); PDF p. 192 (statement 25,00); PDF p. 195 (statement 187,00)","verbatim_quote":"         81,00   71322    CURRENT.DEPT               2        AA"},{"claim":"NEGATIVE RESULT (the central one): the 90.05 sample program contains no unequal-length alphameric = or NOT = comparison anywhere in its PROCEDURE division, so the object listing cannot exhibit the folded form. The complete relation inventory across PDF pp. 195-197 is: 192,00 (= and LT on 6-char groups), 197,00/198,00 (= HIGH.VALUE), 200,00 (NOT EQUAL TO on 2-char AA fields), 203,00 (WORKING HOURS GT 40.0), 205,00 (NOT EQUAL TO ZERO, numeric), 212,00 (MASTER FICA GT 144.00), 215,00 (TR(WORKING WHT GT 0)), 219,00 (MASTER BONDENOMINATION NOT GT M.BND.ACC), 225,00 (MASTER RATE GT TABLE.ITEM RATE (INDEX)). All magnitude tests are numeric; all equality tests are equal-length alphameric or figurative-constant.","citation":"J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 195-197 (exhaustive)"},{"claim":"POSITIVE CONTROL: the WHEN clauses at COMPARE.EMPLOYEE.NUMBERS compile to two inline CAL/LAS three-way compares, each followed by a TRA triple decoding high/equal/low — i.e. real run-time comparison code, since rule 2.b.i does not apply.","citation":"J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 201 (LOC 00241-00255)","verbatim_quote":"00241      0535 00 1 01670   10001    COMPARE.EMPLOYEE.NUMBERS\n                                                        LAC   BL)3,1\n00242      7 00000 1 00446   10010            +1      TXL   SYS)294,1,0\n00243      4500 00 1 00000   10000            +2      CAL   2)EMPLOYEE.NUMBER,1\n00244      0535 00 2 01667   10001            +3      LAC   BL)2,2\n00245      7 00000 2 00446   10010            +4      TXL   SYS)294,2,0\n00246      4340 00 2 00000   10000            +5      LAS   1)EMPLOYEE.NUMBER,2\n00247      0020 00 0 00251   10001            +6      TRA   *+2\n00250      0020 00 0 00527   10001            +7      TRA   CHECK.NEW.DEPT\n00251      4500 00 1 00000   10000            +8      CAL   2)EMPLOYEE.NUMBER,1\n00252      4340 00 2 00000   10000            +9      LAS   1)EMPLOYEE.NUMBER,2\n00253      0020 00 0 00256   10001            +10     TRA   *+3\n00254      0020 00 0 00256   10001            +11     TRA   *+2\n00255      0020 00 0 00310   10001            +12     TRA   LOW.DETAIL"},{"claim":"POSITIVE CONTROL: the IF ... IS NOT EQUAL TO sentence at CHECK.NEW.DEPT compiles to the same CAL/LAS comparison primitive (with ANA masking of the 2-character operands into RS)0), confirming that IF and GO TO...WHEN share one comparison code generator in the field-test compiler.","citation":"J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 204-205 (LOC 00527-00541)","verbatim_quote":"00527      4500 00 0 00047   10001    CHECK.NEW.DEPT  CAL   CURRENT.DEPT\n\n...\n\n00530      4763 00 0 00022   10000            +1      LGL   18\n00531      4320 00 0 01732   10001            +2      ANA   CP)+30\n00532      0602 00 0 01621   10001            +3      SLW   RS)0\n00533      0535 00 1 01670   10001            +4      LAC   BL)3,1\n00534      7 00000 1 00446   10010            +5      TXL   SYS)294,1,0\n00535      4500 00 1 00000   10000            +6      CAL   2)DEPARTMENT,1\n00536      4320 00 0 01732   10001            +7      ANA   CP)+30\n00537      4340 00 0 01621   10001            +8      LAS   RS)0\n00540      0020 00 0 00542   10001            +9      TRA   *+2\n00541      0020 00 0 00546   10001            +10     TRA   GN)070"},{"claim":"The IF ... = HIGH.VALUE tests at END.OF.MASTERS compile to the same inline CAL/LAS shape, further confirming a single shared comparison primitive across IF forms.","citation":"J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 201-202 (LOC 00332-00341)","verbatim_quote":"00332      4500 00 0 01723   10001            +1      CAL   CP)+23\n00333      0535 00 1 01670   10001            +2      LAC   BL)3,1\n00334      7 00000 1 00446   10010            +3      TXL   SYS)294,1,0\n00335      4340 00 1 00000   10000            +4      LAS   2)EMPLOYEE.NUMBER,1\n00336      0020 00 0 00342   10001            +5      TRA   GN)066\n00337      0020 00 0 00341   10001            +6      TRA   *+2\n00340      0020 00 0 00342   10001            +7      TRA   GN)066\n00341      0020 00 0 00370   10001            +8      TRA   END.OF.RUN"},{"claim":"NEGATIVE RESULT: SYS)162 (and SYS)161) are never called anywhere in the 90.05 object listing (grep of comtran-manuals/J28-6169/90.05-sample-program.md returns no hits). All the sample's alphameric comparisons are one word or less and are done inline with CAL/LAS. So the sample gives no evidence about the out-of-line comparison path either.","citation":"J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 198-216 (negative search)"},{"claim":"INFERENCE (labelled): the compiler front end shares one conditional-expression path between IF and WHEN, since it will silently reinterpret a misplaced IF as a WHEN. This supports, but does not prove, that a rule stated for one applies to the other.","citation":"J 90.04.01, message 170,00","verbatim_quote":"170,00     0    -WHEN- SUBSTITUTED FOR -IF- BECAUSE OF IMPROPER USE."},{"claim":"NEGATIVE RESULT: no diagnostic in J's complete error-message list warns that a comparison is always unequal; the only two comparison-related messages are 107,00 and 123,00. If the fold happens, it happens silently.","citation":"J 90.04.01, messages 107,00 and 123,00 (exhaustive search of 90.04-error-messages.md for \"compar\")","verbatim_quote":"107,00     0    ILLEGAL COMPARISON STRUCTURE.\n\n...\n\n123,00     0    CANNOT USE VARIABLE LENGTH ITEMS FOR COMPARISON."}],"proposed_annotation":"**Narrowed (90.02 mining, 2026-08-01):** The rule's own scope wording is operator-scoped, not construct-scoped — J 02.04.07 states it as \"In tests for an = or NOT = condition the fields will always be found to be unequal\", under the heading \"C. Conditional Statements\" (J 02.04.06), naming no construct anywhere; only its worked example happens to be an IF sentence (\"IF A = B THEN GO TO C OTHERWISE GO TO D.\"). The semantic half therefore binds every context that evaluates such a condition — IF sentences, the conditional GO TO's WHEN clauses, and TR operands alike — and only the code-shape half, \"the only code generated will be a transfer to D\", is asserted of an IF sentence specifically. Neither appendix that could exhibit a fold does so. J 90.02 has nothing on the point: it \"explains the format and content of the instruction listing\" (J 90.02.00) and then catalogues the IOC)/SYS) runtime library, with no per-construct code templates; its two nearest items are SYS)162, a *run-time* routine that \"performs an alphabetic comparison on two fields\" and takes independent LENGTH(1)/LENGTH(2) (J 90.02.12), and the MOVPAK move routine SYS)242, used \"where some information was unknown at compile time\" (J 90.02.25) — neither concerns comparison folding. And the sample program contains no unequal-length alphameric `=`/`NOT =` comparison at all: its equality tests are `D.EMP.NO = M.EMP.NO` (both 6-character non-format groups, `AA` + `AAAA`), the two `= HIGH.VALUE` sentinel tests, `CURRENT.DEPT IS NOT EQUAL TO D.DEPT` (both `AA`), and `M.BND.DED IS NOT EQUAL TO ZERO` (numeric) (J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 192–193, 195–196). What the listing does establish is that IF and WHEN share one comparison code generator: the WHEN pair at COMPARE.EMPLOYEE.NUMBERS and the IF at CHECK.NEW.DEPT both compile to an inline `CAL`/`LAS` three-way compare followed by a TRA decode, SYS)162 never being called anywhere in the object listing (J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 201, 204–205) — which, together with msg 170,00 \"-WHEN- SUBSTITUTED FOR -IF- BECAUSE OF IMPROPER USE.\" showing a shared front-end path (J 90.04.01), makes it likely, though nowhere stated, that the fold applies to WHEN identically. Still open: the exact emitted form for a folded WHEN alternative — plausibly no code at all for an always-false `=` clause, and an unconditional transfer (stranding every later clause, since \"Any remaining conditional expressions are left unevaluated\", F p. 48) for an always-true `NOT =` one — and whether folding is diagnosed at all, which J's message list suggests it is not, warning only \"ILLEGAL COMPARISON STRUCTURE.\" (msg 107,00) and \"CANNOT USE VARIABLE LENGTH ITEMS FOR COMPARISON.\" (msg 123,00) (J 90.04.01).","remaining_open":"Two residues remain unrecoverable from the manuals. (1) Code shape for a folded WHEN clause. J 02.04.07 asserts the folded output only for an IF/THEN/OTHERWISE sentence (\"the only code generated will be a transfer to D\"). For a WHEN alternative in a conditional GO TO the analogue is not stated: an always-false `=` alternative would presumably generate no code at all, and an always-true `NOT =` alternative an unconditional transfer that renders every later alternative in the same GO TO dead (F p. 48 makes later alternatives unevaluated once one is true). Nothing in J confirms this, and the field-test compiler could equally have emitted the doomed comparison and let it fail at run time in a WHEN context. (2) Whether the fold extends to TR(condition) operands, where the \"transfer\" framing has no meaning and the compiler would have to fold to the literal 0 or 1. Both would only be settled by a compiler listing containing such a comparison, or by an external period source (e.g. a later CT bulletin or the SHARE library); the 90.02 appendix and the 90.05 sample are exhausted. Note also that whether the fold is silent is inferred from the absence of any such diagnostic in J 90.04.01's list, not from a positive statement.","notes":"Method: (a) read J 02.04.06-02.04.07 in comtran-manuals/J28-6169/02-compiler.md lines 526-552 and verified the transcription character-for-character against comtran-manuals/J28-6169/images/page-025.png — the conversion is exact, including the operator-scoped phrasing and both IF examples; (b) exhaustively searched comtran-manuals/J28-6169/90.02-generated-code.md (1914 lines, sections 90.02.00-90.02.33, PDF pp. 138-172) for fold / \"compile time\" / \"compile-time\" / unequal / truncat / collat / compar / WHEN / \"IF \" / OTHERWISE / THEN / branch / transfer / condition / test / \"GO TO\" / verb / \"source statement\" — every hit was checked and none bears on constant folding or on conditional-construct code shape; (c) read the full 90.05 source listing (PDF pp. 192-197) and built a complete inventory of every relation in the PROCEDURE division, resolving each operand's declared length from the DATA division (PDF pp. 192-193) and the CALL synonym table (statement 187,00); (d) read the corresponding object code at PDF pp. 201-205 for all four alphameric comparisons.\n\nCorrection to the task pointers: two are slightly off. The \"unknown at compile time\" phrase is at J 90.02.25 (SYS)242, PDF p. 164) as stated, but it is a MOVPAK *move* routine, not comparison-adjacent. The SYS)162 three-way alphabetic comparison is at J 90.02.12 (PDF p. 151) as stated. The pointer's expectation that the sample contains no unequal-length alphameric `=` comparison is confirmed — but the reason is worth recording precisely: the WHEN comparison the survey presumably had in mind (`D.EMP.NO = M.EMP.NO`) is between two non-format group fields of *exactly equal* 6-character length, so it is a clean positive control for unfolded code rather than merely an absence.\n\nDefinition-document context checked: line 1971 (§5.3.2 item 2.b.i) already carries the rule and adds the editorial gloss \"I.e. the compiler folds the comparison at compile time.\"; line 1416 (§4.1, TR write-up) imports it as one of the J comparison restrictions applying inside TR — that import is consistent with the operator-scoped reading confirmed here and needs no change. Line 3878 in §8.5.5 is the *2.b.ii* item (\"Tests for NOT greater or less conditions\"), a different question; there is no §8.5 entry that duplicates Open Question 42, so only the Open Question 42 line at 4205 needs the annotation. If a §8.5.5 companion is wanted later, the natural place is a new bullet beside 3878, not an edit to it.\n\nNo files were edited."}
{"question_id":"31","status":"narrowed","evidence":[{"claim":"SYS)131 is defined only as a flag cell set by MOVPAK's numeric move/convert subroutines; the definition states no reaction, no reader, and no reset.","citation":"J 90.02.10 (comtran-manuals/J28-6169/90.02-generated-code.md line 414); wording verified character-for-character against comtran-manuals/J28-6169/images/page-148.png","verbatim_quote":"**SYS)131** This cell is set non-zero whenever any one of the numeric move or convert subroutines of MOVPAK detects an improper data condition."},{"claim":"SYS)131 is one of a set of three sibling condition-flag cells (overflow, improper data, floating-point underflow), all worded identically as 'set non-zero'/'set non zero' with no consumer named.","citation":"J 90.02.10 (line 412) and J 90.02.11 (line 469)","verbatim_quote":"**SYS)130** This cell is set non-zero whenever any one of the numeric move or convert subroutines of MOVPAK detects the truncation of significant high order values (i.e. overflow).  ...  **SYS)134** This cell is set non zero whenever a floating point underflow results from a Move."},{"claim":"MOVPAK is the packaged set of object-time field-move subroutines; the cell is therefore set on the ordinary MOVE/SET/ADD data path, not on an I/O path.","citation":"J 90.02.10 (lines 386)","verbatim_quote":"Many of the SYS Reference numbers are concerned with subroutines to move fields at object time. The interaction of various of these 'MOVE' subroutine made it desirable to package several of them together into one subroutine (called MOVPAK)."},{"claim":"SYS)131 is a Type 2 communication cell (loaded per object program), not a subroutine — it has no entry point and cannot itself do anything.","citation":"J 90.02.07 (lines 289, 291)","verbatim_quote":"Type 2. Subroutines contained on the system tape which are placed in core by the CT Loader as required by the particular object program being loaded.  ...  In addition to System Subroutines, there are individual cells and groups of cells which contain information which must be accessible to various parts of the CT system. These Communication Cells are categorized in the same manner as System Subroutines, i.e., those communication cells which are part of the CT Monitoring System are Type 1, and those communication cells which are a part of a particular object program are Type 2."},{"claim":"Negative finding from reading the whole generated-code appendix (J 90.02.00–90.02.33, PDF pp. 138–172, 1914 lines): no SYS) or IOC) entry anywhere is documented as testing, branching on, printing from, or clearing SYS)130, SYS)131 or SYS)134. A repository-wide grep confirms SYS)130/131/134 occur only in their own definitions at 90.02.10–11 and nowhere else in either manual.","citation":"J 90.02.00–90.02.33 (whole appendix read); grep over comtran-manuals/ for 'SYS)130', 'SYS)131', 'SYS)134' returns only lines 412, 414, 469 of 90.02-generated-code.md"},{"claim":"The complete compiled sample program never references SYS)130, SYS)131 or SYS)134. Its SYS/IOC references (extracted from the printed symbolic column and cross-checked against the octal address fields of SYS-relocated words, CNTRL 10010, whose address field equals the decimal SYS number — e.g. 0o266=182, 0o414=268, 0o446=294) are: SYS 132, 133, 175, 177, 178, 180, 182, 184, 185, 190, 193, 198, 212, 214, 225, 226, 239, 240, 241, 243, 244, 245, 267, 268, 269, 275, 294 and IOC 1, 8, 9, 29, 40.","citation":"J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 198–216 (comtran-manuals/J28-6169/90.05-sample-program.md)"},{"claim":"A real MOVPAK numeric-convert site in the compiled sample (edited field to internal decimal, SYS)268/269/275) is followed immediately by the store and ordinary arithmetic, with no test of any flag cell — direct positive evidence that the field-test compiler emits no post-convert data-validity test.","citation":"J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 212","verbatim_quote":"    01356  0074 00 4 00266   10010        +30   TSX    SYS)182,4\n    01357  1 00001 1 00414   10010        +31   TXI    SYS)268,1,1\n    01360  1 00005 1 00415   10010        +32   TXI    SYS)269,1,5\n    01361  1 00005 1 00423   10010        +33   TXI    SYS)275,1,5\n    01362  0601 00 0 01643   10001        +34   STO    3.RS)1"},{"claim":"The same listing shows the compiler DOES emit an inline object-time check where one exists (the base-locator-not-loaded trap), so the absence of a flag test after MOVPAK calls is a design choice, not a limitation of the listing.","citation":"J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 212; routine defined at J 90.02.33","verbatim_quote":"    01405  0535 00 1 01667   10001         +1   LAC    BL)2,1\n    01406  7 00000 1 00446   10010         +2   TXL    SYS)294,1,0"},{"claim":"The GET-path check is a different mechanism: SYS)261 is a subroutine (not a MOVPAK cell), it checks a system control word (the first word of a variable-length BCD tape record giving the remaining record length), not programmer data, and it reacts by alternate return rather than by setting a flag.","citation":"J 90.02.29; wording verified against comtran-manuals/J28-6169/images/page-168.png","verbatim_quote":"Subroutine SYS)261 converts the logical accumulator from a BCD number to binary, checking for non-numeric characters and/or imbedded or trailing blanks; and leaves the result in the decrement of the AC. This routine is used in conjunction with getting a variable length BCD record from tape where the first word of the record gives the remaining length of the record. If an error is detected during conversion, return is 1,4 to routine SYS)263 which prints an error message."},{"claim":"The GET-path reaction is fatal — it prints and terminates the run — unlike SYS)131, which does nothing.","citation":"J 90.02.29","verbatim_quote":"This subroutine prints an error message in conjunction with SYS)261 upon GET error condition (see SYS)261) and exits to the CT monitor."},{"claim":"Every object-time error printer documented in J 90.02 concerns I/O or addressing and every one terminates the run: SYS)260 (record length error), SYS)263 (GET conversion error), SYS)264 (record size exceeds Blocksize), SYS)265 (unexpected end-of-file with no AT END), SYS)283 (GET error with no ON ERROR), SYS)291 (Write to card equipment in Locating Mode), SYS)294 (Base Locator used before loaded). None concerns numeric data validity.","citation":"J 90.02.28 (SYS)260), 90.02.29 (SYS)263–265), 90.02.32 (SYS)283), 90.02.33 (SYS)291, SYS)294)","verbatim_quote":"This subroutine prints an error message whenever a reference is made to a Base Locator before the locator has been loaded, and exits back to the CT Monitor."},{"claim":"J's own inventory of object-time on-line messages has exactly four categories, and the error category is I/O-oriented and always fatal — leaving no slot for a non-fatal data-validity message.","citation":"J 05.06.04","verbatim_quote":"4. Object program error messages, usually concerning I/O errors. Object-time processing will terminate and control will revert back to the CT Monitor."},{"claim":"J 90.04's improper-data and non-numeric-character messages are compile-time diagnostics, not object-time reactions: the listing carries its own header saying so, and severity codes 1–5 govern only whether the object program is allowed to run.","citation":"J 90.04.01 (listing header; msgs 25, 67, 111, 112, 120, 182), J 90.04.02","verbatim_quote":"THE FOLLOWING ERRORS WERE DETECTED DURING COMPILATION-\n...\n 67,00     0    THERE IS AN ILLEGAL NON-NUMERIC CHARACTER IN THE NUMERIC FIELD.\n...\n182,00     0    IMPROPER DATA FORMAT."},{"claim":"No source-language construct exists that could react to an improper data condition. The only condition options in either manual are ON OVERFLOW (SET/ADD, single result field only) and the Environment FILE-card ON ERROR (unrecoverable redundancy, block checksum, block sequence errors only). OVERFLOW appears in J's key-word list but no data-condition word does; ERROR appears only among the Environment-restricted words.","citation":"F p. 44, F p. 47, F p. 110 (word list); J 02.03.02–03 (key-word lists); J 02.07.07 (ON ERROR scope)","verbatim_quote":"IOCS cannot continue processing after discovering an unrecoverable redundancy error, a block checksum error or a block sequence error without direction from the programmer. The ON ERROR option of the Environment FILE card provides for communication between IOCS and the programmer in these three error situations."},{"claim":"The cell is not even nameable from CRYPT: the right parenthesis in 'SYS)131' is forbidden in CRYPT symbols, and CRYPT may reference only names defined in the Commercial Translator sections of the program.","citation":"J 02.08.01 (CRYPT rule 1 and 'Flexibility Above that of SCAT' item 1; typo 'Parentheis' preserved per fidelity policy)","verbatim_quote":"The characters, \"Quotation Mark,\" \"Left Parenthesis,\" \"Right Parentheis,\" \"Dollar Sign\" may not be used as part of a symbol."},{"claim":"The field-test processor's general stance was to omit object-time validity checks, which is consistent with an improper-data flag that nothing consults.","citation":"J 90.01.02","verbatim_quote":"No object time check is made to insure that subscript references conform to the limits specified by the array dimensions in the Data Description."},{"claim":"Caveat on the negative finding: the appendix's SYS list has gaps (135–154, 157–159, 259, 284–285, 289–290, 293, 295), so an undocumented routine could in principle consult the flags; the appendix claims only to list entries 'which may appear in the symbolic listing of a CT program'.","citation":"J 90.02.07; enumeration of documented entries across J 90.02.10–90.02.33"}],"proposed_annotation":"PRIMARY — append to Open Question 31 (docs/comtran-language-definition.md line 4191):\n\n**Narrowed (90.02 mining, 2026-08-01):** SYS)131 is a Type 2 *communication cell*, not a mechanism — \"This cell is set non-zero whenever any one of the numeric move or convert subroutines of MOVPAK detects an improper data condition\" (J 90.02.10), MOVPAK being the packaged object-time move/convert subroutines (J 90.02.10; SYS)183–238, 246–258, 267–282), and Type 2 entries being cells the CT Loader places in core per object program (J 90.02.07). Mining the whole generated-code appendix establishes the negative: no SYS) or IOC) entry in J 90.02.00–90.02.33 is documented as testing, branching on, printing from, or clearing SYS)131 — or its siblings SYS)130 (overflow) and SYS)134 (floating-point underflow from a Move) — and the compiled sample program references SYS 132, 133, 175–190, 193, 198, 212, 214, 225–226, 239–245, 267–269, 275 and 294 but never 130, 131 or 134; its numeric-convert sites (`TSX SYS)182,4` / `TXI SYS)268,1,1` / `TXI SYS)269,1,5` / `TXI SYS)275,1,5`) run straight into `STO 3.RS)1` and ordinary arithmetic, while the same page *does* carry an inline object-time trap where one exists (`LAC BL)2,1` / `TXL SYS)294,1,0`, the base-locator-not-loaded check). Nor can a program name the condition: the only condition options in either manual are ON OVERFLOW (SET/ADD, single result field) and the Environment FILE-card ON ERROR (redundancy/checksum/sequence errors only), no key-word list holds a data-condition word, and CRYPT cannot even write the symbol, since \"Right Parentheis\" (sic) \"may not be used as part of a symbol\". So the reading is: the move or arithmetic completes, whatever the conversion produced is stored, execution continues silently, and the event survives only as a non-zero flag cell that no documented code reads or resets — of a piece with \"No object time check is made to insure that subscript references conform to the limits specified by the array dimensions in the Data Description.\" This is a **different mechanism** from the GET-path check it is easily confused with: SYS)261 is a subroutine *outside* MOVPAK that converts \"the logical accumulator from a BCD number to binary, checking for non-numeric characters and/or imbedded or trailing blanks\", operating on the length control word of a variable-length BCD tape record rather than on programmer data, and it reacts by alternate return — \"If an error is detected during conversion, return is 1,4 to routine SYS)263 which prints an error message\" — SYS)263 then \"prints an error message ... and exits to the CT monitor\", i.e. it kills the run. Every object-time error printer in J 90.02 (SYS)260, 263, 264, 265, 283, 291, 294) is likewise fatal and I/O- or addressing-related, matching J 05.06.04's inventory of object-time on-line messages (\"Object program error messages, usually concerning I/O errors. Object-time processing will terminate and control will revert back to the CT Monitor.\"); none concerns numeric data validity. J 90.04's improper-data messages (25, 67 \"THERE IS AN ILLEGAL NON-NUMERIC CHARACTER IN THE NUMERIC FIELD.\", 111, 112, 120, 182) belong to the compile-time list headed \"THE FOLLOWING ERRORS WERE DETECTED DURING COMPILATION-\" and carry compilation severity codes, so they are not object-time reactions either. Still open: what MOVPAK counts as an \"improper data condition\" and which of its subroutines set the cell; what value is left in the target field; and whether the cell is ever reset (all three flag cells are described only as \"set non-zero\"). (J 90.02.07, 90.02.10–11, 90.02.28–29, 90.02.33; J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 212; J 02.03.02–03; J 02.07.07; J 02.08.01; J 90.01.02; J 90.04.01–02; J 05.06.04; F pp. 44, 47, 110; images/page-148.png, images/page-168.png)\n\nCOMPANION — §4.3.3 has no matching §8.5 entry for improper data (the nearest, §8.5.4 \"Overflow with no ON OVERFLOW clause\", covers SYS)130 only). Suggested replacement for the trailing clause of the §4.3.3 bullet at line 1537, which currently mentions SYS)131 in passing:\n\n...there is also SYS)131 for \"an improper data condition\" and SYS)134 for floating point underflow resulting from a Move (J 90.02.10, 90.02.11). All three are write-only flag cells: mining the whole generated-code appendix found nothing documented that tests, prints from, or clears any of them, and the compiled sample program — which contains numeric MOVPAK conversions but no ON OVERFLOW — never references 130, 131 or 134 at all, storing the converted value and continuing (J 90.02.00–90.02.33; J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 212). See the §8.5 catalog and Open Question 31.","remaining_open":"Four things the manuals still do not settle. (1) What MOVPAK actually classifies as \"an improper data condition\" — non-digit characters, misplaced or missing sign overpunches, imbedded blanks, or all of these — and which of its ~90 numeric move/convert subroutines set the cell; the phrase is never expanded anywhere in J. (2) What value MOVPAK leaves in the target field when it detects one (nothing says the store is suppressed, and the SYS)131 entry describes only the flag), so the resulting target contents are undefined by the manuals. (3) Whether SYS)130/131/134 are ever reset — all three are described only as being \"set non-zero\", never cleared, which if literal means the flags are sticky for the whole run and could not distinguish which statement raised the condition. (4) A residual possibility, honestly stated: the 90.02 SYS list has gaps (135–154, 157–159, 259, 284–285, 289–290, 293, 295) and claims only to list entries \"which may appear in the symbolic listing of a CT program\", so an undocumented routine consulting the flags cannot be formally excluded — though the sample program's clean, check-free numeric-convert code makes it unlikely. Separately unverifiable from the surviving text: whether the compiler generates an inline test of SYS)130 to implement F's ON OVERFLOW, since the sample program contains no ON OVERFLOW clause and no 90.02 entry documents such a test (bears on Open Questions 26 and 27, not on 31).","notes":"Method: read all 1914 lines of comtran-manuals/J28-6169/90.02-generated-code.md; verified the two load-bearing passages against the page scans (images/page-148.png for 90.02.10, images/page-168.png for 90.02.29) — both transcriptions are exact. Read all of 90.04-error-messages.md and 90.01-deferred-features.md, the 02.04 and 02.08 sections of 02-compiler.md, J 05.06.04, and docs/comtran-language-definition.md lines 1505–1560, 3840–3899, 4150–4247. No files were edited.\n\nTwo pointer corrections for the parent agent. (a) The pointer described 90.02.29 as \"SYS)261/263: the GET-path BCD-to-binary conversion that checks for non-numeric characters\" — correct, but note it is *not* a check on user data: 90.02.29 says explicitly it is used \"in conjunction with getting a variable length BCD record from tape where the first word of the record gives the remaining length of the record\", i.e. it validates the record-length control word. That strengthens the \"different mechanism\" point considerably. (b) There is no §8.5 entry matching Open Question 31; the SYS)131 mention in the body is in §4.3.3 at line 1537, so the companion annotation targets that line, not §8.5.\n\nThe strongest new evidence is negative-plus-positive rather than textual: the compiled sample program (J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 198–216) contains real MOVPAK numeric conversions on tape-read data yet never references SYS)130/131/134, while on the very same listing page it *does* emit an inline object-time trap (TXL SYS)294) for the one condition the processor does check. Cross-check of the SYS-reference extraction: for SYS-relocated words (CNTRL 10010) the printed octal address field equals the decimal SYS number (0o204=132, 0o266=182, 0o414=268, 0o446=294), so the symbolic column and the octal column agree independently — 130 and 131 would appear as 00202 and 00203 under CNTRL 10010 and do not occur.\n\nStatus is \"narrowed\" rather than \"resolved\": the question as posed (\"what happens at object time\") is now answerable in practice — nothing program-visible happens — but the manuals still do not define the condition itself or the resulting target value, so a compiler implementer has a defined control-flow answer and an undefined data answer."}
{"question_id":"Q41+Q47","status":"narrowed","evidence":[{"claim":"[Q47] J's generated-code appendix documents a dedicated open-all-files subroutine, SYS)175, taking the file-list pointer IOC)1 (scan-verified on images/page-153.png).","citation":"J 90.02.14 (PDF p. 153); images/page-153.png","verbatim_quote":"**SYS)175**\n\n```\nTSX  SYS)175,4\nPZE  IOC)1\n```\n\nThis routine opens all files in the file list located by IOC)1."},{"claim":"[Q47] The named-file OPEN has its own subroutine, SYS)174, immediately preceding SYS)175 — so the appendix documents both OPEN forms.","citation":"J 90.02.13 (PDF p. 152)","verbatim_quote":"**SYS)174**\n\n```\nTSX  SYS)174,4\nPZE  FILENAME\n```\n\nThis routine opens the file designated FILENAME."},{"claim":"[Q47] The CLOSE side is symmetric: SYS)176 closes a named file, SYS)177 closes all files via the same IOC)1 list — a full four-way OPEN/CLOSE runtime.","citation":"J 90.02.14 (PDF p. 153); images/page-153.png","verbatim_quote":"**SYS)176**\n\n```\nTSX  SYS)176,4\nPZE  FILENAME\n```\n\nThis routine closes the file designated FILENAME.\n\n**SYS)177**\n\n```\nTSX  SYS)177,4\nPZE  IOC)1\n```\n\nThis routine closes all files in the file list located by IOC)1."},{"claim":"[Q47] IOC)1 is the CT Monitor cell holding the file list used for both opening and closing — the run-time embodiment of \"ALL FILES\".","citation":"J 90.02.08 (PDF p. 146)","verbatim_quote":"**IOC)1**\n```\nPZE L,,N\n```\nA cell in the CT Monitor communications area which locates (L) a list of files, and designates the number (N) of files in the list. This List is used in Opening and Closing files."},{"claim":"[Q47] The field-test sample program's first procedure statement is OPEN ALL FILES (scan-verified on images/page-195.png).","citation":"J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 195; images/page-195.png","verbatim_quote":"        188,00   71466  START.          OPEN ALL FILES,\n                                         MOVE ZEROS TO INTERNAL.TOTALS, GRAND.TOTALS,\n                                         GET MASTER, AT END DO END.OF.MASTERS."},{"claim":"[Q47] That statement compiled to a call on the open-all subroutine SYS)175 with the file-list pointer IOC)1, at the label START (scan-verified on images/page-200.png).","citation":"J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 200; images/page-200.png","verbatim_quote":"00165                                 GN)000\n00165      0074 00 4 00257   10010    START           TSX   SYS)175,4\n00166      0 00000 0 00001   10010            +1      PZE   IOC)1"},{"claim":"[Q47] The whole compilation, including OPEN ALL FILES, produced no diagnostics.","citation":"J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 197","verbatim_quote":"        NO ERRORS WERE DETECTED DURING COMPILATION"},{"claim":"[Q47] The sample's CLOSE ALL FILES, STOP RUN. compiles to SYS)177/IOC)1 (the coded close-all), then the STOP display SYS)178, then a second SYS)177/IOC)1 before the end-of-job return TXI IOC)40,0 — the implicit close-all J attributes to STOP RUN. (The attribution of the second SYS)177 to STOP RUN is inference from the instruction order plus J 02.04.06.)","citation":"J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 196, 204; J 02.04.06","verbatim_quote":"00517      0074 00 4 00261   10010            +87     TSX   SYS)177,4\n00520      0 00000 0 00001   10010            +88     PZE   IOC)1\n00521      0074 00 4 00262   10010            +89     TSX   SYS)178,4\n00522      0 01727 0 01726   10101            +90     PZE   CP)+26,,CP)+27\n00523      0 01731 0 01730   10101            +91     PZE   CP)+28,,CP)+29\n00524      0074 00 4 00261   10010            +92     TSX   SYS)177,4\n00525      0 00000 0 00001   10010            +93     PZE   IOC)1\n00526      1 00000 0 00050   10010            +94     TXI   IOC)40,0"},{"claim":"[Q47] J 02.04.06 states the STOP RUN rule that the second SYS)177 call implements.","citation":"J 02.04.06","verbatim_quote":"A STOP RUN instruction must be included in each program to provide for transfer of control to the CT Supervisor at conclusion of execution of the object program. All open files are closed prior to this transfer of control as if a CLOSE ALL FILES had been supplied."},{"claim":"[Q47] The named-file OPEN form is separately policed by a J compiler diagnostic, confirming the OPEN verb is parsed with file-name operands as well as ALL FILES.","citation":"J 90.04.01 msgs 138, 139; images/page-184.png (message listing)","verbatim_quote":"138,00     0    FILE NAME SHOULD FOLLOW -CLOSE-.\n139,00     0    FILE NAME SHOULD FOLLOW -OPEN-."},{"claim":"[Q47] ALL, FILES, OPEN and CLOSE are all J reserved key words (list 2: may not be used as Data or Procedure names), consistent with OPEN ALL FILES being part of the implemented vocabulary.","citation":"J 02.03.02","verbatim_quote":"| ALL | EQUAL | LESS | SECTION |\n| AND | EQUALS | LIBRARY | SET |\n| AT | EXACTLY | LOAD | STOP |\n| BLANK | FILES | LT | THAN |"},{"claim":"[Q47] F's two OPEN forms, which J's object code is now shown to implement.","citation":"F p. 39","verbatim_quote":"OPEN file.name.1, file.name.2, ... file.name.n\nOPEN ALL FILES"},{"claim":"[Q41] J's restriction on the AT END clause, the wording that prompted the question.","citation":"J 02.07.05","verbatim_quote":"The AT END clause which may be used with the GET command may consist of a single imperative statement only. The clause is used to specify the action"},{"claim":"[Q41] F's unrestricted form, which J narrows.","citation":"F p. 40 (also F p. 109 skeleton)","verbatim_quote":"..., AT END any imperative clause"},{"claim":"[Q41] J's generic GET calling sequence names the AT END slot END-OF-FILE-PROCEDURE and the ON ERROR slot ERROR-PROCEDURE — i.e. the clause is reached by an address handed to IOCS (scan-verified on images/page-142.png; 'END-OF-FILE-PROCEDURE' is an OCR correction recorded in the chunk's conversion notes and confirmed on the scan).","citation":"J 90.02.04 (PDF p. 142); images/page-142.png","verbatim_quote":"TSX      IOC)8,4\nPZE      FILENAME,,SYS)260\nPZE      END-OF-FILE-PROCEDURE,,ERROR-PROCEDURE\nIOCDN*   BL)2,,14"},{"claim":"[Q41] SYS)265 is the default occupant of the address field of that third word when AT END is omitted, and it terminates the run.","citation":"J 90.02.29 (PDF p. 168)","verbatim_quote":"This SYS number appears as part of the GET calling sequence to the IOCS Read routine whenever the 'AT END' option is not used with the GET verb.\n\n```\nTSX     IOC)8, 4\nPZE     FILENAME, , SYS)260\nPZE     SYS)265, , SYS)283\nIOCDN*  BL)NN, , 14\n```\n\nSYS)265 prints a message concerning the unexpected end-of-file and exits to the CT Monitor."},{"claim":"[Q41] SYS)283 is the parallel default in the decrement of the same word when ON ERROR is not specified (printed typo \"in not used\" preserved), confirming address field = AT END exit, decrement = ON ERROR exit.","citation":"J 90.02.32 (PDF p. 171)","verbatim_quote":"This SYS number appears as part of a GET calling sequence to the IOCS Read routine whenever the 'ON ERROR' option in not used in the Environment description of the file."},{"claim":"[Q41] SYS)260, in the decrement of the first PZE word, is the record-length-error handler — not an AT END slot; images/page-201.png","verbatim_quote":"00233      0074 00 4 00010   10010            +1      TSX   IOC)8,4\n00234      0 00404 0 04003   11010            +2      PZE   DETAILFILE,,SYS)260\n00235      0 00433 0 00240   11001            +3      PZE   GN)062,,SYS)283\n00236      5 00003 6 01670   10001            +4      IOCTN*  BL)3,,3\n00237      0020 00 0 00241   10001            +5      TRA   GN)063\n00240      0020 00 0 00351   10001    GN)062          TRA   END.OF.DETAILS"},{"claim":"[Q41] Msg 106's wording belongs to a diagnostic family used elsewhere only for operands that are literally bare names (the FILE-card ONERROR and FORLABEL options) — pointing the other way, toward a name-taking AT END (scan-verified on images/page-183.png).","citation":"J 90.04.01 msgs 92, 93, 106; images/page-183.png","verbatim_quote":"106,00     0    STATEMENT OR SECTION NAME MUST FOLLOW -AT END-. CHECK 'NAME.1'."},{"claim":"[Q41] The identically-templated FILE-card messages whose operands are bare names.","citation":"J 90.04.01 msgs 92, 93; images/page-183.png","verbatim_quote":"92,00     0    STATEMENT OR SECTION NAME MUST FOLLOW -ONERROR- IN THE -FILE- CARD.\n93,00     0    STATEMENT OR SECTION NAME MUST FOLLOW -FORLABEL- IN THE -FILE- CARD."},{"claim":"[Q41] F's timing rule for the clause, unchanged by J and consistent with the compiled DO-and-return shape.","citation":"F p. 40","verbatim_quote":"3. If end of file is reached, any alternate command specified in the AT END phrase\n   is performed."}],"proposed_annotation":"=== Q47 — append to the Open Questions entry 47 (line 4213) ===\n\n**Resolved (90.02 mining, 2026-08-01): yes — `OPEN ALL FILES` is in the implemented language.** The field-test sample's first procedure statement is `188,00   71466  START.          OPEN ALL FILES,` (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 195), the listing closes with \"NO ERRORS WERE DETECTED DURING COMPILATION\" (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 197), and the object code generated at that label is `START           TSX   SYS)175,4` / `+1      PZE   IOC)1` (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 200) — SYS)175 being the routine of which J says \"This routine opens all files in the file list located by IOC)1\" (J 90.02.14), and IOC)1 being \"A cell in the CT Monitor communications area which locates (L) a list of files, and designates the number (N) of files in the list. This List is used in Opening and Closing files\" (J 90.02.08). The processor in fact carries the complete four-way OPEN/CLOSE runtime that F's two forms of each verb require: SYS)174 \"opens the file designated FILENAME\" (J 90.02.13), SYS)175 opens all, SYS)176 \"closes the file designated FILENAME\" and SYS)177 \"closes all files in the file list located by IOC)1\" (J 90.02.14). The named-file OPEN is independently attested by diagnostic 139,00 \"FILE NAME SHOULD FOLLOW -OPEN-.\" (J 90.04.01), and ALL, FILES, OPEN and CLOSE are all J reserved key words (J 02.03.02). The CLOSE side corroborates the reading: `CLOSE ALL FILES, STOP RUN.` (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 196) compiles to `TSX SYS)177,4` / `PZE IOC)1`, then the STOP display call `TSX SYS)178,4` with its two constant-pool words, then a *second* `TSX SYS)177,4` / `PZE IOC)1` immediately before the end-of-job return `TXI IOC)40,0` (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 204) — the implicit close-all that J 02.04.06 ascribes to STOP RUN (\"All open files are closed prior to this transfer of control as if a CLOSE ALL FILES had been supplied\"). J's silence about OPEN ALL FILES in 02.04.06 is therefore an omission of prose, not of the feature; F p. 39 stands as its definition. (F p. 39; J 02.03.02; J 02.04.06; J 90.02.08, 90.02.13–14; J 90.04.01 msgs 138–139; J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 195–197, 200, 204; images/page-153.png, page-195.png, page-200.png)\n\n=== Q41 — append to the Open Questions entry 41 (line 4204) ===\n\n**Narrowed (90.02 mining, 2026-08-01):** the generated code settles the mechanism, not the parse. J's generic GET calling sequence is `TSX IOC)8,4` / `PZE FILENAME,,SYS)260` / `PZE END-OF-FILE-PROCEDURE,,ERROR-PROCEDURE` / `IOCDN* BL)2,,14` (J 90.02.04); the address field of the third word holds the AT END exit — occupied by SYS)265, which \"appears as part of the GET calling sequence to the IOCS Read routine whenever the 'AT END' option is not used with the GET verb\" and \"prints a message concerning the unexpected end-of-file and exits to the CT Monitor\" (J 90.02.29) — while its decrement holds the ON ERROR exit, defaulting to SYS)283 (J 90.02.32). In the sample, `GET MASTER, AT END DO END.OF.MASTERS.` compiles that word as `PZE GN)058,,SYS)283`, and the clause becomes a compiler-generated out-of-line block placed after the calling sequence and jumped over by the normal-return `TRA GN)059`: `GN)058          AXT   *+3,7` / `SXA   END.OF.MASTERS,4` / `TRA   END.OF.MASTERS+1` (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 201). `*+3` is GN)059, which is the code of the *next source sentence* (statement 189, `MOVE MASTER DEPARTMENT TO CURRENT.DEPT, GO TO GET.DETAIL`) — so an AT END clause that returns resumes at the statement following the GET, exactly the continuation a non-transfer imperative would need. `GET DETAIL, AT END GO TO END.OF.DETAILS.` likewise receives its own generated block, `GN)062          TRA   END.OF.DETAILS`, although the bare target address would have served in the calling-sequence word — *inference:* the AT END clause is emitted through ordinary statement code generation rather than being planted as a name, and nothing in the IOCS interface confines it to a transfer. Pulling the other way, msg 106,00 \"STATEMENT OR SECTION NAME MUST FOLLOW -AT END-. CHECK 'NAME.1'.\" is worded from the same template as 92,00 and 93,00 (\"STATEMENT OR SECTION NAME MUST FOLLOW -ONERROR- / -FORLABEL- IN THE -FILE- CARD.\"), whose operands are literally bare names, and J's own template calls the slot END-OF-FILE-PROCEDURE. **Still open:** whether the field-test parser accepted a clause such as `AT END MOVE ...`; both surviving samples use only `DO` and `GO TO`. Recommended implementation: accept any single imperative statement (F p. 40; J 02.07.05), compile it out-of-line with a return to the statement after the GET as the DO case demonstrates, and diagnose non-transfer clauses at low severity. (F p. 40, p. 109; J 02.07.05–06; J 90.02.04, 90.02.28–29, 90.02.32; J 90.04.01 msgs 92, 93, 106; J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 195, 201; images/page-142.png, page-183.png, page-201.png)\n\n=== Q41 — variant to append to the matching §8.5.6 catalog entry, \"AT END: 'any imperative clause' (F) vs 'a single imperative statement only' (J)\" (line 3891) ===\n\n**Narrowed (90.02 mining, 2026-08-01):** the object code fixes the mechanism. The AT END exit is the address field of the third word of the IOCS READ calling sequence — `PZE END-OF-FILE-PROCEDURE,,ERROR-PROCEDURE` (J 90.02.04), defaulting to the terminating routine SYS)265 when no AT END is coded (J 90.02.29). A coded clause is compiled as a compiler-generated out-of-line block whose label goes in that field: `PZE GN)058,,SYS)283` with `GN)058  AXT *+3,7 / SXA END.OF.MASTERS,4 / TRA END.OF.MASTERS+1` for `AT END DO END.OF.MASTERS`, and `GN)062  TRA END.OF.DETAILS` for `AT END GO TO END.OF.DETAILS` (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 201). Two facts follow: the DO form's return link is set to GN)059 — the code of the sentence *after* the GET — so a returning AT END clause continues in line; and even the trivial GO TO gets a generated block, indicating (inference) that the clause passes through ordinary code generation, so the runtime interface imposes no transfer-only restriction. What restricts it, if anything, is the parser: msg 106,00 shares its wording with the bare-name FILE-card operands of msgs 92,00/93,00. Implement as one imperative statement, out-of-line, returning to the statement after the GET; treat a non-transfer clause as accepted-but-warned. (J 90.02.04, 90.02.29, 90.02.32; J 02.07.05–06; J 90.04.01 msgs 92, 93, 106; J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 195, 201; images/page-142.png, page-201.png)","remaining_open":"Q47: nothing. It is fully resolved affirmatively — OPEN ALL FILES appears in J's own sample source, compiled with zero diagnostics, and generates the documented open-all subroutine call SYS)175/IOC)1. The only residue is stylistic: J never restates OPEN's object-time semantics, so F p. 39's account of what opening does (label read/check, buffer fill, record count) remains the sole description, as §6.4 already records. Optional follow-up edit: §6.4 (line 2443) currently says \"J does not restate OPEN semantics; it refers the reader back to the General Information manual\" — that sentence could gain \"though J's generated-code appendix and sample listing confirm both forms are implemented (J 90.02.13–14; J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 195, 200)\".\n\nQ41: the parse question is unresolved. Nothing in either manual, in the 210-message diagnostic table, or in the two surviving sample programs exercises an AT END clause whose verb is not DO or GO TO. The object-code interface is now known to be clause-agnostic (an out-of-line generated block, entered by transfer from IOCS, with the in-line continuation address known to the compiler), and the semantics of a returning clause are now verified (execution resumes at the statement after the GET). What cannot be recovered is whether the 1962 field-test parser's AT END routine would accept a non-transfer imperative or issue msg 106,00 against it. Only a surviving compiler listing/source, or a period program using e.g. `AT END MOVE ...`, could close it; it is unresolvable from J28-6169 and F28-8043 alone.","notes":"Aggregate status is reported as \"narrowed\" because the object covers two questions with different outcomes: Q47 is RESOLVED (affirmative), Q41 is NARROWED. The proposed_annotation is split into three clearly labelled blocks — Q47's Open-Questions annotation, Q41's Open-Questions annotation, and a shorter Q41 variant for the matching §8.5.6 catalog entry at line 3891 (Q47 has no §8.5 entry; it exists only in the Open Questions list at line 4213).\n\nVerification method: every load-bearing reading was checked against the 150-dpi page scans, not just the Markdown — images/page-142.png (the END-OF-FILE-PROCEDURE / ERROR-PROCEDURE calling-sequence template, where the transcription had applied an OCR correction), page-153.png (SYS)175/176/177), page-183.png (msgs 92, 93, 106), page-195.png (source OPEN ALL FILES and both AT END forms), page-200.png (START TSX SYS)175,4 / PZE IOC)1), page-201.png (the compiled AT END blocks GN)058 and GN)062). All matched the conversions.\n\nTwo incidental findings outside the two questions, worth logging elsewhere:\n1. Q40 (DO return linkage, line 4203) is largely answered by the same page. The AT END DO block shows the convention concretely: `AXT *+3,7` loads the return address into the index registers (tag 7 = all three on the 709), `SXA END.OF.MASTERS,4` stores it into the *procedure's own entry cell* (00331, the word labelled END.OF.MASTERS), and control enters at `END.OF.MASTERS+1`. That is a per-procedure return cell, not a stack — which directly supports the §8.5.5 conclusion that recursion is unsupported (a re-entry overwrites the cell) while distinct simultaneously-active DOs do not interact. (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 201; images/page-201.png.) A separate pass could resolve Q40 on this evidence.\n2. The object listing renders the IOCS record-transfer pseudo-op as `IOCTN*` (e.g. `IOCTN*  BL)2,,15`) where J 90.02.04 and 90.02.29 print `IOCDN*`. Both spellings are as printed in their respective sources; not load-bearing here, but it is a genuine T/D divergence between the appendix and the sample listing that a future scan pass might want to record.\n\nTerminology check for the annotation: J 90.02.32's SYS)283 paragraph contains the printed typo \"the 'ON ERROR' option in not used\" (already flagged in that chunk's conversion notes); I paraphrased rather than quoted it in the annotation text to avoid importing the typo into the language definition, and quoted it verbatim only in the evidence list."}

{"question_id":"9PAC","facts":[{"claim":"9PAC is a SHARE-developed business-oriented programming system for the IBM 7090 (or 709 with Data Channel Trap), consisting of two generators — the File Processor and the Reports Generator — that build 'object programs' from parameter decks rather than from flowcharted procedural code.","citation":"external: J28-6166, PDF p. 1 / printed p. (unnumbered cover) and PDF p. 8 / printed p. 6","verbatim_quote":"SHARE 7090 9PAC is a business-oriented programming system which facilitates the establishment and maintenance of data files and enables the user to obtain any desired report on this data with a minimum of programming effort... 9PAC does not use this type of language [procedural]... 9PAC object programs have a predetermined logic (or generalized framework) into which are inserted various modules that are designed to do the various functions which the programmer specifies in his source language program."},{"claim":"9PAC's file model: a file is composed of records (identified by 'record type', analogous to a business-form number), each record divided into 'fields' (and referenceable 'partial fields'); a file may have mixed record types of differing length, and every master file carries a self-describing 'dictionary' at the start of its first reel.","citation":"external: J28-6166, PDF p. 16 / printed p. 14, PDF p. 17 / printed p. 15, PDF p. 22 / printed p. 20","verbatim_quote":"A file is composed of records, each record containing information about some area of activity in the file... Each record may be divided into separate pieces of information called fields... Any file may have mixed record types providing each record type has a fixed length. If no dictionary is attached, all record types must have the same length; if a dictionary is attached, each record type may have a different length."},{"claim":"9PAC 'Grouping' lets a lower-numbered 'group header' record carry information common to a following run of detail records of higher record type(s); the generators keep group headers available for automatic 'look back' reference. This is the closest analogue found to COMTRAN's PATTERN concept of grouping several record types within one file, though it is presented as a general file-structuring idea, not a named language keyword.","citation":"external: J28-6166, PDF p. 17 / printed p. 15","verbatim_quote":"GROUPING. A number of records may be grouped together when it is desired that one record carry some information that is common to all of the records in a group... Grouping must be planned in the file, but it does not require special coding other than that the group header (the record being referred to) have a lower record type than the detail records pertaining to it. The File Processor and the Reports Generator automatically recognize records treated as group headers and keep them available for ready \"look back\" reference. Thus, at any time, reference may be made to the previous record of each lower record type."},{"claim":"9PAC also has a formalized 'parent-offspring' relationship (a hierarchy of record types where a parent record supplies part of the sequencing key for the offspring records that immediately follow it), letting redundant sequence-field data be omitted from offspring records — a second, more structural analogue to PATTERN-style multi-record-type files. It can only be used in a master file and precludes sorting.","citation":"external: J28-6166, PDF p. 20 / printed p. 18","verbatim_quote":"This relationship merely means that the parent record contains some of the sequencing information for the offspring records... As indicated earlier, a file which utilizes a parent-offspring relationship may not be sorted; this is due to the fact that a record which is to be sorted must contain all sequence fields."},{"claim":"Tape label handling in 9PAC is delegated wholesale to IOCS standard labels, controlled through *FILE control-card columns (Labeling Conventions, Reel Sequence Number of first reel, File Serial Number, Retention Days); no 9PAC-specific label format is defined.","citation":"external: J28-6166, PDF p. 21 / printed p. 19, PDF p. 28 / printed p. 26","verbatim_quote":"Files may be labeled or unlabeled. A labeled file is one which contains control information at the beginning of a reel of tape (header label) and/or at the end of a reel of tape (trailer label)... All labels must be standard IOCS labels. (...the reader is referred to the IBM Reference Manual 709/7090 Input/Output Control System, Form C28-6100-1.)"},{"claim":"No explicit 'record-count checking' mechanism (e.g., a trailer-record count verified against records actually read/written) is described anywhere in Part 1. The only integrity-checking machinery present is physical/block-level: Block Check Sums and Block Sequence Numbers, both restricted to binary files.","citation":"external: J28-6166, PDF p. 22 / printed p. 20","verbatim_quote":"BLOCK CHECK SUMS. Binary files may have standard IOCS block check sums if block sequence numbers are present. BLOCK SEQUENCE NUMBERS. Binary files may have standard IOCS block sequence numbers."},{"claim":"Shared/period vocabulary found: 'Record Mark' (BCD character, card code 0-8-2) appears in 9PAC's own collating-sequence table; 'record type', 'field', 'sequence field', 'control (field) break', 'master file', 'change file'/'transaction file', and file-description control cards (*JOB, *FILE, *END) are all used essentially as COMTRAN/COBOL-era report-processing vocabulary. No 'Group Mark' term appears anywhere in Part 1.","citation":"external: J28-6166, PDF p. 32 / printed p. 30, PDF p. 23 / printed p. 21, PDF p. 24 / printed p. 22","verbatim_quote":"Record Mark   0-8-2"},{"claim":"The *JOB/*FILE/*END IOCS control cards are 9PAC's file-description-card mechanism: *JOB declares job type (RGGEN/RGBIN/FPGEN/FPBIN) and global switches; *FILE (one per input/output file) declares physical characteristics — dictionary density, max block length, close-file action, file number, I/O unit(s), density, mode (BCD/binary), labeling, block-sequence/checksum flags, checkpoint conventions, retention days, file name; *END terminates the IOCS card group before 9PAC parameter packets.","citation":"external: J28-6166, PDF p. 23 / printed p. 21 through PDF p. 29 / printed p. 27"}],"answer_summary":"Read all 34 PDF pages of J28-6166 (SHARE 7090 9PAC Part 1: Introduction and General Principles, 1961; poppler-utils had to be installed via `brew install poppler` first to render the scan).\n\n(a) What 9PAC is / file-record model: 9PAC is a program-generator system (not a procedural language) for the IBM 7090/709, split into the File Processor (file establishment and maintenance — insertion, deletion, modification, applied as 'vertical', 'update', or 'horizontal' changes merged from change files against a master file, producing a new master, an error file, and an optional activity file) and the Reports Generator (reads a master or activity file and produces printed reports — heading/detail/total lines — or auxiliary tape files, without altering the input). Both are 'program generators': the programmer fills parameter fields on coding forms (e.g. File Processor functions SEL, MTCH, FLD; Reports Generator format/relationship forms) that are slotted into a 'predetermined logic (generalized framework)' to produce the object program — explicitly contrasted with flowchart-driven procedural languages. The file model: File → Records (typed by 'record type', explicitly compared to a business-form number) → Fields (with 'partial field' addressing by increment+length). Files may mix record types of different lengths if a dictionary is attached. Records are strictly sequenced by 'sequence fields'; a 'sequence field break' (= control field break) triggers totals/report breaks. 'Summary records' (File Processor) / 'total lines' (Reports Generator) accumulate totals from other records' fields at a sequence break. Every master file must carry an attached 'dictionary' (B/P begin-end, E=record-type header, F=field description, G=summary definition records — Appendix B) describing every record type's fields, mode, size, min/max range, and parent-offspring links.\n\n(b) PATTERN / label / record-count analogues: Two structural concepts resemble COMTRAN's PATTERN (grouping several record types of one file): 'Grouping' (p.15, quoted above) — a lower-numbered 'group header' record carries data common to detail records that follow, with automatic 'look-back' reference — and the more formal 'parent-offspring relationship' (pp.18-19/20-21), where a parent record type supplies part of the sequence key for its offspring record types that immediately follow it, letting the file omit redundant sequencing data (at the cost of losing sortability). Neither is a named single keyword like PATTERN, but both address the same problem (multiple related record types coexisting in one sequenced file). Tape label handling is *not* a 9PAC-specific mechanism — it is explicitly delegated to 'standard IOCS labels' (referencing the IOCS manual C28-6100-1) and configured via *FILE-card columns (Labeling Conventions H/L/blank, Reel Sequence Number of first reel, File Serial Number, Retention Days). Record-count checking as such (e.g., trailer-count verification) does **not** appear anywhere in Part 1; the only integrity checks described are physical/block-level 'Block Check Sums' and 'Block Sequence Numbers', both restricted to binary files and both requiring the other to be present.\n\n(c) Shared vocabulary: 'Record Mark' (BCD char, card code 0-8-2) appears in 9PAC's own two collating-sequence tables (Appendix C) — pure 7090/709 hardware/BCD vocabulary, not a 9PAC invention. No 'Group Mark' term appears anywhere in Part 1. File-description-card vocabulary is present via the *JOB/*FILE/*END IOCS control cards (Chapter 3): *JOB declares job type and system-wide switches; *FILE (one card per file) declares the physical characteristics (mode BCD/binary, density, blocking, labeling, checkpoint conventions, file name, etc.); *END closes the IOCS card group. Other shared report/file-processing terms: 'record type', 'field'/'field number', 'sequence field', 'control break' (= sequence field break), 'master file', 'change file'/'transaction file', 'heading line'/'detail line'/'total line', 'insertion'/'deletion'/'modification', 'vertical'/'update'/'horizontal' change.\n\n(d) Assessment of fetching Parts 2 & 3: Worth it only selectively. Part 1 already answers the file-structure and label questions reasonably fully (grouping, parent-offspring, dictionary format, *FILE label fields) and shows no record-count-checking terminology exists at this level of the system — that absence is itself informative but not conclusive, since Part 2 (File Processor, J28-6167) is exactly where match/validation logic (SEL, MTCH, FLD functions) and any object-time error/record-count checks would be spelled out in detail; it is the more promising of the two remaining parts if the open question specifically concerns record-count verification or the mechanics of matching change records to master records (which Part 1 only sketches via the toy 'project/sub-project' example). Part 3 (Reports Generator, J28-6168) mainly elaborates report-layout coding (heading/detail/total line 'pictures', control breaks, accumulation) already introduced in Part 1's worked example, and seems the less likely of the two to contain new PATTERN/label/record-count material — it would matter more if the open question concerns COMTRAN's printed-report or accumulation vocabulary specifically. Net: fetch Part 2 if the driving question is about change-matching, error detection, or record counting; Part 3 is lower priority and probably only needed for report-format/accumulation questions.","proposed_annotation":"","confidence":"probable"}
