If it differs from the number of blocks actually read in, a sequence error is given. In this case it should be noted that a sequence error for the reel can occur even though each block is not being checked for sequence.' PDF41 (p.33) Section 9 'Block Sequence Numbers and Check Sums': every FCB carries a block sequence number for the current reel, written into the trailer label at end of reel and checked when the trailer is read; separately, binary files can carry an optional per-block sequence+checksum word (18-bit folded checksum) appended to each block, transparent to data processing. PDF75 (p.67), Appendix A, FCB Word 4 bits 3-17: 'Counter for block sequence checking' -- this is the running in-memory counter IOCS increments per block and compares against the label's Block Count / per-block sequence words. FCB Word 2 bits 10-11 (PDF74/p.66) select whether a block-sequence word and/or checksums are present/to be computed."},{"topic":"Files with no labels (unlabeled) or nonstandard labels","pdf_pages":"32-34, 74","printed_pages":"24-26, 66","notes":"PDF32-33 (p.24-25) 'Non-Standard Labels' (see target-2 notes for full detail on the entirely-replaced or partially-replaced IOCS labeling routines). PDF34 (p.26) Section 6 'Unlabeled File Procedures', three subsections: 'Single-Reel Unlabeled Files' (any number of EOF marks; each EOF suspends buffering and gives an EOF exit; programmer must recognize the true end); 'Multi-Reel Unlabeled Files' (exactly one EOF mark per reel, written automatically at EOT; each EOF gives an exit and triggers reel switching/resumed buffering); 'Multi-File Reels' (several labeled or unlabeled files may share one physical reel by closing each without rewind before opening the next; caveat that BSF with count>1 may not land at true file start because of EOF marks from labels/trailers/checkpoint records). PDF74 (p.66), Appendix A FCB Word 2 bit 9: 'Label: 0-No, 1-Yes' is the flag distinguishing labeled vs unlabeled files; Word 2 bit 12 'Reel control flag' (single- vs multi-reel, or label-search-on-open for labeled files) is also relevant to unlabeled multi-reel handling."}]}
```json
{"outline":"22-6528-4_7090Manual.pdf, 156 PDF pages. Front matter PDF pp.1-6 (cover, blank, copyright, preface, TOC, photo) are unnumbered; printed page numbers begin at 7 and equal the PDF page number thereafter with NO offset (PDF p.N = printed p.N for N≥7) — confirmed by reading page-bottom folios through p.156.\n\n- System Description ................................ printed/PDF 7-17\n- Computer Instructions, Commands, Orders ....... 18-76\n  - Fixed Point Operations 20-24, Floating Point 25-30, Shifting 31-32,\n    Word Transmission 33-34, Control Operations 35-44 (CAS/LAS on p.43),\n    Index Transmission 44-47, Logical Operations 47-49,\n    Sense Indicator Operations 50-55, Convert Instructions 55-57,\n    Input-Output Operations 57-60, I/O Transmission 60-61,\n    Data Channel Commands 61-65, Channel Trap/System Compatibility 65-67,\n    7909 Data Channel commands 67-76\n- Interrupt ........................................... 77-79\n- Input-Output Components ............................ 80-107\n  - Magnetic Tape Units 80 (BCD character-alteration table),\n    Disk Storage 80-83 (incl. 7909 BCD-translation table p.83),\n    7340 Hypertape Drive 83-85 (track/BCD-bit chart p.84),\n    1414-6 I/O Synchronizer 85-89, 7607 Data Channels 89-96,\n    Card Reader 96-98, Card Punch 99-101,\n    Printer 102-107 (Type-wheel/punched-card code charts p.103)\n- Programming Examples .............................. 108-129\n  (Assembly 109-111, Packing/Unpacking 112-113, Subroutines 114-115,\n   Convert Instructions 115-119 [collating-sequence remark p.115-116],\n   Sense Indicators 120-121, Floating Overflow/Underflow 122-125,\n   Format Track / Disk 126-130)\n- Appendix ............................................ 131-153\n  A. Number Systems and Conversion 131-135 (numeric only, no char chart)\n  B. Octal-Decimal Integer Conversion Table 136-139 (numeric only)\n  C. Octal-Decimal Fraction Conversion Table 140-141 (numeric only)\n  D. Powers of Two 142-143\n  E. SCAT Mnemonic Operation Codes 144-146\n  F. Listing of Instructions (alphabetic/numeric) 147-151\n  G. Instructions by Operation Group 152-153\n- Index ............................................... 154-156","targets":[{"topic":"PRIMARY BCD/punched-card character code chart (digit+zone -> letter/digit/special-char, called 'standard IBM code' in body text) — best single target for full character-set extraction","pdf_pages":"103","printed_pages":"103","notes":"Three figures on one page, in the 'Printer' subsection of Input-Output Components: Fig. 81 'Type Wheel Schematic (FORTRAN)' (circular diagram of all 48 type-wheel characters with 12/11/0/N-zone and 8-3/8-4 digit-impulse labels); Fig. 82 'Alternate Type Wheel Characters' (table of alternate special chars &,.,□,-,$,*,%,#,@,/,+,) etc. by zone/digit combo — a second print-chain option, wheel-rows A-G); Fig. 83 'Punched Card Code (FORTRAN)' (clean table: digit 1-9 x {no(N) zone, 12(Y) zone, 11(X) zone, 0 zone} -> characters, plus no-digit row giving +/-/0, and 8-3/8-4 rows giving =,.,$/  ' , ( ). Body text explicitly calls this 'the standard IBM code shown in Figure 83.' NOTE: labelled '(FORTRAN)' — this is the FORTRAN-configured print chain's character set, presented as embodying the standard IBM BCD/Hollerith code; worth flagging that caveat when citing."},{"topic":"BCD character alteration table (tape <-> core zone-bit mapping) + explicit BCD digit codes, zero code, and BCD tape-mark code","pdf_pages":"80","printed_pages":"80","notes":"Magnetic Tape Units section. States: digits 1-9 = binary 000001-001001 (zone 00); zero = 001010 on tape, altered to 000000 in core on read; 'the integer 15 (001111) is identical to the BCD tape mark, signifying an end-of-file condition.' Zone-bit alteration table: Numeric BA=00->00, A-to-I 01->11, J-to-R 10->10, S-to-Z 11->01 (core vs tape). Also mentions a BCD 'blank' character that suppresses printing/punching. No table of A-Z/0-9 -> full 6-bit codes here, just digits/zero/blank/tape-mark and the zone-alteration rule."},{"topic":"7909 Data Channel BCD Translation table (Core Storage vs Disk Storage 6-bit configs, B A 8 4 2 1)","pdf_pages":"83","printed_pages":"83","notes":"Optional-feature table for disk-storage BCD translation; rows include numeric 0-9 (with the same 000000<->001010 zero mapping as p.80), 'A to I'/'J to R'/'S to Z' zone groups marked '(any)'/'(other)', not an enumerated per-letter chart — a bit-pattern remapping rule, not a character list."},{"topic":"7340 Hypertape track/bit-position chart for BCD Code and Binary Code (Check bits C0/C1, B, A, 8,4,2,1 per physical track)","pdf_pages":"84","printed_pages":"84","notes":"This is a track-assignment diagram (which physical tape track carries which bit), not a character-to-code chart."},{"topic":"CAS (Compare Accumulator with Storage) and LAS (Logical Compare Accumulator with Storage) instruction definitions — the two comparison instructions relevant to collating/sort order","pdf_pages":"43","printed_pages":"43","notes":"CAS: compares c(AC) to c(Y) ALGEBRAICALLY (signed fixed-point magnitude compare; note 'a plus zero is algebraically greater than a minus zero' stated for TLQ on p.39, same convention applies). LAS: treats c(AC)_{Q,P,1-35} as unsigned 37-bit and c(Y) as unsigned 36-bit and compares as unsigned magnitudes -- this is the instruction that gives BCD character strings their natural ascending/descending order, since it orders purely by the numeric value of the 6-bit-per-character BCD word. No explicit prose 'collating sequence' table accompanies these definitions here; the ordering must be inferred by combining LAS's unsigned-compare semantics with the BCD code values in Fig. 83 (p.103)."},{"topic":"Explicit textual reference to 'modification of collating sequences' as a purpose of the CRQ/CVR/CAQ convert (table-lookup) instructions","pdf_pages":"115-116","printed_pages":"115-116","notes":"Opening paragraph of 'Convert Instructions' programming-example section: '...These convert instructions provide the programmer with a rapid means of performing such operations as BCD-to-binary and binary-to-BCD conversion, BCD arithmetic, editing of records, and modification of collating sequences.' (sentence spans bottom of PDF/printed p.115 to top of p.116). This CONFIRMS the manual's premise that the machine's native/default collating sequence is the raw BCD bit-pattern order (per Fig.83, p.103) and that CRQ-style table lookups are the documented mechanism for imposing a different (e.g. business/alphameric) collating sequence for sorting. No separate table gives the actual default sequence explicitly as a list — it must be derived from the Fig. 83 code chart plus LAS's unsigned-compare rule (p.43)."},{"topic":"NEGATIVE FINDING: no dedicated 'BCD code chart' appendix, and no table anywhere in this manual naming record mark, group mark, lozenge, plus-zero/minus-zero, or delta as special BCD characters","pdf_pages":"131-156 (Appendix + Index) plus full-manual Index check pp.154-156","printed_pages":"131-156","notes":"Appendix A (131-135, Number Systems/Conversion), B (136-139, Octal-Decimal Integer Conversion Table), C (140-141, Octal-Decimal Fraction Conversion Table), D (142-143, Powers of Two), E (144-146, SCAT mnemonics), F (147-151, instruction listing), G (152-153, instructions by group) are ALL purely numeric/instruction reference material -- none is a character-code chart. Full-manual Index (pp.154-156) was scanned: has 'Character Alteration in BCD Mode....80' and 'Type Wheel Characters....[Printer subsection, ~103]' but no entries for 'record mark', 'group mark', 'lozenge', 'delta', or 'BCD code chart'/'collating sequence' as such. Appendix E/F do list SCAT pseudo-op mnemonics PZE ('Plus Zero') and MZE ('Minus Zero') (pp.144,149) but these are ASSEMBLER CONSTANT-DEFINING PSEUDO-OPS (for building signed-zero machine words), not BCD special characters -- do not conflate the two. Conclusion: this A22-6528-4 manual does not contain the record-mark/group-mark/lozenge-style special-character catalog; that vocabulary, if it exists in the period literature at all, is more likely to be found in 1401-family or COBOL/COMTRAN-adjacent documents, not here."}]}
{"question_id":"45","facts":[{"claim":"The IOCS manual states the label length explicitly as 14 words, in the sentence introducing the standard label formats. This is the direct corroboration of J28-6169's \"14 word area\".","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 29 / printed p. 21","verbatim_quote":"IOCS will automatically process 14-word labels of the following formats:"},{"claim":"The 14-word figure is restated a second time, in the Non-Standard Labels section, together with the label area's symbolic location (IOCS+43).","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 32 / printed p. 24","verbatim_quote":"3. The standard label area at symbolic locations IOCS + 43 (see page 39) will be used; its length is 14 words."},{"claim":"The 14-word figure appears a third time in the IOCS storage-conventions table, as the symbol LAREA occupying exactly IOCS+43 through IOCS+56 (= 14 consecutive words). This is the physical area COMTRAN's IOC)29 and the LABEL type code redefine.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 47 / printed p. 39","verbatim_quote":"LAREA | IOCS + 43 to IOCS + 56 | Label area (14 words)"},{"claim":"HEADER LABEL, field-by-field table; columns are Word(s) / BCD Position(s) / Content / Description. Word 1 is the label identifier.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 30 / printed p. 22","verbatim_quote":"1 | 1- 6 | 1HDRbb | Label identifier"},{"claim":"Header word 2 is the tape serial number (5 digits preceded by a blank).","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 30 / printed p. 22","verbatim_quote":"2 | 7-12 | bXXXXX | Tape serial number"},{"claim":"Header word 3 is the file serial number.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 30 / printed p. 22","verbatim_quote":"3 | 13-18 | bXXXXX | File serial number"},{"claim":"Header word 4 is the reel sequence number: 4 digits flanked by blanks.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 30 / printed p. 22","verbatim_quote":"4 | 19-24 | bXXXXb | Reel sequence number"},{"claim":"Header word 5 is the creation date, coded as 2-digit year, blank, 3-digit day-of-year.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 30 / printed p. 22","verbatim_quote":"5 | 25-30 | XXbYYY | Creation date / XX - Year / YYY - Day of year (001-365)"},{"claim":"Header word 6 is the retention period in days, right-justified 3 digits in the 6-character word.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 30 / printed p. 22","verbatim_quote":"6 | 31-36 | bbbXXX | Retention days"},{"claim":"Header word 7 is a packed flags word: its six BCD character positions 37-42 carry a blank, file density, file mode, block check sum indicator, block sequence indicator, and checkpoint record indicator. The manual lists these as five table rows all attributed to word 7.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 30 / printed p. 22","verbatim_quote":"7 | 37-38 | bX | File density / 0 - Low / 1 - High\n7 | 39 | X | File mode / 0 - BCD / 1 - Binary\n7 | 40 | X | Block check sum indicator (Binary files only) / 0 - No check sums / 1 - Check sums present\n7 | 41 | X | Block sequence indicator (Binary files only) / 0 - No sequence word appears / 1 - Sequence word is present\n7 | 42 | X | Checkpoint record indicator / 0 - No checkpoint record / 1 - Checkpoint record follows label"},{"claim":"Header words 8-10 hold the 18-character BCD file name — the field checked against the FILE card's file name.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 30 / printed p. 22","verbatim_quote":"8-10 | 43-60 | X....X | BCD file name"},{"claim":"Header words 11-12 are unused but reserved for Sort compatibility; words 13-14 are unused. The header ends at BCD position 84 = 14 words x 6 characters, confirming the 14-word length arithmetically.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 30 / printed p. 22","verbatim_quote":"11-12 | 61-72 | Arbitrary | Not used, but reserved for 709/90 Sort compatibility\n13-14 | 73-84 | Arbitrary | Not used"},{"claim":"TRAILER LABEL, complete field-by-field table — same 14-word length and same BCD-position numbering, but only three words are defined. Word 1 is the identifier, taking one of two values distinguishing end-of-reel from end-of-file.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 31 / printed p. 23","verbatim_quote":"1 | 1- 6 | 1EORbb | End of reel trailer\n         or\n         1EOFbb | End of file trailer\n2 | 7-12 | bXXXXX | Number of blocks in file\n3 | 13-18 | bXXXXX | Unit control word\n4-14 | 19-84 | Arbitrary | Not used"},{"claim":"The trailer's word-2 count is a BLOCK count, not a record count, and the prose defines it per-reel even though the table column glosses it 'Number of blocks in file'. It is verified on input against blocks actually read; a mismatch raises a sequence error. No record count appears in either label (bears on open question 50).","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 31 / printed p. 23","verbatim_quote":"BLOCK COUNT is the number of data blocks on this reel. This is checked for every input reel. If it differs from the number of blocks actually read in, a sequence error is given. In this case it should be noted that a sequence error for the reel can occur even though each block is not being checked for sequence."},{"claim":"Trailer word 3, the Unit Control Word, is a storage address rather than data — it points at the Unit Control Block for the drive the reel was prepared on.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 31 / printed p. 23","verbatim_quote":"UNIT CONTROL WORD is the location in storage of the first word of the Unit Control Block which specifies the tape unit the reel was prepared on, and the logical number it was assigned."},{"claim":"Which trailer identifier is written is determined by the event: 1EOR at end of reel during writing, 1EOF at close. On reading, 1EOR drives automatic tape switching silently; 1EOF produces the EOF exit.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 31 / printed p. 23","verbatim_quote":"Note: During writing, an EOR label is prepared by IOCS when the end of a reel is encountered. An EOF label is prepared when the file is closed."},{"claim":"A third standard 14-word format exists: the BLANK REEL label, written card-to-tape, whose table is dimensioned in Card Column(s) rather than BCD Position(s). Inference/observation: its columns stop at 80 while the 14-word label runs to character 84, so the final 4 characters of word 14 cannot be supplied from an 80-column card.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 29 / printed p. 21","verbatim_quote":"A temporary header label can be placed on an otherwise blank reel by a card-to-tape operation. It should have the following format:\nWord(s) | Card Column(s) | Content | Description\n1 | 1-6 | 1BLANK | Label identifier\n2 | 7-12 | bXXXXX | Tape serial number\n3-14 | 13-80 | Arbitrary | Not used"},{"claim":"Field semantics for the header's serial/sequence fields, which define how COMTRAN's SERIAL/REEL Environment information maps into the label.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 30 / printed p. 22","verbatim_quote":"TAPE SERIAL NUMBER identifies the serial number of the physical reel. It is recommended that the number which externally identifies the reel be used as the tape serial number for the label.\n\nFILE SERIAL NUMBER is the tape serial number of the first reel of the file. This number is taken from the label of the first reel on which the output file is prepared.\n\nREEL SEQUENCE NUMBER is the number of the reel within a given file (i.e., the first reel of a file is reel 0001, the second is reel 0002, etc.)."},{"claim":"The three header fields IOCS validates at open and at each new reel are file serial number, reel sequence number, and file name — i.e. header words 3, 4 and 8-10. Failure gives LABEL ERROR, INPUT REEL INVALID.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 31 / printed p. 23","verbatim_quote":"1. The serial number given on the FILE card must agree with the file serial number in the label.\n2. The reel sequence number in the label must agree with the reel sequence number on the FILE card, unless, of course, reel switching has occurred, in which case IOCS will have advanced the reel sequence number stored in the File Control Block.\n3. The file name given on the FILE card must agree with the file name in the label. If no file name is given on the FILE card, this test is bypassed."},{"claim":"The header's creation-date and retention-days fields (words 5-6) gate reuse of an output reel; an output reel must carry either a 1BLANK label or an expired 1HDR label.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 32 / printed p. 24","verbatim_quote":"Every reel upon which a labeled output file is to be prepared must have a label written on it. This label may be a 1BLANK type or a 1HDR type on which (Creation Date + Retention Days) has been reached. If these conditions are not found, either or both of the following messages will be printed:\n\nLABEL ERROR, OUTPUT REEL INVALID\nLABEL ERROR, RETENTION NOT EXPIRED"},{"claim":"Physical placement and recording mode of the labels on tape: header label, optional checkpoint record, data blocks, trailer label, each label followed by an EOF mark; header labels always BCD in either density; trailer always BCD at the file's density.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 29 / printed p. 21","verbatim_quote":"2. Header labels are always written in the BCD mode and may be in either high or low density.\n3. The EOF mark following the header label is in the same density as the label.\n4. The optional checkpoint record and the EOF following it are in the same density as the file.\n5. The file itself may be either high or low density.\n6. The EOF mark, the trailer label, and its EOF mark are always written in the BCD mode in the same density as the file itself."},{"claim":"Bonus, bearing on Open Question 46 (FOR LABEL calling convention): the non-standard label package is reached through an entry address in the File Control Block and consists of five fixed entry points MYLBLS..MYLBLS+4 with prescribed TRA n,1 returns. Bit 35 of word 7 of the label area is the checkpoint-skip flag on return from entry 1.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 33 / printed p. 25","verbatim_quote":"1. TSX MYLBLS, 1 — To check header label input file ... TRA 1, 1 if label is correct / TRA 2, 1 if the input label is invalid ... Bit 35 of the 7th word of the label area must be 1 upon either return if a checkpoint record is to be skipped.\n2. TSX MYLBLS + 1, 1 — To check trailer label, input file ... Upon return, the sign of the MQ must be: + if EOR; tape switching will occur / - if EOF; an EOF exit will be taken\n3. TSX MYLBLS + 2, 1 — To check label on tapes to be used for output ... TRA 1, 1 if the reel may be used / TRA 2, 1 if (creation date + retention days) has not been reached / TRA 3, 1 if it is not possible to use the reel\n4. TSX MYLBLS + 3, 1 — To prepare output trailer label ... bits P, 1-35 of the AC must contain: 1EORbb if an EOR trailer is to be prepared / 1EOFbb if an EOF trailer is to be prepared\n5. TSX MYLBLS + 4, 1 — To prepare output header label / Return is: TRA 1, 1"},{"claim":"The File Control Block carries the labeled/unlabeled flag, the label-routine entry address, and the running block counter compared against the trailer's Block Count.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF pp. 74-75 / printed pp. 66-67","verbatim_quote":"Word 2, bit 9* — Label: 0 - No / 1 - Yes\nWord 3, bits 3-17 — Location of entry point for standard or non-standard label image routines.\nWord 4, bits 3-17 — Counter for block sequence checking."},{"claim":"The block sequence number written into the trailer is maintained per reel in the FCB and is separate from the optional per-block sequence/checksum word available on binary files.","citation":"external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 41 / printed p. 33","verbatim_quote":"Every File Control Block carries a block sequence number for the current reel of the file. On a labeled file, this sequence number is always written in a trailer label at the end of each reel and checked when it is read."}],"answer_summary":"Fully resolved. C28-6100-2 gives the complete standard label layout in two tables (header, printed p.22; trailer, printed p.23) plus a third blank-reel format (printed p.21). The number 14 appears three times in three independent forms: the prose \"IOCS will automatically process 14-word labels of the following formats:\" (PDF 29 / printed 21); \"The standard label area at symbolic locations IOCS + 43 (see page 39) will be used; its length is 14 words.\" (PDF 32 / printed 24); and the storage-conventions table entry \"LAREA | IOCS + 43 to IOCS + 56 | Label area (14 words)\" (PDF 47 / printed 39), where 43..56 inclusive is exactly 14. It is also implicit in the tables' BCD-position column, which runs 1-84 = 14 words x 6 BCD characters. This is precisely the area J28-6169 calls IOC)29, \"a 14 word area within IOCS which is used to process all labels\" (J 90.02.08), and the area the LABEL type code redefines (J 02.05.03) — the external source corroborates the COMTRAN manuals rather than contradicting them.\n\nHEADER LABEL (word: BCD positions, content, meaning): 1: 1-6 `1HDRbb` label identifier; 2: 7-12 `bXXXXX` tape serial number; 3: 13-18 `bXXXXX` file serial number; 4: 19-24 `bXXXXb` reel sequence number (first reel 0001); 5: 25-30 `XXbYYY` creation date (2-digit year, blank, day-of-year 001-365); 6: 31-36 `bbbXXX` retention days; 7: a packed flag word whose six character positions are 37 blank, 38 file density (0 low / 1 high), 39 file mode (0 BCD / 1 binary), 40 block check sum indicator (binary only), 41 block sequence indicator (binary only), 42 checkpoint record indicator (1 = checkpoint record follows label); 8-10: 43-60 18-character BCD file name; 11-12: 61-72 \"Not used, but reserved for 709/90 Sort compatibility\"; 13-14: 73-84 not used.\n\nTRAILER LABEL: 1: 1-6 `1EORbb` \"End of reel trailer\" or `1EOFbb` \"End of file trailer\"; 2: 7-12 `bXXXXX` block count; 3: 13-18 `bXXXXX` unit control word (address of the first word of the Unit Control Block for the drive the reel was prepared on); 4-14: 19-84 not used. Header and trailer are therefore the same length and share only the convention that word 1 is a six-character identifier beginning `1`; their bodies are entirely different and the trailer defines only 3 of its 14 words. IOCS writes 1EOR at end of reel during writing and 1EOF at close; on reading, 1EOR triggers silent tape switching, 1EOF (or any non-EOR record where a trailer is expected) causes the EOF exit.\n\nCount field: the only count in either label is a BLOCK count, in trailer word 2. The table gloss says \"Number of blocks in file\" but the prose defines it per reel — \"BLOCK COUNT is the number of data blocks on this reel. This is checked for every input reel. If it differs from the number of blocks actually read in, a sequence error is given.\" The running value is FCB word 4 bits 3-17, \"Counter for block sequence checking\". There is no record count in any standard label, which bears directly on Open Question 50.\n\nThird format, BLANK REEL (dimensioned in card columns because it is punched card-to-tape): word 1 cols 1-6 `1BLANK`, word 2 cols 7-12 `bXXXXX` tape serial number, words 3-14 cols 13-80 not used. An output reel must carry either this or a 1HDR whose (creation date + retention days) has been reached, else LABEL ERROR, OUTPUT REEL INVALID / LABEL ERROR, RETENTION NOT EXPIRED.\n\nBonus material recovered en route: the three header fields IOCS validates at open and each reel switch (file serial no., reel sequence no., file name = words 3, 4, 8-10), the label recording-mode/density rules, and the full five-entry-point non-standard label package convention (MYLBLS..MYLBLS+4 with prescribed TRA n,1 returns, IR2 = 2's complement of FCB address), which is strong evidence for Open Question 46.","proposed_annotation":"**Resolved (external source, 2026-08-01):** The IOCS manual gives the layout in full and confirms the length three independent ways: \"IOCS will automatically process 14-word labels of the following formats:\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 29 / printed p. 21); \"The standard label area at symbolic locations IOCS + 43 (see page 39) will be used; its length is 14 words.\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 32 / printed p. 24); and the storage-conventions table entry \"LAREA | IOCS + 43 to IOCS + 56 | Label area (14 words)\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 47 / printed p. 39), 43–56 inclusive being exactly 14 words. LAREA is the area J calls IOC)29, \"a 14 word area within IOCS which is used to process all labels\" (J 90.02.08), and the area the LABEL type code redefines (J 02.05.03). The label is 14 words = 84 BCD characters, and the manual's tables index it by BCD position 1–84.\n\nHeader label — word (BCD positions) content, meaning (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 30 / printed p. 22): 1 (1–6) `1HDRbb`, label identifier; 2 (7–12) `bXXXXX`, tape serial number; 3 (13–18) `bXXXXX`, file serial number; 4 (19–24) `bXXXXb`, reel sequence number (\"the first reel of a file is reel 0001, the second is reel 0002\"); 5 (25–30) `XXbYYY`, creation date (\"XX — Year\", \"YYY — Day of year (001-365)\"); 6 (31–36) `bbbXXX`, retention days; 7, a packed flag word — position 38 file density (0 low, 1 high), 39 file mode (0 BCD, 1 binary), 40 block check sum indicator (binary files only), 41 block sequence indicator (binary files only), 42 \"Checkpoint record indicator / 0 – No checkpoint record / 1 – Checkpoint record follows label\"; 8–10 (43–60) BCD file name; 11–12 (61–72) \"Not used, but reserved for 709/90 Sort compatibility\"; 13–14 (73–84) not used. Words 3, 4 and 8–10 are exactly the three fields IOCS validates against the FILE card at open and at the start of each new reel, failure giving `LABEL ERROR, INPUT REEL INVALID` (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 31 / printed p. 23) — the object-time counterpart of COMTRAN's SERIAL/REEL/RETAIN information (J 02.06.12).\n\nTrailer label — same 14 words, only three defined (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 31 / printed p. 23): 1 (1–6) `1EORbb` \"End of reel trailer\" or `1EOFbb` \"End of file trailer\"; 2 (7–12) `bXXXXX` block count; 3 (13–18) `bXXXXX` unit control word, \"the location in storage of the first word of the Unit Control Block which specifies the tape unit the reel was prepared on, and the logical number it was assigned\"; 4–14 (19–84) not used. Header and trailer therefore share only their length and the convention that word 1 is a six-character identifier beginning `1`. \"During writing, an EOR label is prepared by IOCS when the end of a reel is encountered. An EOF label is prepared when the file is closed.\"\n\nThe only count in either label is a **block** count, in the trailer. Its table gloss reads \"Number of blocks in file\", but the accompanying prose defines it per reel: \"BLOCK COUNT is the number of data blocks on this reel. This is checked for every input reel. If it differs from the number of blocks actually read in, a sequence error is given\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 31 / printed p. 23); the running value is held in File Control Block word 4 bits 3–17, \"Counter for block sequence checking\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 75 / printed p. 67). No *record* count appears in any standard label — see open question 50.\n\nA third standard format, the blank-reel label, is dimensioned in card columns because it is written card-to-tape: word 1 (cols 1–6) `1BLANK`, word 2 (7–12) `bXXXXX` tape serial number, words 3–14 (13–80) not used (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 29 / printed p. 21). A reel accepted for labeled output must carry either a `1BLANK` label or a `1HDR` label on which (creation date + retention days) has been reached, else `LABEL ERROR, OUTPUT REEL INVALID` or `LABEL ERROR, RETENTION NOT EXPIRED` (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 32 / printed p. 24).","confidence":"certain"}
```

(iv) No entry in J 90.02.00\u201390.02.33 tests, branches on, or clears SYS)130, and the compiled sample never references it. **Inference (not stated in either manual):** the overflow condition belongs to the move/convert path alone \u2014 matching F's \"In the process of storing the final result of a SET command\" (F p. 44, verified on F28-8043 images/page-050.png) \u2014 and intermediate upscale/downscale/multiply/divide truncation cannot raise it. This rests on SYS)163\u2013171 lying outside MOVPAK altogether, not merely on their lacking a test step, and it is exactly why the compiler diagnoses the scaling losses statically, where they are computable from the declared pictorials: \"DOWNSCALE GENERATED WHICH LOSES ALL SIGNIFICANT FIGURES.\" (27,00) and \"UPSCALE MAY CAUSE HIGH ORDER TRUNCATION FOR STORE INTO 'NAME.1'\" (199,00), the latter itself attributing the loss to the store, both under the heading \"THE FOLLOWING ERRORS WERE DETECTED DURING COMPILATION-\" (J 90.04.01).\n\n**Added qualification (new, from the compiled sample and the ID-source converts):** *no* store of an arithmetic result emits a documented overflow test, whatever the target format \u2014 the gap is universal, not peculiar to internal-decimal targets. A SET's result reaches the store as internal decimal in the AC/AC-MQ, and each of the four store paths open to it is testless. **Internal decimal right justified:** no MOVPAK call at all. `SET WORKING GROSS = (WORKING HOURS * 1.5 - 20) * MASTER RATE` (statement 203,00) into a field declared `IR9(5)V99` compiles to inline machine arithmetic ending `MPY 1)RATE,2 / XCA / ACL CP)+32 / LRS 35 / DVP CP)+33 / STQ 4)GROSS`, and `SET WORKING FICA = .03 * WORKING GROSS` (211,00) into `IR9(4)V99` compiles as `LDQ CP)+9 / MPY 4)GROSS / XCA / ACL CP)+34 / LRS 35 / DVP CP)+31 / STQ 4)FICA` \u2014 multiplication, inline downscaling by `LRS 35` and `DVP`, and a bare store, with no MOVPAK call, no SYS)163\u2013171 call and no overflow test anywhere (J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 206, 210; verified on images/page-210.png; the `AXT 0` word preceding the FICA sequence is the BEGIN SECTION linkage cell for statement 210,00, loaded by `SXA FICA.ROUTINE,4` on PDF p. 206, not part of the SET). **External decimal:** SYS)186/187/188, \"a MOVPAK subroutine which converts from internal decimal in the AC or AC-MQ to unsigned external decimal\" (and the two overpunch-sign variants) \u2014 a single `TXI SYS)18n,1,NUMBER-OF-CHARACTERS-TO-DEVELOP` word with no follow-on instruction list and so no test step (J 90.02.18). **Edited:** SYS)267, \"This MOVPAK subroutine converts from internal decimal in the AC-MQ to form an edited field\" \u2014 three words (`TXI` / `OCT TARGET-CONTROL-WORD-BITS` / `AXT NUMBER-OF-DIGITS-TO-CONVERT, 1`), again no test step (J 90.02.30); the sample exhibits it, `MOVE CORRESPONDING GRAND.TOTALS TO PAYRECORD` (statement 199,00) storing internal-decimal totals into the `88889.99` print fields as `CLA 6)GROSS / TSX SYS)180,4 / PZE 2)GROSS,,1 / TXI SYS)267,1,4 / OCT 000005000004` (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 203). **Internal decimal not justified:** SYS)246, one word, no test step (J 90.02.26). The five families that do carry a TEST-FOR-OVERFLOW step are all character-source routines \u2014 the no-arithmetic case (`SET alpha.field.1 = alpha.field.2` and field-to-field moves) and operand-side conversions. **Inference:** two readings survive and the manuals decide between them nowhere. Either (a) detection is intrinsic to MOVPAK's numeric converts \u2014 which is what SYS)130's class-wide wording most naturally says, the explicit `TXI` steps then being required only by the character-scripted families that need an instruction per character group; or (b) ON OVERFLOW compiles inline code the appendix never exhibits. Reading (b) is not architecturally implausible: the compiler demonstrably implements SET's *other* store-time option inline in the fixed-point path \u2014 MOVPAK rounds with a scripted step (\"TRA  SYS)219               Round current character\", J 90.02.16, 90.02.23) whereas the fixed-point sequences round by the `ACL CP)+nn` that precedes `LRS 35 / DVP CP)+nn` (absent from the plain rescale at `CLA 5)NETPAY / LRS 35 / DVP CP)+24` on PDF p. 203) \u2014 though that reading of the `ACL` constant is inference from the object code, not stated anywhere.\n\nThree residuals keep this short of resolved. First, the generated ON OVERFLOW sequence is nowhere shown: J's own SET write-up never mentions the clause (J 02.04.05), OVERFLOW survives only in the key-word list (J 02.03.02), the deferred-features appendix does not defer it (J 90.01), and the compiled sample uses no ON OVERFLOW \u2014 so how the object program suppresses the store, as F promises (\"instead of storing the erroneous result\"), is unrecoverable, and MOVPAK detecting overflow *while* moving characters sits awkwardly with a store that never happens. Second, and consequently, it is unknown whether an arithmetic SET could raise the condition at all under any target format, since none of its four store paths is documented to test. Third, the same MOVPAK families also run on the *operand* side of a statement \u2014 the sample converts a 5-character edited field to internal decimal with `TSX SYS)182,4 / TXI SYS)268,1,1 / TXI SYS)269,1,5 / TXI SYS)275,1,5` before accumulating it, for `ADD BONDORDER BONDENOMINATION TO INTERNAL.TOTALS BONDPURCHASES` (statement 221,00; J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 212) \u2014 and **inference:** since SYS)130 is described as one cell set by any MOVPAK numeric move or convert, it must be a statement-wide flag rather than a store-only one, even though only stores are documented as raising the language-level condition; \"final store only\" is therefore established against the arithmetic scaling routines SYS)163\u2013171, not against every MOVPAK call the statement makes. Also unresolved: whether the 7090's own AC-overflow indicator plays any role in the inline fixed-point arithmetic (the manuals never mention it, nor any divide check). (Not counted among the overflow-test steps above: SYS)231\u2013234 occupy that slot in the family calling-sequence lists at J 90.02.16\u201319 but their own entries print \"NUMBER-OF-CHARACTERS-TO-TEST-FOR-OVERPUNCH\", verified on images/page-163.png \u2014 a genuine printed inconsistency, preserved.) (F p. 44; J 02.03.02; J 02.04.05; J 90.01; J 90.02.10, 90.02.12\u201313, 90.02.15\u201321, 90.02.24, 90.02.26, 90.02.29\u201332; J 90.04.01 msgs 27, 199; J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 203, 206, 210, 212; images/page-148.png, page-151.png, page-152.png, page-155.png, page-163.png, page-210.png; F28-8043 images/page-050.png)\n\n---\n\nMatching variant to append to the \u00a78.5.4 entry **\"Overflow with no ON OVERFLOW clause\"**:\n\n**Amended (90.02 re-mining + 90.05 listing evidence, 2026-08-01):** the \"recorded in SYS)130\" half of this resolution needs narrowing. No routine is individually documented to set the cell; SYS)130's entry names a class \u2014 \"any one of the numeric move or convert subroutines of MOVPAK\" (J 90.02.10) \u2014 and the only routines carrying an explicit `NUMBER-OF-CHARACTERS-TO-TEST-FOR-OVERFLOW` step are the five character-source families SYS)183, 185, 189, 190 and 268 (J 90.02.15\u201321, 90.02.30\u201332). Nothing outside MOVPAK can arm it at all: the fixed-point scaling and arithmetic routines SYS)163\u2013171 are never called MOVPAK subroutines and are never said to set any cell (J 90.02.12\u201313). On the store side, an arithmetic result held in the AC/AC-MQ reaches its target either by a bare `STQ`/`STO` (internal decimal right justified \u2014 `SET WORKING FICA = .03 * WORKING GROSS` into an `IR9(4)V99` field compiles as `LDQ CP)+9 / MPY 4)GROSS / XCA / ACL CP)+34 / LRS 35 / DVP CP)+31 / STQ 4)FICA`, J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 210, verified on images/page-210.png; likewise `STQ 4)GROSS` at PDF p. 206) or by a MOVPAK convert that carries no test step \u2014 SYS)186\u2013188 for external decimal (J 90.02.18), SYS)267 for edited fields (J 90.02.30, exhibited at PDF p. 203 as `CLA 6)GROSS / TSX SYS)180,4 / PZE 2)GROSS,,1 / TXI SYS)267,1,4`), SYS)246 for internal decimal not justified (J 90.02.26). The behaviour is unchanged \u2014 the truncated result is stored and execution continues \u2014 but whether the flag cell records it on the store at all is undetermined: for an internal-decimal target no MOVPAK routine runs, and for the other targets the routine that runs is a MOVPAK numeric convert with no documented test. (J 90.02.10, 90.02.12\u201313, 90.02.15\u201321, 90.02.26, 90.02.30; J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 203, 206, 210; images/page-210.png)"}}

I re-read the page scans images/page-148.png, page-151.png, page-152.png and page-206.png: SYS)163/164 (\"single precision AC\", \"the number in the MQ\", \"10**10\"), SYS)165/166/167/168/169/170/171/172, SYS)128-129 and the statement-203,00 instruction chain with RS)0=01621 / RS)1=01623 all confirmed. All ten octal->decimal conversions are correct (CP)+6=15, +7=20, +9=3, +11=18, +12=13, +24=1,000,000, +31=100, +32=500, +33=1000, +34=50), the scale algebra checks out in both branches of 203,00 and in 211,00 and 215,00, the TARGET-CONTROL-WORD decode (prefix/address/decrement vs 88889.99 and 8889.99) is right, no SYS)163-171 call appears anywhere in the sample, there is no source-level division, and \u00a78.5.4 genuinely has no matching entry. The draft's catch that \u00a74.2.2 line 1461 (\"double-precision binary integer work registers\") is wrong is correct and its suggested replacement is sound.", "CITATION ERROR - declared formats.
