The DVP CP)+24 store-side split occurs on PDF p. 203 (`CLA 5)NETPAY`) and twice on p. 209 (`CLA 3)NETPAY`). Correct range: pp. 192-196, 203, 206, 209-211, 215-216.", "CITATION ERROR - negative finding. The survey item cites \"J 02.03.01\" for the intermediate-compilation-tape use of the word \"intermediate\". The sentence (\"**C.** An intermediate tape containing a deck of the form described in (A) above will be produced...\") sits at 02-compiler.md line 103, i.e. inside 02.02.01 (PDF 12); the 02.03.01 marker begins at line 105. The MOVE-chart uses (02.04.03) and F pp. 84-85 are cited correctly.", "OVER-CLAIM - \"20 digits in double\". The annotation states \"the answer to the digit question is 10 decimal digits carried in single precision and 20 in double\" and frames it as \"stated by the manual only piecewise\". It is not. J states only that fixed-point double precision is \"formats representing more than 10 digits\" (J 02.05.06 d) and that such a field occupies two words (J 02.05.04). Nothing anywhere gives a double-precision digit capacity; 20 is an unlabelled inference (two decimal halves of 10**10), and on the raw-binary reading 70 magnitude bits would carry ~21 digits. The double-precision digit count must be reported as unknown.", "OVER-CLAIM - the 10**10 quantum. \"The quantum of every precision change is 10**10\" and \"the promotion and demotion step being a factor of 10^10\" over-read the appendix. Four of the nine routines change scale with no 10**10 step at all: SYS)165 and SYS)166 \"upscale... by the constant located at CP)+NN\", SYS)167 \"downscales... by the constant located at CP)+NN and leaves the result in the AC-MQ\", SYS)170 \"scales the product down by the constant located at CP)+NN\". And the manual's own promotion trigger is an operand's declared format (J 02.05.06 d) under the mode rule of J 02.04.05.01 - not a scale factor. The simpler reading, which the draft never considers, is that 10**10 is the largest power of ten a single 35-bit word can hold (10**11 > 2**35-1), so any scale change exceeding it must be done as two multiplies/divides; that this coincides with the 10-digit boundary is the same word-size fact seen twice, not a separate promotion rule. Either way it is inference and must be labelled.", "OVER-CLAIM - the CONSERVE SPACE argument, stated as fact. \"SYS)163-171 are the CONSERVE SPACE realization of scaling and double-precision arithmetic; the default mode generates the same operations in line, which is why the compiled sample calls none of SYS)163-171\" is not established. Decisively against it: the SYS)163-171 range contains **no** routine for an ordinary single-precision scale by a constant - every one of the nine involves either the 10**10 step or the AC-MQ pair - so it cannot be the complete CONSERVE SPACE scaling package, and the sample's non-use of them is fully explained by the sample's own limits (largest scale factor 10**6, widest field IR9(5)V99), which the draft itself concedes under \"Still open\". The in-line MPY/DVP scaling in the sample therefore tells us nothing about the CONSERVE mode.", "MISSED EVIDENCE that repairs the previous point. J 02.06.16 states the default outright, two paragraphs above the CONSERVE SPACE sentence the draft quotes: \"The compiler normally generates comparison type instructions based on the 709/7090 collating sequence and an object program which is conservative of time rather than space.\" Combined with the fact that the sample's ENVIRONMENT division carries no OPTION card at all (only FILE and SPECIF cards, J 90.05 listing PDF p. 195), this establishes the default-mode point directly and makes the SYS)163-171 inference unnecessary.", "CATEGORY SLIP. SYS)172 is described as a \"floating point exponential routine\"; enlisting it in the fixed-point argument that \"the appendix does not use double-precision registers uniformly\" mixes modes. SYS)163 and SYS)164 - which are decimal-scaling, i.e. fixed-point, routines - carry that point on their own.", "UNQUOTED LOAD-BEARING TEXT. The evidence item for SYS)169 supplies only the SYS)160 sign-adjustment quote. SYS)169's own wording is load-bearing and reverses the operand roles relative to SYS)170/171: \"This routine divides the double precision fixed point number in SYS)128 and SYS)129 by the AC-MQ. The result is left in the AC-MQ.\" (J 90.02.13) - dividend in the cells, divisor in the registers. Worth stating, since residual (3) is about how a source-level divide is staged.", "MINOR. Evidence item 4's claim text (\"Evaluation begins in single precision fixed point\") is an implicature of J 02.04.05.01, not its text - harmless here only because the annotation body never asserts it (and \u00a74.2.2 line 1459 already covers it). Also: the annotation cites SYS)180 material as \"J 90.02.17, 90.02.30\"; SYS)180 itself is defined in 90.02.15. And a supporting observation the draft missed - the compiler emits the literal 20's upscale as a runtime `LDQ CP)+7` / `MPY CP)+31` rather than folding it to the constant 2000, which is direct evidence that the align-by-upscale step is a generic mid-expression rule and not constant arithmetic."], "corrected_annotation": "**Narrowed (90.02 mining + 90.05 listing evidence, 2026-08-01; adversarially re-verified against the page scans, 2026-08-02):** No prose rule exists anywhere \u2014 neither manual states a working digit count for intermediates or says where scaling steps fall, and F's only precision language is expressly about the endpoint: \"Ordinarily the result of the SET operation will be rounded to the number of places indicated by the format description of the result field or fields\" and \"In the process of storing the final result of a SET command it is possible for a loss of significant high-order digits to occur\" (F p. 44); the word \"precision\" does not occur in F at all. The appendix and the compiled sample together nevertheless fix the machine model. **Working width.** Fixed point is \"a binary integer arithmetic with decimal scaling\" (J 02.04.05) whose unit is the machine word: a right-justified internal numeric field \"appears by itself in the low order positions of a full word (two if double precision) with the sign value in the sign bit of the word\" (J 02.05.04), and \"Fixed point double precision numbers are denoted in the Data Description by formats representing more than 10 digits\" (J 02.05.06 d). 90.02 carries that one-word/two-word split into the registers, and \u2014 contrary to a first reading of the appendix, and to the present \u00a74.2.2 wording \u2014 the fixed-point routines are *not* uniformly double precision: SYS)163 \"upscales the single precision AC by 10\\*\\*10\", SYS)164 \"upscales the number in the MQ by 10\\*\\*10\", while SYS)165\u2013171 work on the AC-MQ pair and SYS)160 is \"a subroutine for sign adjustment for double precision fixed point numbers \u2026 entered with the number in the AC-MQ\", the second multi-precision operand living in two dedicated cells \u2014 \"SYS)128, SYS)129 These two cells serve as storage for multi precision arithmetic operations\" (J 90.02.10\u201313, 90.02.11.01; verified on images/page-148.png, page-151.png, page-152.png). So the attested working width is **one 35-bit word for single precision and the AC-MQ pair for double** \u2014 a register statement, not a digit count. *Inference (not manual text):* a 709/7090 word is a sign bit plus 35 magnitude bits and 2\u00b3\u2075\u22121 = 34,359,738,367, so one word holds every 10-digit decimal integer and not every 11-digit one \u2014 which is why the declared field boundary is 10 digits, and why 10\u00b9\u2070 is the largest power of ten a one-word constant-pool scale factor can hold. **The double-precision digit capacity is nowhere stated and must not be guessed:** \"more than 10 digits\" is all J says; 20 would follow only if the pair is a decimal split at 10\u00b9\u2070, whereas 70 magnitude bits would carry about 21. The recurring 10\\*\\*10 step is best read the same way rather than as a promotion quantum. SYS)163 and SYS)164 each \"upscale \u2026 by 10\\*\\*10 and then upscale by the constant located at CP)+NN\"; SYS)168 \"downscales the double precision AC-MQ by 10\\*\\*10 and then downscales by the constant located at CP)+NN leaving the result in the MQ\"; SYS)171 multiplies AC-MQ by SYS)128 SYS)129 and \"scales the product down by 10\\*\\*10, and then downscales by the constant located at CP)+NN\" \u2014 but their partners do the same jobs without it: SYS)165 and SYS)166 \"upscale \u2026 by the constant located at CP)+NN\", SYS)167 \"downscales \u2026 by the constant located at CP)+NN and leaves the result in the AC-MQ\", SYS)170 \"scales the product down by the constant located at CP)+NN\" (J 90.02.12\u201313). *Inference, and the correction of this question's own premise:* the 10\\*\\*10 is not the mode-promotion step \u2014 promotion is triggered by an operand's declared format (J 02.05.06 d) under the rule of J 02.04.05.01 \u2014 but the two-step form of a scale change whose factor exceeds one word's largest power of ten; its coincidence with the 10-digit boundary is the same word-size fact seen twice. Consistently, the routine that sheds ten digits delivers one register: SYS)168 leaves \"the result in the MQ\". Two double-precision layouts are documented, distinguished only by which half is high \u2014 SYS)165 takes the AC-MQ plain, SYS)166 takes it \"On entry to the routine, the high order part of the number is in the MQ and the low order in the AC\" \u2014 *inference:* respectively what an MPY leaves and what a DVP leaves, the machine placing a double-length product high-half-in-AC but a quotient in the MQ with the remainder in the AC. Division stages its operands the opposite way from multiplication: SYS)169 \"divides the double precision fixed point number in SYS)128 and SYS)129 by the AC-MQ. The result is left in the AC-MQ\" \u2014 dividend in the cells, divisor in the registers (J 90.02.13). **Where the scaling falls.** Scale is a compile-time attribute computed from the pictorials before any code is emitted: hence the compile-time diagnostics \"DOWNSCALE GENERATED WHICH LOSES ALL SIGNIFICANT FIGURES.\" (27,00) and \"UPSCALE MAY CAUSE HIGH ORDER TRUNCATION FOR STORE INTO 'NAME.1'\" (199,00), the latter itself attributing the loss to a store (J 90.04.01), and hence the compiler's own conversion parameter block, whose fields are \"**Address** \u2014 = Scale applied to internal decimal value\", \"**Prefix** \u2014 is the sign of the scale\" and \"**Decrement** \u2014 = Numeric length of value\" \u2014 scale and digit count carried as an explicit pair (J 90.02.27, CONTROL-WORD-TYPE-ID; the scientific-decimal CONTROL-WORD-TYPE-SD likewise pairs \"Scale applied to mantissa in pictorial\" with \"Total length in characters, of the field\", J 90.02.26). The compiled sample exhibits the placement directly. It carries no OPTION card \u2014 its ENVIRONMENT division holds only FILE and SPECIF cards (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 195) \u2014 so it was compiled in the default mode: \"The compiler normally generates comparison type instructions based on the 709/7090 collating sequence and an object program which is conservative of time rather than space\" (J 02.06.16), i.e. \"CONSERVE TIME requests an object program which will be executed in a minimum amount of time; this is achieved by the generation of in-line instructions to perform the operations. This is the normal mode of operation\" (J 02.06.17), as against CONSERVE SPACE, in which \"subroutines are generated once for operations such as scaling, double precision arithmetic, etc., in-line calling sequences to the subroutines are used to perform the operations\" (J 02.06.16). *Caution against a tempting inference:* the sample calls none of SYS)163\u2013171, but that is **not** evidence about the mode \u2014 no routine in that range performs an ordinary single-precision scale by a constant (each involves either the 10\\*\\*10 step or the AC-MQ pair), so the set cannot be the whole CONSERVE SPACE scaling package, and the sample needs none of them, its largest scale factor being 10\u2076 and its widest field `IR9(5)V99`. Statement 203,00, `IF WORKING HOURS GT 40.0 THEN SET WORKING GROSS = (WORKING HOURS * 1.5 - 20) * MASTER RATE OTHERWISE SET WORKING GROSS = WORKING HOURS * MASTER RATE` (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 196), over `3)HOURS IR99V9` and `4)GROSS IR9(5)V99` (PDF p. 194) and `1)RATE IR99V999` (PDF p. 192) \u2014 scales 10\u00b9, 10\u00b2, 10\u00b3 \u2014 compiles to `LDQ CP)+6` / `MPY 3)HOURS` / `STQ RS)1+0` / `LDQ CP)+7` / `MPY CP)+31` / `STQ RS)0` / `CLA RS)1` / `SUB RS)0` / `XCA` / `MPY 1)RATE,2` / `XCA` / `ACL CP)+32` / `LRS 35` / `DVP CP)+33` / `STQ 4)GROSS`, the constants being CP)+6 = 15 (the literal 1.5 as a scaled integer), CP)+7 = 20, CP)+31 = 100, CP)+32 = 500, CP)+33 = 1000 (J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 206, 215\u2013216; images/page-206.png, page-216.png). Three rules read straight off it and off the other two SETs: **(i)** additive operands are brought to a common scale by *upscaling the lesser-scaled one* mid-expression \u2014 the literal 20 is multiplied by 100 before the subtract, and emitted as a runtime `LDQ CP)+7` / `MPY CP)+31` rather than folded to the constant 2000, i.e. as a generic alignment step; likewise in `SET WORKING WHT = 0.18 * (WORKING GROSS - 13 * MASTER EXEMPTIONS)` (215,00) the integer product `MPY EXEMPTIONS,1` is followed immediately by `MPY CP)+31` to reach GROSS's two decimal places before the `SUB` (J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 196, 210\u2013211; images/page-210.png); **(ii)** products merely accumulate scale and are *never* downscaled mid-expression \u2014 `LDQ CP)+6` / `MPY 3)HOURS` / `STQ RS)1+0` parks 1.5\u00d7HOURS in a Result Storage cell at its full accumulated scale, and the expression reaches 10\u2075 before its single scaling step even though every operand and the result have at most three decimal places; **(iii)** the one downscale is generated at the store, as a single `DVP` by the accumulated excess power of ten immediately preceding the `STQ` into the result field \u2014 by 1000 for statement 203,00's overtime branch, by 100 for its straight-time branch, for `SET WORKING FICA = .03 * WORKING GROSS` (211,00) and for statement 215,00. Rounding is folded into that same divide as an `ACL` of exactly half the divisor beforehand \u2014 CP)+32 = 500 against divisor 1000, CP)+34 = 50 against divisor 100 \u2014 the machine form of F's rule that the retained digit \"is increased by 1 when the part removed is greater than or equal to one-half\" (F p. 115, ROUND), applied where F says it is applied, at the store and not to intermediates. *Two inferences from 7090 instruction semantics, not manual text:* the `STQ` of a product keeps only the MQ, discarding the AC half, and a chained `MPY` re-multiplies the MQ, so a single-precision intermediate really is one 35-bit word and the high half of every single-precision product is dropped; and `LRS 35` / `DVP` is the idiom for a divide with a properly positioned double-length dividend, the `ACL` carry surviving across the shift. Result Storage is nevertheless sized for the wider case: 90.02.03 says only that RS cells \"temporarily store the results of a CT statement\" and that \"N is the sum of maximum Result Storage used in each section\", but in the sample RS)0 and RS)1 lie two words apart (01621 and 01623) and each section's block begins at an even offset from `RS) BSS 30` (01621, 01627, 01633, and 3.RS)1 at 01643) \u2014 *inference from the listing's LOC values*: two words per cell, i.e. every expression intermediate is allocated storage able to hold a double-precision value even where the code stores one word into it. **Still open:** the rule is reconstructed from one compiled program plus routine descriptions and is nowhere stated. The sample exercises no source-level division (every `DVP` in it is compiler-generated scaling), no `TRUNCATED` clause, and no field wider than 10 digits (its widest is `IR9(5)V99`), so double precision \u2014 SYS)128\u2013129, SYS)160, SYS)165\u2013171 \u2014 is attested only by the 90.02 prose and never seen in generated code; nothing shows the circumstances in which the compiler would prefer a mid-expression *downscale* to an upscale, although msg 27,00 proves it generates them; whether `TRUNCATED` merely suppresses the `ACL` half-divisor or changes the scaling plan is unattested; and the ceiling behind \"MAXIMUM NUMERIC LENGTH EXCEEDED. 'NAME.1' FORMAT USED.\" (35,00), and behind msg 52,00's \"MAXIMUM NUMERIC LENGTH EXCEEDED FOR CONSTANT OR LITERAL.\", is never given. One further store-side mechanism observed but not explained by any text: when the value stored has more digits than the target pictorial can hold, the compiler splits it with a divide by 10 raised to the target's digit count before the edit \u2014 `CLA 3)NETPAY` / `LRS 35` / `DVP CP)+24` (CP)+24 = 1,000,000) preceding `TSX SYS)180,4` / `PZE 1)NETPAY,,4` / `TXI SYS)267,1,4` / `OCT 000004000003` / `AXT 6,1`, where the source `WORKING NETPAY IR9(5)V99` has seven digits and the target edited pictorial `8889.99` six, while the seven-digit-to-seven-digit moves on the same listing page (`4)GROSS` \u2192 `2)GROSS 88889.99`, `OCT 000005000004`, `AXT 7,1`) carry no divide at all (J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 193, 203, 209; J 90.02.15, 90.02.17, 90.02.30) \u2014 *inference:* since SYS)267 \"converts from internal decimal in the AC-MQ to form an edited field\", the divide isolates the digits that fit as the remainder in the AC and the excess as the quotient in the MQ, matching the MQ-high/AC-low layout SYS)166 documents; but neither the purpose nor the overflow consequence is stated. (F pp. 44, 115; J 02.04.05, 02.04.05.01; J 02.05.04, 02.05.06 d; J 02.06.16\u201317; J 90.02.03, 90.02.10\u201313, 90.02.11.01, 90.02.15, 90.02.17, 90.02.26\u201327, 90.02.30; J 90.04.01 msgs 27, 35, 52, 199; J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 192\u2013196, 203, 206, 209\u2013211, 215\u2013216; images/page-148.png, page-151.png, page-152.png, page-206.png, page-210.png, page-216.png)"}}
{"key": "Q31", "verdict": {"verdict": "CORRECTED", "problems": ["FACTUAL ERROR in the annotation (the pass's headline new finding is misstated). The draft writes: 'the step lists under SYS)183/185/189/190 print \"TXI SYS)231, 1, NUMBER-OF-CHARACTERS-TO-TEST-FOR-OVERFLOW\"'. Only SYS)183's list prints SYS)231 (90.02-generated-code.md:758). SYS)185's list prints SYS)232 (line 816), SYS)189's prints SYS)233 (line 903), SYS)190's prints SYS)234 (line 948) \u2014 each its own subroutine, all four spelled OVERFLOW. The OVERPUNCH readings are the four standalone entries SYS)231-234 (lines 1300, 1308, 1316, 1324). The inconsistency is real (confirmed on images/page-155.png = 90.02.16 and images/page-163.png = 90.02.24) but the draft's description of it is wrong. Also, the note quoted ('The last character processed under control of this instruction is examined for source field sign.') is *Note 1 under SYS)183 and applies to a group of three instructions (SYS)227/231/235), and is renumbered *Note 3 in the SYS)185/189/190 lists.", "CITATION ERROR in evidence item 2. It quotes SYS)130 and SYS)134 together and cites 'J 90.02.10\u201311; images/page-148.png', claiming scan verification. SYS)134 is not on page-148 \u2014 that scan (checked directly) ends mid-SYS)132; SYS)134 is at 90.02.11 = images/page-149.png (90.02-generated-code.md:469, after the 90.02.11/PDF-149 marker at line 424). The project's own language definition already gets this right at docs/comtran-language-definition.md:1537 ('J 90.02.10, 90.02.11'). SYS)130 and SYS)131 are correctly on page-148.", "OVERREACH presented as stated fact: 'It is a Type 2 entry ... placed in core by the CT Loader (J 90.02.07)'. J 90.02.07 states the CT-Loader placement of Type 2 *subroutines* ('Subroutines contained on the system tape which are placed in core by the CT Loader as required by the particular object program being loaded'); of communication cells it says only that they are 'categorized in the same manner'. Loader placement of the cell is an inference from the categorization, not a statement about cells.", "QUOTE INACCURACY: 'each introduced as \"This MOVPAK subroutine\u2026\"'. The three wordings actually used are 'This MOVPAK subroutine\u2026' (SYS)183, 191-238, 246-258, 267-282), 'This is a MOVPAK subroutine which\u2026' (SYS)184, 186-190), and 'SYS)185 is a MOVPAK subroutine which\u2026' (SYS)185). Under this project's verbatim rule a purported uniform quote that is not uniform is an error; 'each identified in its own entry as a MOVPAK subroutine' is the accurate form. (The membership claim itself is correct: SYS)183-258 and 267-282 all exist with no gaps except the absent SYS)259, and SYS)179-182 are the entry points.)", "UNSUPPORTED AND FALSE AS WRITTEN: 'No J key-word list holds a data-condition word (J 02.03.02\u201303).' J's key-word list 2 contains OVERFLOW and list 3 contains ERROR (02-compiler.md:155, 175) \u2014 precisely the two conditions that *do* have options. No evidence item supports this claim (evidence 22 cites 02.03.03 only for the arithmetic-mode sentence). The defensible claim is narrower: J's key-word lists hold OVERFLOW and ERROR and no word naming an improper-data condition.", "NON-VERBATIM 'verbatim_quote' in evidence items 10 and 12. The 90.05 object listing lines carry octal location, instruction-word and flag columns ('00563      0535 00 1 01670   10001    COMPUTE.PAY     LAC   BL)3,1') which the draft silently drops. The symbolic content is exactly right and the page numbers check out (PDF 205 = file lines 1096-1115; PDF 212 = lines 1568-1574), but the extraction should be labelled as the symbolic columns, not offered as verbatim. Same for statement 202,00: the printed source has a double space after 'MASTER DATE,' and breaks across a continuation line (90.05-sample-program.md:518-519).", "LOOSE F CITATION: evidence item 20 attaches the MOVE general-form quote to 'F p. 44, p. 47, pp. 108\u2013109'. MOVE is printed on p. 109 (a2-supplementary-information.md, after the page-109 marker at line 176); pp. 44/47 are the ON OVERFLOW passages. The draft's own note 3 correctly rejects the old 'F p. 110' citation, so this should be tightened rather than left as a range.", "STYLE MISMATCH with the Open-questions list. Entries 27, 40, 41 and 42 (docs/comtran-language-definition.md:4187, 4203, 4204, 4205) are each a single flowing paragraph with inline bold lead-ins. The draft uses four paragraphs with bold sub-headings plus a `---` horizontal rule before the companion \u00a78.5.4 bullet; a `---` inside an ordered-list item terminates the list in Markdown and would break the numbering of items 32 onward.", "INFERENCE NOT LABELLED: 'so the single-exit MOVPAK sequence ... is a real fact about the interface rather than an abbreviation.' That the appendix spells out alternate exits *wherever the design has them* is generalized from two examples (SYS)162's three comparison returns, SYS)292's two conversion exits) plus SYS)261's error return; it is an inference, and the surrounding text labels its other inferences explicitly.", "NOTHING DECISIVE MISSED \u2014 checked and confirmed. Greps of both manuals for improper / invalid / non-numeric / unpredictable / object-time turn up nothing further; J 03-loader and 90.03 are silent on the cells; J 02.04.03-04 (the MOVE section) adds no validity rule. One adjacent passage the draft did not use is worth adding because it slightly narrows residual (1): J 02.04.04 e) states 'For the source fields of the scientific decimal type, a free form of data is allowed within the limits of the field' (with worked examples using embedded blanks), so 'improper data' cannot simply mean 'any character that is not a digit' \u2014 at least for SD sources, blanks and free placement are explicitly legal."], "corrected_annotation": "**Narrowed (90.02 mining, 2026-08-01; scan-verified 2026-08-02):** SYS)131 is a flag cell, not a mechanism. J defines it in one sentence \u2014 \"This cell is set non-zero whenever any one of the numeric move or convert subroutines of MOVPAK detects an improper data condition.\" (J 90.02.10, verified on images/page-148.png) \u2014 and gives it no calling sequence, unlike the SYS) routines and unlike the pointer cells SYS)132/133, which are shown with a `PZE LOC,,BYTE` format word; its siblings SYS)130 (overflow) and SYS)134 (floating-point underflow from a Move) are bare cells in the same way (J 90.02.10\u201311; images/page-148.png, page-149.png). It is a Type 2 entry, \"Type 2 entries received a number greater than 127\" and Type 2 communication cells being \"those communication cells which are a part of a particular object program\"; J states CT-Loader placement of Type 2 *subroutines* and says the cells are \"categorized in the same manner\", so loader placement of the cell follows by that categorization rather than being stated of cells (J 90.02.07). The setting side is bounded though not pinpointed: MOVPAK is entered at SYS)179\u2013182 and its members run SYS)183\u2013258 and SYS)267\u2013282 (no SYS)259 exists; each member is identified in its own entry as a MOVPAK subroutine), and the cell's own wording confines it to the *numeric* members \u2014 SYS)183\u2013238 (character-serial external-decimal and edited-field moves), SYS)246\u2013258 (internal-decimal / scientific-decimal / floating-point converts) and SYS)267\u2013282 (internal-decimal\u2194edited converts) \u2014 excluding the alphabetic and figurative-constant movers SYS)239\u2013245 (J 90.02.14\u201316, 90.02.25\u201328, 90.02.30\u201332). Within a generated MOVPAK call the only step kinds the appendix names are move, convert, develop, bypass, insert leading/trailing zeros, develop decimal zeros, test for overflow, test for overpunch, scan for sign, \"Round current character\", and the terminating target-numeric-length word (\"This type of MOVPAK call is always terminated by the instruction TXI SYS)223, 1, TARGET-NUMERIC-LENGTH\", J 90.02.16); none is a validity test. **Inference (not stated in either manual):** improper-data detection is therefore internal to the subroutines' own character loop rather than a separately generated step, which is why no compiled listing can display it. The appendix is itself inconsistent about the four character-examining steps SYS)231\u2013234: the collected step lists print them as OVERFLOW \u2014 \"TXI SYS)231, 1, NUMBER-OF-CHARACTERS-TO-TEST-FOR-OVERFLOW\" under SYS)183, and SYS)232, SYS)233, SYS)234 identically under SYS)185, SYS)189 and SYS)190 \u2014 each flagged with the note \"The last character processed under control of this instruction is examined for source field sign.\" (J 90.02.16\u201319; images/page-155.png), whereas the four standalone entries for the same subroutines all print \"NUMBER-OF-CHARACTERS-TO-TEST-FOR-OVERPUNCH\" (J 90.02.24; images/page-163.png). Both readings verified on the scans: a genuine print inconsistency in the 1962 manual, not an OCR artifact. **Nothing reads it.** Across the whole of J 90.02.00\u201390.02.33 no SYS) or IOC) entry is documented as testing, branching on, printing from, initialising or clearing SYS)131 or its two siblings; the three cells occur exactly three times in the appendix, in their own definitions. The field-test sample's object listing never names them either: its complete set of SYS references is 132, 133, 175, 177, 178, 180, 182, 184, 185, 190, 193, 198, 212, 214, 225, 226, 239, 240, 241, 243, 244, 245, 260, 267, 268, 269, 275, 283 and 294 (J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 198\u2013216). The decisive demonstration is statement 202,00, \"COMPUTE.PAY.    MOVE DETAIL DATE TO MASTER DATE,  MOVE DETAIL HOURS TO WORKING HOURS, PAYRECORD HRS.\" (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 196; printed across two lines). DETAIL HOURS is declared `99V9` \u2014 three characters, external decimal (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 192) \u2014 in a record the sample's own narrative describes as punched: \"Data Description entries specify that all fields of the detail records are in external mode since they are generated on a card punch. HOURS is the only field upon which arithmetic operations are performed, and consequently, is the only field specified as numeric (9's in description).\" (J 90.05.02). It is thus precisely the field most exposed to a blank or a stray punch. Its generated code (symbolic columns of the listing; the octal location and instruction-word columns are elided here) is `COMPUTE.PAY LAC BL)3,1` / `TXL SYS)294,1,0` / `CAL 2)DATE,1` / `LAC BL)2,2` / `TXL SYS)294,2,0` / `SLW 1)DATE,2` / `CAL BL)3` / `ACL CP)+52` / `SLW SYS)132` / `TSX SYS)182,4` / `TXI SYS)184,1,3` / `STO 3)HOURS`, followed by the same source field's second move `TSX SYS)182,4` / `TXI SYS)185,1,4` into the edited PAYRECORD HRS (`8889.9`), and then at +23 straight into statement 203's arithmetic, `CLA 3)HOURS` / `CAS CP)+5` (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 205) \u2014 SYS)184 being the MOVPAK routine that \"converts from external decimal to internal decimal leaving results in the AC or AC-MQ. The sign is assumed over the low order digit.\" (J 90.02.16), and WORKING HOURS being `IR99V9`, internal (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 194). The converted value goes straight to store and thence into the pay arithmetic with nothing tested, while those same twelve words carry two instances of the one inline object-time trap the compiler *does* emit, `TXL SYS)294,n,0`, which \"prints an error message whenever a reference is made to a Base Locator before the locator has been loaded, and exits back to the CT Monitor\" (J 90.02.33) \u2014 so the absence of a data check is a design decision, not a gap in the listing. The pattern repeats at the edited-to-internal-decimal convert `TSX SYS)182,4` / `TXI SYS)268,1,1` / `TXI SYS)269,1,5` / `TXI SYS)275,1,5` / `STO 3.RS)1`, which runs straight into `CLA 2)BONDPURCHASES` / `ADD 3.RS)1` (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 212). **Nor can a program name the condition.** The only condition options in the language are ON OVERFLOW, restricted to SET and ADD with a single result field (F p. 44, p. 47) \u2014 F's complete verb-format summary gives MOVE no option whatever, \"MOVE [CORRESPONDING] data.name.1 TO data.name.2, data.name.3, ... data.name.n\" (F p. 109, in the command list of F pp. 108\u2013109) \u2014 and the Environment FILE-card ON ERROR, which J confines to three I/O conditions: \"IOCS cannot continue processing after discovering an unrecoverable redundancy error, a block checksum error or a block sequence error without direction from the programmer. The ON ERROR option of the Environment FILE card provides for communication between IOCS and the programmer in these three error situations.\" (J 02.07.07). J's key-word lists hold OVERFLOW and ERROR \u2014 the two conditions that do have options \u2014 and no word naming an improper-data condition (J 02.03.02\u201303). CRYPT cannot even write the symbol \u2014 \"The characters, 'Quotation Mark,' 'Left Parenthesis,' 'Right Parentheis,' 'Dollar Sign' may not be used as part of a symbol\" (J 02.08.01; \"Parentheis\" sic) \u2014 and \"No debugging facilities are currently available.\" (J 90.01.05 B.3). **Reading (inference from the stated facts):** the move or arithmetic runs to completion, MOVPAK stores whatever its character loop produced, execution continues silently, and the event survives only as a non-zero cell that no documented code reads, prints from, or resets \u2014 of a piece with J's stated policy elsewhere, \"No object time check is made to insure that subscript references conform to the limits specified by the array dimensions in the Data Description.\" (J 90.01.02). **This is a different mechanism from the GET-path conversion check it is easily confused with.** SYS)261 is not a MOVPAK subroutine and does not operate on programmer data: \"Subroutine SYS)261 converts the logical accumulator from a BCD number to binary, checking for non-numeric characters and/or imbedded or trailing blanks; and leaves the result in the decrement of the AC. This routine is used in conjunction with getting a variable length BCD record from tape where the first word of the record gives the remaining length of the record. If an error is detected during conversion, return is 1,4 to routine SYS)263 which prints an error message.\" (J 90.02.29, verified on images/page-168.png). Four differences: its subject is the record's own length control word, not a described data field; its calling sequence is `TSX SYS)261,4` / `TSX SYS)263,6` / `(Normal Return)`, i.e. an explicit alternate error exit, which no MOVPAK numeric call has; its reaction is fatal, SYS)263 being the routine that \"prints an error message in conjunction with SYS)261 upon GET error condition (see SYS)261) and exits to the CT monitor\" (J 90.02.29); and it sets no cell. *Inference:* since the appendix does spell out alternate exits where the design has them \u2014 SYS)162's \"HIGH RETURN / EQUAL RETURN / LOW RETURN from comparison\" (J 90.02.12) and SYS)292's \"(Partial Conversion Exit)\" and \"(Total Conversion Exit)\" (J 90.02.33) \u2014 the single-exit MOVPAK sequence, always closed by its target-numeric-length word, is most likely a real fact about the interface rather than an abbreviation. Every object-time error printer in 90.02 (SYS)260 record length, 263 GET conversion, 264 record exceeding BLOCKSIZE, 265 unexpected end-of-file, 283 GET error, 291 FILE to card equipment in locating mode, 294 base locator not loaded) is I/O- or addressing-related and terminates the run, matching J's own inventory of object-time on-line messages: \"Object program error messages, usually concerning I/O errors. Object-time processing will terminate and control will revert back to the CT Monitor.\" (J 05.06.04). None concerns numeric data validity. The compiler's improper-data diagnostics are not object-time reactions either: messages 25,00 \"OPERATION IGNORED BECAUSE 'NAME.1' HAS IMPROPER DATA FORMAT ( E  A(2) ) FOR THIS USE.\", 67,00 \"THERE IS AN ILLEGAL NON-NUMERIC CHARACTER IN THE NUMERIC FIELD.\", 111,00, 112,00, 120,00 and 182,00 all stand under the heading \"THE FOLLOWING ERRORS WERE DETECTED DURING COMPILATION-\" and carry compilation severity codes, \"numbered 1 through 5\" (J 90.04.01\u201302); message 67 is the compile-time namesake of the object-time condition, not its handler. **Still open:** what MOVPAK counts as an \"improper data condition\" \u2014 it cannot simply be \"any non-digit\", since J allows scientific-decimal *source* fields free-form content, \"For the source fields of the scientific decimal type, a free form of data is allowed within the limits of the field\", with worked examples containing embedded blanks (J 02.04.04 e) \u2014 and the class matters, since \"Arithmetic operations are performed only in the internal (binary) mode\" (J 02.03.03), so every external field feeding arithmetic passes through one of these converts; which individual subroutine sets the cell; what the target field or accumulator holds afterwards; and whether the cell is ever cleared \u2014 all three flag cells are described only as \"set non-zero\", and no initialisation, reset or per-statement clearing is stated anywhere in J 90.02, J 90.03, or the Loader chapter J 03. (F p. 44, p. 47, pp. 108\u2013109; J 02.03.02\u201303; J 02.04.04; J 02.07.07; J 02.08.01; J 05.06.04; J 90.01.02, 90.01.05; J 90.02.07, 90.02.10\u201312, 90.02.14\u201319, 90.02.24, 90.02.25\u201333; J 90.04.01\u201302; J 90.05.02; J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 192, 194, 196, 198\u2013216; images/page-148.png, page-149.png, page-155.png, page-163.png, page-168.png)\n\nCompanion entry proposed for \u00a78.5.4 (Arithmetic and data manipulation), to sit beside \"Overflow with no ON OVERFLOW clause\" \u2014 to be inserted as a separate bullet there, not appended to the open-question entry:\n\n- **Invalid characters in a numeric field at object time.** Neither manual defines any program-level reaction, and no verb option can request one. *Resolution:* there is none \u2014 the MOVPAK numeric move/convert subroutines set communication cell SYS)131 non-zero when they detect \"an improper data condition\" (J 90.02.10) and carry on; nothing in the generated-code appendix reads, prints from, or clears that cell, the only condition options in the language are ON OVERFLOW (SET/ADD, single result field) and the FILE-card ON ERROR (unrecoverable redundancy, block checksum, block sequence only, J 02.07.07), and the field-test sample converts a card-punched external-decimal field (DETAIL HOURS, `99V9`) straight into pay arithmetic through SYS)184 with no check, while emitting two base-locator traps on the same twelve words (J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 192, 196, 205). The compile-time diagnostics (msgs 25, 67, 111, 112, 120, 182) and the fatal GET-path conversion check SYS)261/263, which validates a variable-length record's length control word and exits to the CT Monitor, are separate mechanisms and must not be read as the MOVE/arithmetic reaction. (F p. 44, p. 47, p. 109; J 02.07.07; J 90.01.02; J 90.02.07, 90.02.10, 90.02.16, 90.02.29; J 90.04.01\u201302; J 90.05.02; J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 192, 196, 205)"}}
{"key": "Q40support", "verdict": {"verdict": "CORRECTED", "problems": ["MISSED DECISIVE EVIDENCE (J 02.08.03, comtran-manuals/J28-6169/02-compiler.md line 1923): the draft's central inference in point (iii) \u2014 'a return left in IR4 would be destroyed by the called procedure's first data reference' \u2014 is STATED OUTRIGHT by J, not inferred. For a locate-mode field the compiler generates `LAC     BASE.LOCATOR.OF.DATE,4` / `TXL     SYS)294,4,0` / `CLA     DATE.DISP,4`, and J says: \"Note that the contents of index register 4 are destroyed, and that instructions have been inserted in the program.\" Verified on images/page-068.png. This also pins the generic tag N of SYS)294's diagnostic form (J 90.02.33) to 4 in the compiler's actual output, which removes the draft's own hedge (evidence item 12) \u2014 a hedge the draft carried in its evidence list but silently dropped from the annotation.", "COUNTING ERROR: '37 distinct TSX forms'. Exhaustive grep of the file yields 35 distinct TSX targets \u2014 GN)019, IOC)8, IOC)9, SYS)160, SYS)162\u2013182 (21), SYS)250, 251, 257, 258, 261, 262, 263, 264, 286, 292 \u2014 i.e. exactly the draft's own enumeration, which sums to 35, not 37 (36 if the second tag on SYS)263 is counted as a separate form). Corrected to 35.", "COUNTING ERROR: SYS)162's returns 'follow six in-line parameter words'. The calling sequence at J 90.02.12 has FIVE parameter words (`OP SYS)161`, and four `PZE` words), so the three returns sit at 6,4 / 7,4 / 8,4. Corrected to five.", "ARCHITECTURAL ERROR: `TSX     SYS)263, 6` is described as being 'tagged with a DIFFERENT index register (6)'. The 709/7090 has three index registers, designated by tag bits 1, 2 and 4; tags 3, 5, 6 and 7 designate combinations. A tag of 6 therefore names index registers 2 and 4 together \u2014 it is not a sixth register, and it still loads IR4, so it is not really an exception to the IR4 convention at all. This is external architecture knowledge of exactly the kind the draft elsewhere labels as inference, and it must be labelled. It also matters upstream: the 90.05-derived resolution immediately above cites `AXT *+3,7`, which on this machine loads all three index registers (IR4 among them), which is what makes the following `SXA P,4` coherent. Left uncorrected, the fragment would license reading tags 6 and 7 as registers 6 and 7 in compiler-design work.", "OVERSTATEMENT: 'Every calling sequence in the appendix is written `TSX name,4` ... with a single exception.' Not every invocation in the appendix is a TSX. MOVPAK's continuation words are in-line `TXI SYS)nnn,1,\u2026` and `TRA SYS)nnn` transfers (SYS)183\u2013247, 267\u2013282, and the `TRA SYS)219\u2013222/274` rounding entries), and \u2014 a genuine counterexample the draft missed \u2014 the panic exit is invoked by `TRA     SYS)266` or `TXI     SYS)266, 0, 0` (J 90.02.29, verified on images/page-168.png), tag 0, no return. The defensible claim is: every TSX is tagged 4 except one word.", "OVERSTATEMENT: 'the only source verbs it reaches are those implemented as library calls'. SET is reached and is shown compiled wholly in line, with no library call: \"When a CT operation such as SET causes a subscript to change value, the corresponding positional indicator is modified\", illustrated `CLA    CP)+1` / `ADD    PI)6` / `STO    PI)6` (J 90.02.05, verified on images/page-143.png). The claim that survives is the narrower and more relevant one: the appendix carries no code template for any CONTROL-FLOW verb.", "MISSED INTERNAL CORROBORATION: the draft labels the n,4 offset semantics as pure external 7090 inference ('no verbatim source'). The appendix demonstrates it on itself. SYS)182's text says \"The specific Move instructions are executed beginning at 1,4\" (J 90.02.15), and the worked XD-to-XD example puts those instructions immediately after the call \u2014 `TSX  SYS)182,4` / `TXI  SYS)183,1,1` / `TXI  SYS)191,1,10` / `TXI  SYS)223,1,10` (J 90.02.16, verified on images/page-155.png). So 1,4 is demonstrably the word following the TSX from within J itself; the architecture note is corroboration, not the sole basis.", "FRAMING OVERSTATEMENT: 'every part of it points to a stored return rather than a live register.' Parts (i) and (ii) attest the OPPOSITE for the library: an IR4-relative, live-register return with nothing saved anywhere. Only (iii)\u2013(iv) show that this convention cannot be carried into a procedure of arbitrary body. Reframed so the two halves are not conflated.", "CITATION SLIPS: (a) the `LAC    BL)2,4` / `CAL    DATANAME,4` block AND its explanatory sentence are both wholly inside J 90.02.04 (PDF 142); '90.02.04\u201305' over-reaches. (b) All three location-counter sentences are in J 90.02.01 (PDF 139); 90.02.02 (PDF 140) contains only the follow-on \"In other words, Location Counter 1 begins where Location Counter 0 ends.\" \u2014 so the range is defensible but the quotes should be cited to 90.02.01.", "INACCURATE VERIFICATION CLAIM (notes, not annotation): 'All matched the transcription character for character.' False for page-149. The Markdown reads `ACL    CP)+NN2     OCT 777772000000`, but the page image plainly shows `OCT 77777200C000`; the file's own conversion note documents this as a deliberate correction of a stray glyph. The annotation does not quote that line, so no annotation text changes, but the evidence item's quote should be trimmed to the two IR4 lines and the blanket verification claim withdrawn for that page.", "STATUS/DUPLICATION NOTE for the parent (not a defect in the reasoning): Open Question 40 already carries a Resolved annotation whose closing sentence is itself a compressed 90.02 corroboration ('all generated storage is fixed blocks with no stack or save-area chain, and IR4 (the TSX linkage register) is reused as data base register and scratch, so a surviving return must live in storage (J 90.02.03\u201306, 90.02.11, 90.02.29)'). The fragment expands exactly that sentence; if appended verbatim the parent should delete or shorten the existing one-liner rather than leave both. The draft's status of 'narrowed' describes 90.02's contribution only and must not be allowed to downgrade the question's Resolved status.", "Date kept at 2026-08-01 to match the sibling annotations of this mining pass (questions 27, 31, 41, 42, 47 all carry that date), as the draft recommended; the session clock is now 2026-08-02."], "corrected_annotation": "**Corroboration (90.02 mining, 2026-08-01; page-image verified):** The generated-code appendix never describes DO. An exhaustive search of J 90.02.00\u201390.02.33 finds no occurrence of the token at all, and no code template for any control-flow verb; the appendix's declared scope is \"This appendix explains the format and content of the instruction listing\" (J 90.02.00), and past the listing-format preamble it is a catalogue of the IOC)/SYS) runtime library. The source-level operations it reaches are the ones that library carries \u2014 OPEN (SYS)174\u2013175), CLOSE (SYS)176\u2013177), STOP (SYS)178, \"a message concerning a STOP verb\"), GET (IOC)8 with SYS)260/265/283/287), FILE (IOC)9 with SYS)291/296) and MOVE (MOVPAK) \u2014 plus the arithmetic helpers SYS)155\u2013173 and the comparison routine SYS)162. The one verb it shows compiled wholly in line is SET, and only in its subscript aspect: \"When a CT operation such as SET causes a subscript to change value, the corresponding positional indicator is modified\", illustrated `CLA    CP)+1` / `ADD    PI)6` / `STO    PI)6` (J 90.02.05). There is accordingly no SYS) or IOC) \"DO linkage\" routine of any kind: DO must be compiled wholly in-line, which is why only the object listing can settle its form. (The appendix's only generated-code use of the word \"procedure\" is the IOCS placeholder pair `PZE      END-OF-FILE-PROCEDURE,,ERROR-PROCEDURE` (J 90.02.04) \u2014 the AT END and ON ERROR exits, not DO.)\n\nWhat 90.02 does supply is the machine-level convention any such linkage had to obey.\n\n**(i) Calls go through index register 4, including calls to the compiler's own generated labels.** Every TSX in the appendix is tagged 4 \u2014 35 distinct targets (GN)019, IOC)8, IOC)9, SYS)160, 162\u2013182, 250\u2013251, 257\u2013258, 261\u2013264, 286, 292) \u2014 the sole exception being the alternate-return word `TSX     SYS)263, 6` (J 90.02.29). The same convention serves in-program code: \"During compilation it is necessary to give certain instructions names in order to refer to these instructions from a remote part of the program\", the one illustration being `TSX    GN)019,4` / `GN)019     CLA    PI)3` (J 90.02.06). Two caveats. Not every invocation is a TSX: MOVPAK's continuation words are in-line `TXI SYS)nnn,1,\u2026` and `TRA SYS)nnn` transfers within a sequence already anchored by a `TSX \u2026,4`, and the no-return panic exit is `TRA     SYS)266` or `TXI     SYS)266, 0, 0` (J 90.02.29). And *inference (709/7090 architecture; not stated in J):* the machine has three index registers, designated by tag bits 1, 2 and 4, so tags 3, 5, 6 and 7 designate combinations \u2014 a tag of 6 names index registers 2 and 4 together, and that lone \"exception\" therefore still loads IR4. (The same encoding is what makes the DO triple above coherent: `AXT *+3,7` loads all three index registers, IR4 among them, which is the one the following `SXA P,4` stores.)\n\n**(ii) Returns are expressed as small offsets counted off IR4, with nothing saved.** MOVPAK's four entry points are one routine differing only in how many in-line parameter words each consumes before resuming the caller's stream: for SYS)179, \"After these data pointers have been set, the instructions beginning at 3,4 are executed to actually perform the moving operation\"; for SYS)180 and SYS)181, \"the specific MOVE instructions beginning at 2,4 are executed\"; for SYS)182, \"The specific Move instructions are executed beginning at 1,4\" (J 90.02.14\u201315). The appendix demonstrates that arithmetic on itself: its worked XD-to-XD example places the \"specific Move instructions\" of a `TSX  SYS)182,4` immediately after the call \u2014 `TSX  SYS)182,4` / `TXI  SYS)183,1,1` / `TXI  SYS)191,1,10` / `TXI  SYS)223,1,10` (J 90.02.16) \u2014 so 1,4 is the word following the TSX. One routine states a return numerically: SYS)261's sequence is `TSX     SYS)261, 4` / `TSX     SYS)263, 6` / `(Normal Return)`, of which \"If an error is detected during conversion, return is 1,4 to routine SYS)263 which prints an error message\" (J 90.02.29). SYS)162 shows the same shape with three in-line returns after five parameter words \u2014 \"HIGH  RETURN from comparison\" / \"EQUAL RETURN from comparison\" / \"LOW   RETURN from comparison\" (J 90.02.12). *Inference (7090 architecture; not stated in J, but corroborated by the 90.02.16 example above):* TSX leaves the two's complement of the calling location in the tagged index register and 7090 indexing subtracts it, which is why n,4 is the n-th word after the TSX. Note what kind of convention this is: the return lives in IR4 for the duration of the call and is consumed as an offset \u2014 serviceable for a short library routine that leaves IR4 alone, and unusable for a procedure of arbitrary body.\n\n**(iii) IR4 cannot hold a live return across a procedure body \u2014 and J says so outright.** The base-locator idiom is `LAC    BL)2,4` / `CAL    DATANAME,4` \u2014 \"by first loading an index register with the 2's complement of the location of the first word (the base) of the data block and then referencing any data item within that block by using the relative word position or displacement from the beginning of the block\" (J 90.02.04) \u2014 and the in-line pointer-setup code emitted before a MOVE from a complex Base Locator uses IR4 as pure scratch, `PDX    0,4` / `TXL    *2,4,5` (J 90.02.11). Where 90.02 writes the base-locator tag generically as N (`LAC     BL)NN, N` / `TXL     SYS)294, N, 0`, J 90.02.33), J 02.08.03 gives the same sequence as the compiler actually emits it \u2014 tagged 4 \u2014 and states the consequence in its own words: for a locate-mode field the system generates `LAC     BASE.LOCATOR.OF.DATE,4` / `TXL     SYS)294,4,0` / `CLA     DATE.DISP,4`, and \"Note that the contents of index register 4 are destroyed, and that instructions have been inserted in the program\" (J 02.08.03). This is stated, not inferred: a DO'd procedure's first reference to a located data item destroys any return left in IR4, so a return that survives an arbitrary body must be planted in storage. The appendix exhibits precisely that planting shape \u2014 `AXT     0, 4` / `SXA     SYS)296, 4`, taking an address into IR4 and storing it into the address field of the named pointer word SYS)296 (J 90.02.33) \u2014 though there it plants a buffer address, not a return; *inference:* it is the same instruction pair a stored return linkage requires.\n\n**(iv) The object storage model has no stack.** Everything reserved is a fixed block: \"Unique result storage cells are reserved for each section of the program as they are needed\", allocated en bloc by `RS)    BSS    N` where \"N is the sum of maximum Result Storage used in each section\" (J 90.02.03); `TS)    BSS    N` for Temporary Storage, which unlike Result Storage is \"not section-qualified\" (J 90.02.04); `PI)    BSS    N` for Positional Indicators (J 90.02.05); constants \"pooled together in a block of storage immediately following the Positional Indicators\" (J 90.02.06) \u2014 all laid down under three static location counters, 0 for \"in-line instructions\" and fixed data, 1 for \"out-of-line subroutines and working storage areas\", 2 for pointer-word initialization (J 90.02.01). No save area, save-area chain, activation record or dynamic allocation appears anywhere in the appendix. *Inference:* each section's working cells are shared by every activation of that section, so the generated object program is non-reentrant by construction \u2014 precisely the environment in which a per-procedure return cell is the natural mechanism and re-entering an already-active procedure destroys its pending return. (J 90.02.00\u201306, 90.02.11\u201316, 90.02.29, 90.02.33; J 02.08.03; images/page-142.png, page-143.png, page-144.png, page-149.png, page-153.png, page-154.png, page-155.png, page-168.png, page-172.png, page-068.png)\n\n---\n\n*Variant for the \u00a78.5.5 catalog entry \"Nested and recursive DO\":* **Corroboration (90.02 mining, 2026-08-01):** the generated-code appendix supports the non-reentrancy premise independently of the object listing. It contains no stack, save area or activation record of any kind \u2014 all generated storage is fixed blocks (`RS)    BSS    N`, with \"N is the sum of maximum Result Storage used in each section\"; `TS)    BSS    N`, \"not section-qualified\"; `PI)    BSS    N`; and a Constant Pool block) laid down under three static location counters (J 90.02.01\u201306), so a section's working cells are shared by every activation of that section. And index register 4, in which a `TSX name,4` call leaves the return \u2014 the convention used even for the compiler's own generated labels, `TSX    GN)019,4` / `GN)019     CLA    PI)3` (J 90.02.06) \u2014 is simultaneously the data-addressing register of generated code (`LAC    BL)2,4` / `CAL    DATANAME,4`, J 90.02.04) and in-line scratch (`PDX    0,4` / `TXL    *2,4,5`, J 90.02.11); J states the consequence explicitly of the code it generates for a locate-mode reference, \"Note that the contents of index register 4 are destroyed\" (J 02.08.03). No return can therefore survive a procedure body in a register. 90.02 itself never mentions DO \u2014 it documents no control-flow code templates at all, only the IOC)/SYS) runtime library \u2014 so it constrains the mechanism without attesting the DO sequence itself. (J 90.02.01\u201306, 90.02.11; J 02.08.03)"}}
{"key": "Q42", "verdict": {"verdict": "CORRECTED", "problems": ["FACTUAL ERROR (annotation body, material): the draft says the five alphameric relations each compile to \"an inline `CAL`/`LAS` pair followed by a three-transfer decode\" and describes both cited sites as having \"three `TRA`s\". Two of the five have only TWO transfers. At COMPARE.EMPLOYEE.NUMBERS the first WHEN clause is `00246 LAS 1)EMPLOYEE.NUMBER,2` / `00247 TRA *+2` / `00250 TRA CHECK.NEW.DEPT`, and the third LAS skip slot (00251) falls straight through into the next WHEN clause's `CAL 2)EMPLOYEE.NUMBER,1` (90.05-sample-program.md lines 844-847, PDF p. 201; confirmed on images/page-201.png). At CHECK.NEW.DEPT the decode is `00537 LAS RS)0` / `00540 TRA *+2` / `00541 TRA GN)070`, the third slot (00542) being `AXT *+3,7`, the head of the DO of DEPARTMENT.END (lines 1076-1079, PDF p. 205). Only the second WHEN clause (00252) and the two HIGH.VALUE tests (00335, 00354) have three TRAs. The claim as written is refuted by the very lines the draft cites.", "LOGICAL NON-SEQUITUR (annotation body, material): the HIGH.VALUE argument does not support the conclusion drawn from it. The draft writes \"the fold is length-driven, not value-driven: `IF D.EMP.NO = HIGH.VALUE` compares against a wholly compile-time-known operand ... and is still compiled as a full run-time comparison ... J describes no general constant folding\". Only ONE operand (HIGH.VALUE) is known at compile time; D.EMP.NO is a run-time value, so the comparison's outcome is not compile-time determinable and NO constant-folding optimizer of any kind could fold it. The evidence therefore proves nothing about the presence or absence of general constant folding. What the code does legitimately show is that a figurative constant is materialised at exactly the length of the field it is compared with (CP)+23 = OCT 747474747474, six characters, against a 6-character field), so it never presents an unequal-length case \u2014 which bears on the draft's own open item about differently-lengthed literal operands. Reframed in the corrected annotation. (The conclusion \"length-driven\" is anyway stated outright by the manual: rule 2 is titled \"Comparisons of fields of unequal length\".)", "CITATION SLIP (evidence item 11): the page list \"PDF pp. 201, 202, 203, 205\" is paired with line range 1057-1083, but line 1057 (`00527 CHECK.NEW.DEPT CAL CURRENT.DEPT`) sits on PDF p. 204 \u2014 the `<!-- 90.05 listing | PDF 205 -->` marker is at line 1060. The page list should read 201-203, 204-205. The annotation body gets this right (\"PDF pp. 204-205\"), so the evidence list and annotation disagree.", "IMPRECISION (annotation body, minor): \"differing only in the masking needed for a 2-character field\" understates the difference. CHECK.NEW.DEPT additionally emits `LGL 18` and `SLW RS)0` (a scratch-word store) because CURRENT.DEPT is not word-aligned: the full sequence is `CAL CURRENT.DEPT` / `LGL 18` / `ANA CP)+30` / `SLW RS)0` / `LAC BL)3,1` / `TXL SYS)294,1,0` / `CAL 2)DEPARTMENT,1` / `ANA CP)+30` / `LAS RS)0` (lines 1057, 1069-1076).", "OVERSTATED GREP CLAIM (evidence item 4, minor): \"'GO TO' occurs exactly twice in the whole file\" is a `grep -c` LINE count. Both matching lines contain two occurrences each, so the string occurs four times on two lines. The annotation's own wording (\"appears only inside those two examples\") is accurate; the evidence claim's arithmetic is not.", "UNLABELLED INFERENCE (evidence item 3, minor): the equation of \"non-format field\" (J 02.04.07 rule 4) with \"a field for which no pictorial is given\" (J 02.05.06 c) is an inference, not a statement. J 02.05 never defines the term \"non-format field\"; 02.05.05's chart defines field types by what appears \"in format field\", and 02.05.06 e) uses \"non-format character\" in a different sense (\"Any non-format character appearing in the pictorial will result in the pictorial being interpreted as a name\"). The reading is almost certainly right and matches the definition's existing \u00a75.3.2 note 4, but it must be labelled inference since the whole equal-length finding for D.EMP.NO/M.EMP.NO rests on it.", "SECTION-CODE ERROR (if the SYS)130 detail is carried into the annotation): SYS)130 (\"detects the truncation of significant high order values\") is at 90.02-generated-code.md line 412, which falls under the marker `<!-- 90.02.10 | PDF 148 -->` (line 381), i.e. J 90.02.10 \u2014 not 90.02.09. The draft's evidence item 5 mentions SYS)130 without a section code; the corrected annotation cites 90.02.10.", "WHITESPACE (evidence item 11, trivial, not an error of substance): the continuation line \"LAC   BL)3,1\" is quoted with 52 leading spaces; the file has 56 (line 839). The rest of the block is a uniform de-indent of the file's 8-space code-block margin, so this one line is inconsistent with the others.", "VERIFIED CORRECT (no action): the J 02.04.07 transcription matches images/page-025.png character for character, including \"In tests for    NOT    greater or less conditions\" (the scan's wide inter-word spacing around NOT is not reproducible in Markdown and is already catalogued). All 90.02 negative greps reproduce exactly (fold/optimiz/eliminat/suppress/\"only code\"/\"no code\"/conditional/relation/branch/false/unequal = 0; truncat = 1, SYS)130; \"compile time\" = 1, SYS)242). Msgs 107,00 / 123,00 / 170,00 verified at 90.04-error-messages.md lines 171, 192, 248. F p. 48 verified at 03-procedure-description.md lines 857-862; F p. 25 conditional clause/sentence and F p. 24 TR verified at 02-language-structure.md. The five LAS census is exactly right (lines 844, 848, 910, 938, 1076) and maps one-to-one onto the five alphameric relations. The SYS) reference list (132, 133, 175, 177, 178, 180, 182, 184, 185, 190, 193, 198, 212, 214, 225, 226, 239-241, 243-245, 260, 267-269, 275, 283, 294) reproduces exactly, and SYS)162 appears nowhere in 90.05. D.EMP.NO/M.EMP.NO are confirmed equal-length via the CALL synonyms at statement 187,00 (lines 463-467) plus AA+AAAA in both MASTER (lines 226-229) and DETAIL (lines 250-252). The draft's correction of the question stem (D is the branch the constant outcome SELECTS, not the false branch) is correct and confirmed on the scan. No decisive contrary evidence exists in 90.01, 90.02, or 90.04.", "STATUS (upheld): \"narrowed\" is the right status. Nothing is claimed resolved that is inference, and the two genuine inferences (WHEN folds identically; non-format = pictorial-less) are or are now explicitly labelled."], "corrected_annotation": "**Narrowed (90.02 mining, 2026-08-01; verified against page scans):** The rule is written operator-scoped, not construct-scoped. J 02.04.07 rule 2.b reads \"Alphameric fields of unequal length are treated on the basis of the operator used in the comparison\", and sub-rule i \"In tests for an = or NOT = condition the fields will always be found to be unequal. In the following example where fields A and B are of alphameric type with unequal length the only code generated will be a transfer to D.\" (verified character-for-character against images/page-025.png). The subsection is headed \"C. Conditional Statements\" (J 02.04.06) and its six rules speak throughout of \"comparisons\" and \"tests\"; no rule names a construct as its scope, and constructs appear only inside worked examples \u2014 rule 1's \"the following IF statement represents an invalid comparison\" and rule 2.b.i's pair \"IF A = B THEN GO TO C OTHERWISE GO TO D.\" / \"IF A NOT = B THEN GO TO D OTHERWISE GO TO C.\" The semantic half of the rule (\"the fields will always be found to be unequal\") therefore binds every context that evaluates such a condition \u2014 conditional sentences (F p. 25), the conditional GO TO's WHEN clauses (F p. 48) and TR operands (F p. 24) alike \u2014 the more so because J's language chapter documents no conditional construct's syntax at all: across J 02.00\u201302.08 the string \"GO TO\" appears only inside those two examples, and WHEN only in the key-word list (J 02.03.02) and in the Data Description clause BLANK WHEN ZERO (J 02.05.05, 02.05.07), J 02.04 being by its own title \"Procedure Description Clarification and Amplification\" of the F language. Only the code-shape half is exhibited, and only on IF sentences. Note that \"a transfer to D\" names the branch the constant outcome *selects*, not specifically the false branch: in the first example D is the OTHERWISE target of an always-false `=`, in the second it is the THEN target of an always-true `NOT =`, so for IF both directions of the fold are attested. Neither appendix that could exhibit a fold does so. J 90.02 has nothing on the subject \u2014 it \"explains the format and content of the instruction listing\" (J 90.02.00) and then catalogues listing conventions and the IOC)/SYS) runtime library, with no per-construct code templates; an exhaustive search of it finds no occurrence of fold, optimiz-, eliminat-, suppress-, \"only code\", \"no code\", conditional, relation, branch, false or unequal, its one \"truncation\" being SYS)130's overflow flag (J 90.02.10), and its two nearest items are irrelevant to the point: SYS)162, a *run-time* routine that \"performs an alphabetic comparison on two fields\" whose calling sequence carries independent LENGTH(1)/LENGTH(2) operands (J 90.02.12), and the MOVPAK routine SYS)242, which \"moves alphabetic fields to alphabetic fields where some information was unknown at compile time\" (J 90.02.25) \u2014 the appendix's sole occurrence of the phrase \"compile time\". The sample program cannot settle it either, because it contains no unequal-length alphameric `=`/`NOT =` comparison: its entire inventory of alphameric relations is the WHEN pair `GO TO CHECK.NEW.DEPT WHEN D.EMP.NO = M.EMP.NO, LOW.DETAIL WHEN D.EMP.NO LT M.EMP.NO.` \u2014 both operands pictorial-less groups of `AA` + `AAAA`, hence 6-character alphameric fields of equal length by J 02.05.06 c) with rule 4 of J 02.04.07 (*inference*: J nowhere defines the term \"non-format field\", and 02.05.06 c) is the only candidate definition) \u2014 the two `= HIGH.VALUE` sentinel tests, and `CURRENT.DEPT IS NOT EQUAL TO D.DEPT` (both `AA`); every other relation in the program is numeric (J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 192\u2013193, 195\u2013197). All five of those alphameric relations produced object code: the listing contains exactly five `LAS` three-way logical compares, one per relation, and SYS)162 is never called anywhere in the program (J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 201\u2013203, 204\u2013205). The shape is an inline load-and-compare followed by a transfer decode of LAS's three skip slots \u2014 three `TRA`s where all three outcomes branch away (`LAS 1)EMPLOYEE.NUMBER,2` / `TRA *+3` / `TRA *+2` / `TRA LOW.DETAIL`, PDF p. 201), two where one outcome falls through into the following code (`LAS 1)EMPLOYEE.NUMBER,2` / `TRA *+2` / `TRA CHECK.NEW.DEPT`, the third slot falling into the next WHEN clause's `CAL`, PDF p. 201; `LAS RS)0` / `TRA *+2` / `TRA GN)070`, the third slot falling into the `AXT`/`SXA`/`TRA` triple that performs DO DEPARTMENT.END, PDF p. 205). Two things follow. First, IF and WHEN demonstrably share one comparison code generator \u2014 the WHEN pair at COMPARE.EMPLOYEE.NUMBERS (`CAL 2)EMPLOYEE.NUMBER,1` \u2026 `LAS 1)EMPLOYEE.NUMBER,2` plus decode, PDF p. 201) and the IF at CHECK.NEW.DEPT (`CAL CURRENT.DEPT` / `LGL 18` / `ANA CP)+30` / `SLW RS)0` \u2026 `CAL 2)DEPARTMENT,1` / `ANA CP)+30` / `LAS RS)0` plus decode, PDF pp. 204\u2013205) emit the same skeleton, differing only in the shift, mask and scratch-word store needed for a 2-character field held mid-word \u2014 which, together with msg 170,00 \"-WHEN- SUBSTITUTED FOR -IF- BECAUSE OF IMPROPER USE.\" showing a shared front-end path (J 90.04.01), makes it likely \u2014 *inference*, nowhere stated \u2014 that the fold applies to WHEN clauses identically. Second, a figurative constant does not create an unequal-length case: `IF D.EMP.NO = HIGH.VALUE` compiles to a full run-time compare against `CP)+23`, generated as `OCT 747474747474` \u2014 six left-parenthesis characters, HIGH.VALUE in the 709/7090 collating sequence (J 02.04.01) \u2014 materialised at exactly the length of the field it is compared with (J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 202, 216). (That observation says nothing about constant folding in general: only one operand of such a test is known at compile time, so its outcome is not.) The fold rests solely on the statically known field lengths, there being no blank padding of the shorter field. **Still open:** the emitted form of a folded WHEN clause \u2014 presumably no code for an always-false `=` clause and an unconditional transfer for an always-true `NOT =` one, which would strand every later clause of the same conditional GO TO, since \"Any remaining conditional expressions are left unevaluated\" (F p. 48) \u2014 and whether folding is diagnosed at all, which J's message list suggests it is not, carrying only \"ILLEGAL COMPARISON STRUCTURE.\" (msg 107,00) and \"CANNOT USE VARIABLE LENGTH ITEMS FOR COMPARISON.\" (msg 123,00) (J 90.04.01)."}}
{"key": "Q41Q47", "verdict": {"verdict": "CORRECTED", "problems": ["VERBATIM ERROR (in both evidence and annotation): the draft quotes `00350      0020 60 0 00331   10001    GN)067          TRA*   END.OF.MASTERS` with three spaces after TRA*. (The draft's other inferences \u2014 the out-of-line-block reading, the convergence argument \u2014 are correctly tagged.)", "VERIFIED, NO CHANGE: all other quoted lines match character for character (30 listing/message lines checked by exact grep -F). Section markers all point where claimed: SYS)174 in 90.02.13 (PDF 152), SYS)175-178 in 90.02.14 (PDF 153), IOC)1/IOC)6/IOC)7 in 90.02.08, generic GET sequence in 90.02.04, SYS)260 in 90.02.28, SYS)265 in 90.02.29, SYS)283 in 90.02.32 (with the printed typo \"in not used\" and the conversion note at line 1910 both as described), reserved-word table under 02.03.02, OPEN/CLOSE deferral at 02.07.01 line 1441, AT END rule at 02.07.05 line 1562, its continuation at 02.07.06 line 1567, STOP RUN at 02.04.06 line 524, msgs 138/139 at lines 216-217 (PDF 184), F OPEN forms at p. 39, F \"..., AT END any imperative clause\" at p. 40 line 289 and p. 109 line 180. 90.01 contains no OPEN entry. Scan checks I ran independently: images/page-153.png (SYS)175 \"opens all files\", SYS)176, SYS)177 under header 90.02.14), page-195.png (`188,00   71466 START.  OPEN ALL FILES,` and the three AT END statements; no ONERROR on any FILE card), page-200.png (00165 START TSX SYS)175,4 / 00166 PZE IOC)1), page-201.png (GN)058 block, IOCTN*, GN)062 TRA END.OF.DETAILS) \u2014 all confirm the markdown.", "STATUS CHALLENGE SURVIVED: Q47 \"resolved\" is warranted \u2014 it rests on direct attestation (source statement + clean compilation + generated SYS)175 call + documented routine), not inference. Q41 \"narrowed\" is correctly conservative; the draft does not overclaim the parse. OP is a CVR or NOP depending of the need to adjust the collating sequence before the comparison\" (J 90.02.12; \"depending of\" is the original's typo, and the dropped \"y\" in the same paragraph's \"the field is located b the 'pointer' word\" is a defect of the printed page \u2014 both confirmed on images/page-151.png, which is also the page the 90.02 conversion note misdates as PDF p.153). *Inference from code shape, not a stated rule:* four properties of that mechanism make COLLATE COM look comparison-only at object time. (i) The collate switch is a word of the *call site*, not a mode cell the routine interrogates, so it is fixed per comparison at compile time \u2014 precisely what per-section scoping requires (\"limit the modal specification to a particular section \u2026 the mode of generation reverts to the normal mode at the end of the specified section\", J 02.06.17) \u2014 and no communication cell listed in the appendix holds a collating mode (J 90.02.08\u201311, which run to monitor cells, date/time, move source/target pointers and overflow flags). (ii) The adjustment is made \"before the comparison\" inside a routine that has no target operand and only three exits, HIGH/EQUAL/LOW, so it can only re-rank operand characters transiently; stored records, buffers and files keep their 709 representation. (iii) Nothing else in the appendix mentions collation, translation or the 705 sequence: the MOVPAK alphabetic movers SYS)239\u2013242 copy or insert characters with no translate step, and every figurative-constant fill takes its character from the generated code \u2014 blanks and zeros have dedicated routines (\"This MOVPAK subroutine moves blanks to an alphabetic field\", SYS)243; \"\u2026moves zeros to an alphabetic field\", SYS)244), while HV compiles, as the sample shows, into SYS)245, \"This MOVPAK subroutine moves characters to an alphabetic field. The second word contains 6 characters of the type to be moved\" (J 90.02.25\u201326); J classes HV and LV as move source types alongside BL and ZE (J 90.02.10), but which routine carries LV is unrecorded \u2014 natively `0` and under COM blank are exactly the values SYS)244 and SYS)243 already provide, and the point is unaffected either way, since all three fills are given their character by the caller. (iv) SYS)161 is a Type 2 item, one of the \"Subroutines contained on the system tape which are placed in core by the CT Loader as required by the particular object program being loaded\" (J 90.02.07), so a program with no COM comparison never loads the table; and COLLATE leaves no trace outside generated instructions \u2014 the appendix on \"Compiler Use of Environment Description Options in Generation of Loader Symbolic Control Cards\" fills those cards \"on the basis of options exercised on Environment FILE and SPECIF cards\" only (J 90.08.00\u201302), the object-deck format defines no collating flag (J 90.03), the loader has no collate control card (J 03), COLLATE appears nowhere among the deferred features or restrictions (J 90.01), and the sole -OPTION- card diagnostic in the 210-message list is \"-OPTION- CARD FORMAT ERROR.\" (J 90.04.01 msg 3). The blast radius is further bounded by the comparison rules themselves \u2014 \"Numeric fields are compared arithmetically without regard to length\" and edited fields \"are converted to pure numeric fields\" for comparison, so neither can be affected, while \"All non-format fields \u2026 are compared alphamerically\" and therefore do fall under the mode (J 02.04.07) \u2014 and by the fact that neither manual defines a SORT verb or any other ordering operation a collating sequence could govern (the single occurrence of the word, \"In sorting problems, a programmer may use many tapes\", J 06.05.02, is about tape-channel assignment for sort *applications*). **Still open:** (a) what a COM comparison physically looks like \u2014 no compilation with an OPTION card survives (the sample's Environment Description carries only FILE and SPECIF cards, J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 195), and under the default mode its alphameric comparisons, including the two `= HIGH.VALUE` sentinel tests, compile as an inline `CAL`/`LAS` three-way compare followed by a TRA decode, with CVR, SYS)161 and SYS)162 absent from the entire object listing (J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 198\u2013216; compares at pp. 201\u2013203), so whether COM forces such compares through SYS)162 or instead emits inline CVRs is unrecorded; (b) the cross-mode question, since J 02.04.01 words the HIGH.VALUE/LOW.VALUE rule program-wide (\"specified in the Environment Description\") while J 02.06.17 lets COLLATE be scoped to one section \u2014 a sentinel planted as `9` inside a COM section and later compared under the native sequence is undefined (see open question 60); (c) strictly, the evidence bounds the *object-time* footprint and cannot exclude a compile-time-only effect that leaves no runtime trace, which is what J 02.06.02's broader wording would have to mean if it means anything more than 02.06.16 does. Recommended implementation: treat COLLATE COM as affecting exactly (1) the ordering used by alphameric and non-format comparisons and (2) the character value of HIGH.VALUE/LOW.VALUE wherever those constants are compiled, with no effect on stored representation, I/O, moves of other data, or arithmetic. (J 02.04.01; J 02.04.02; J 02.04.07; J 02.06.02; J 02.06.16\u201317; J 90.01; J 90.02.07, 90.02.08\u201311, 90.02.10, 90.02.12, 90.02.25\u201326; J 90.03; J 90.04.01 msg 3; J 90.08.00\u201302; J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 195\u2013196, 198\u2013216, 201\u2013203, 216; images/page-151.png)"}}
