{"iocs": {"extract": {"question_id": "Q46+Q50", "confidence": "certain", "answer_summary": "Both questions are answered by C28-6100-2. **Q46:** the FOR LABEL exit is, underneath, IOCS's \"non-standard label image package\" \u2014 not one entry point but a FIVE-word transfer vector at MYLBLS..MYLBLS+4, entered by `TSX MYLBLS+k, 1`, whose *address* is supplied in the decrement of the file's entry in the ATTACH file list (and held in FCB word 3 bits 3-17). The event is discriminated purely POSITIONALLY (which of the five cells IOCS calls): 0 = check input header, +1 = check input trailer, +2 = check label on a reel to be used for output, +3 = prepare output trailer, +4 = prepare output header. Parameters are passed in storage (the 14-word LAREA at IOCS+43..IOCS+56) plus index register 2 = 2's complement of the FCB address; all index registers used must be saved and restored; there are no parameter words. Return is by skip-return through IR1 \u2014 `TRA 1,1` / `TRA 2,1` / `TRA 3,1` \u2014 and two entries additionally return a decision (entry +1 sets the MQ sign to choose reel-switch vs end-of-file; entry +3 loads the AC with `1EORbb` or `1EOFbb`). The routine may only inspect/fill the label image; IOCS still does all tape I/O, and \"All the system actions and messages will apply.\" This fully explains J's remark that \"portions of the FOR LABEL coding must be done in CRYPT\" \u2014 no COMTRAN statement can execute `TRA 2,1` or set the MQ sign, and IOCS's IR1/IR2 conventions collide with COMTRAN's use of IR1 as a record base register. What remains open is only the COMTRAN-side glue: `FOR LABEL statement.name` names ONE procedure while IOCS wants five entry points, and nothing in J says how the compiler bridges that. **Q50:** answer is NO \u2014 no count check of any kind exists outside standard labels. Unlabeled files have no trailer and hence no count (\"the user must have a recognizable data record to indicate that the end of the file has been reached\"); the sole labeling-independent integrity check is the optional per-block sequence word + folded check sum, and that is available *for binary files only* \u2014 a restriction COMTRAN's SPECIF SEQ/CKSUMS write-up never states. For LABELN the block-count comparison falls to the user's MYLBLS+1 routine (*inference*: IOCS cannot locate a count in a user-defined image; the manual scopes the check to trailers \"written by the system\"). Additionally, F p. 41 cannot be mapped onto IOCS as written: wrong unit (IOCS counts BLOCKS, never records), wrong time (the check fires inside READ when a trailer is read, never at CLOSE \u2014 CLOSE's label work is output-side only), wrong reporting (a sequence-error ERR exit with MQ prefix 1 and NO printed message, not \"notification \u2026 through external display\"), and wrong scope (every input reel, not only end of file). What survives of F is its conditional \"if label records are present\" and the structural fact that the 1EOF trailer written at close does carry a count in word 2. **Errata for the existing Q45 annotation:** it describes the four-item list on printed p. 24 as \"the list of what happens when standard label images are assumed\"; the manual actually says \"If the decrement is zero, standard images are assumed; if it is non-zero, the following procedures will be executed:\" \u2014 the list is the non-standard-package case. The 14-word figure it cites is unaffected. Also settles open question 53 (nothing substitutes for the end-of-reel label check on an unlabeled MULTI input file \u2014 a sentinel data record is the programmer's responsibility).", "facts": [{"claim": "The non-standard label routine's address is supplied in the ATTACH file-list entry's decrement; a non-zero decrement puts IOCS into the user-label mode, and the four consequences follow", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 32 / printed p. 24 (verified on a 300-dpi crop)", "verbatim_quote": "For this purpose, IOCS allows a non-standard label image package to be specified for each file. The location of the first cell of this package can be specified in the decrement of each entry in the list of files used for the ATTACH routine. If the decrement is zero, standard images are assumed; if it is non-zero, the following procedures will be executed: 1. All labels, both headers and trailers, will be read and written by the system. 2. All EOF marks associated with labels will also be read and written by the system. 3. The standard label area at symbolic locations IOCS + 43 (see page 39) will be used; its length is 14 words. 4. All the system actions and messages will apply."}, {"claim": "Register and save/restore conventions for every entry into the label package", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 33 / printed p. 25 (verified on a 300-dpi crop)", "verbatim_quote": "5. At each TSX, index register 2 will contain the 2's complement of the location of the first word of the File Control Block for the file being processed. 6. The contents of all index registers used must be saved and restored."}, {"claim": "The complete five-entry calling sequence, printed verbatim \u2014 this is the calling convention Q46 asks for", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 33 / printed p. 25 (verified on a 300-dpi crop)", "verbatim_quote": "Assuming that the non-standard label package begins at symbolic location MYLBLS, then the following actions will occur as indicated: 1. TSX MYLBLS, 1  To check header label input file \u2014 The routine must return by means of: TRA 1, 1 if label is correct / TRA 2, 1 if the input label is invalid. Bit 35 of the 7th word of the label area must be 1 upon either return if a checkpoint record is to be skipped. 2. TSX MYLBLS + 1, 1  To check trailer label, input file \u2014 Return must always be: TRA 1, 1. Upon return, the sign of the MQ must be: + if EOR; tape switching will occur / \u2212 if EOF; an EOF exit will be taken. 3. TSX MYLBLS + 2, 1  To check label on tapes to be used for output \u2014 Return must be: TRA 1, 1 if the reel may be used / TRA 2, 1 if (creation date + retention days) has not been reached / TRA 3, 1 if it is not possible to use the reel. 4. TSX MYLBLS + 3, 1  To prepare output trailer label \u2014 Return must always be: TRA 1, 1. Upon return, bits P, 1-35 of the AC must contain: 1EORbb if an EOR trailer is to be prepared / 1EOFbb if an EOF trailer is to be prepared. 5. TSX MYLBLS + 4, 1  To prepare output header label \u2014 Return is: TRA 1, 1"}, {"claim": "The package address is held in the File Control Block, word 3 bits 3-17", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 75 / printed p. 67 (detail); PDF p. 73 / printed p. 65 (diagram)", "verbatim_quote": "3 | 3-17 | Location of entry point for standard or non-standard label image routines."}, {"claim": "Only a limited kind of non-standard labeling is reachable through the package; anything else requires replacing IOCS's labeling routines outright", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 32 / printed p. 24", "verbatim_quote": "Non-standard labels may be either a mere arrangement of the standard format or an entirely different labeling procedure. If non-standard labeling procedures are desired, the IOCS labeling routines must be entirely replaced. However, if only changes in label formats are desired, the IOCS label handling routines will still apply."}, {"claim": "The 14-word label area passed to the routine is LAREA, the same area COMTRAN's LABEL type code redefines", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 47 / printed p. 39", "verbatim_quote": "LAREA | IOCS + 43 to IOCS + 56 | Label area (14 words)"}, {"claim": "The Preprocessor's *FILE control card has no field for a label package \u2014 only a labeling-conventions column \u2014 so the package can be established only by the program's own ATTACH list", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 52 / printed p. 44", "verbatim_quote": "32 | Labeling conventions: H\u2014High density label / L\u2014Low density label / S\u2014Standard density convention / blank\u2014No label"}, {"claim": "Standard-label timing that anchors the mapping of the five entries onto COMTRAN's open/close/reel-switch events", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 31 / printed p. 23", "verbatim_quote": "Note: During writing, an EOR label is prepared by IOCS when the end of a reel is encountered. An EOF label is prepared when the file is closed."}, {"claim": "FOR LABEL is meaningless for card files: IOCS provides no labeling on on-line card equipment", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 29 / printed p. 21", "verbatim_quote": "Labeling is not available for files processed on any on-line card equipment."}, {"claim": "The standard trailer's block count is checked for every input reel, and a mismatch gives a sequence error independent of per-block sequence checking", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 31 / printed p. 23", "verbatim_quote": "BLOCK COUNT is the number of data blocks on this reel. This is checked for every input reel. If it differs from the number of blocks actually read in, a sequence error is given. In this case it should be noted that a sequence error for the reel can occur even though each block is not being checked for sequence."}, {"claim": "Restatement scoping the block-count check to trailers written by the system, and identifying the disposition as a sequence error exit", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 32 / printed p. 24 (verified on a 300-dpi crop)", "verbatim_quote": "The Block Count which appears in every trailer written by the system is checked and a sequence error exit is taken if it does not agree with the number of blocks \"read.\""}, {"claim": "A block-count mismatch is delivered as the READ routine's ERR exit, may be ignored, and carries MQ prefix 1 \u2014 there is no printed message for it anywhere in the IOCS message list", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 24 / printed p. 16; corroborated PDF p. 79 / printed p. 71 (\"READ | Block sequence error | ERROR exit MQ(S-2) = 1\") and PDF p. 25 / printed p. 17 (\"1\u2014if a sequence error occurred\")", "verbatim_quote": "ERR is the location to which transfer is made when any of three types of error conditions occur: (a) a redundancy which cannot be corrected; (b) check sum error (binary file); and (c) sequence error. The condition is recognized at the first reference to a buffer in which it occurs. The error encountered may be ignored by continuing to \"read\" the file."}, {"claim": "CLOSE does no input-side count comparison; its label work is writing output trailers only", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 27 / printed p. 19; glossary PDF p. 83 / printed p. 75", "verbatim_quote": "For a regular output file, a close other than the MON type, causes writing of (1) an EOF mark, a trailer label, and another EOF mark, if the file is labeled, or (2) an EOF mark only, if the file is unlabeled."}, {"claim": "The only integrity check independent of labeling is the block sequence word plus folded check sum \u2014 and it exists for binary files only", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 41 / printed p. 33 (verified on a 300-dpi crop); restriction restated on the FILE card, PDF p. 52 / printed p. 44 (\"Block sequence numbering flag (significant for binary files only)\"; \"Check sum flag (available for binary sequence numbered files only)\")", "verbatim_quote": "Every File Control Block carries a block sequence number for the current reel of the file. On a labeled file, this sequence number is always written in a trailer label at the end of each reel and checked when it is read. In addition, for a binary file, a block sequence word can be appended to the end of each block, regardless of whether the file is labeled or unlabeled."}, {"claim": "For an unlabeled multi-reel input file nothing whatever substitutes for the trailer check \u2014 end of data is the programmer's responsibility (this also settles open question 53)", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 34 / printed p. 26 (verified on a 300-dpi crop)", "verbatim_quote": "An EOF exit is given once per reel for each EOF encountered and if the file is again referenced by a READ operation, reel switching will occur and buffering will be resumed. For this type of file, the user must have a recognizable data record to indicate that the end of the file has been reached."}, {"claim": "For an unlabeled single-reel file the meaning of an EOF mark is likewise the programmer's problem", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 34 / printed p. 26", "verbatim_quote": "It is the programmer's responsibility, in this case, to determine which EOF mark signifies the actual end of the file. No reel switching is possible for input files of this type."}, {"claim": "IOCS's own vocabulary makes \"record\" mean a physical record, i.e. a block \u2014 the basis for judging whether F's record count can denote anything in IOCS", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 83 / printed p. 75 (BLOCK, BLOCK COUNT); PDF p. 85 / printed p. 77 (LOGICAL RECORD)", "verbatim_quote": "BLOCK \u2014 A physical record; that is, a tape record, a card, or a line of print. \u2026 BLOCK COUNT \u2014 The number of blocks processed on the current reel of a file, kept in the corresponding File Control Block. \u2026 LOGICAL RECORD \u2014 Data which, as a group, has some logical significance to the user."}, {"claim": "The two other places IOCS uses \"record\" as a count are tape-positioning data and an operator message \u2014 neither is a label field and neither is compared against anything", "citation": "external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 49 / printed p. 41; PDF p. 67 / printed p. 59", "verbatim_quote": "If the Unit Control Block is for a tape unit, it also contains counts which specify the current position of the tape (file count, and record count within the file). \u2026 UNIT XXXXX (file name) REEL XXXX \u2013 XXXXX RECORDS"}, {"claim": "F's claim being tested \u2014 the CLOSE-time record-count comparison on an input file, and the record count written into the output end-of-file label", "citation": "F p. 41", "verbatim_quote": "The record count is compared with the count in the end-of-file label if label records are present and if end of file has been reached. If the count does not agree, notification is given through external display. If the tape is not at end of file the record count is ignored."}, {"claim": "F initiates the record count at OPEN for both input and output files, and maintains it at FILE \u2014 so the count is an F-level object with no IOCS counterpart", "citation": "F p. 39 (OPEN); F p. 41 (FILE)", "verbatim_quote": "3. Checking is performed, and a record count is initiated. \u2026 3. A count of the number of records written is maintained."}], "proposed_annotation": "=== Q46 ===\n\n**Resolved (external source, 2026-08-02):** the IOCS manual prints the linkage in full, and it is not a single exit but a **five-entry transfer vector** \u2014 which is precisely why a single COMTRAN `FOR LABEL statement.name` cannot express it and why \"portions of the FOR LABEL coding must be done in CRYPT\" (J 02.05.03).\n\n*Where the routine's address lives.* \"IOCS allows a non-standard label image package to be specified for each file. The location of the first cell of this package can be specified in the decrement of each entry in the list of files used for the ATTACH routine. If the decrement is zero, standard images are assumed; if it is non-zero, the following procedures will be executed: 1. All labels, both headers and trailers, will be read and written by the system. 2. All EOF marks associated with labels will also be read and written by the system. 3. The standard label area at symbolic locations IOCS + 43 (see page 39) will be used; its length is 14 words. 4. All the system actions and messages will apply.\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 32 / printed p. 24; verified on a 300-dpi crop). The address is then held in the File Control Block as word 3 bits 3\u201317, \"Location of entry point for standard or non-standard label image routines\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 75 / printed p. 67), the FCB diagram labelling the same field \"Entry point for non-standard label image routines\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 73 / printed p. 65). There is no control-card route to it: the Preprocessor's \\*FILE card carries only column 32, \"Labeling conventions: H\u2014High density label / L\u2014Low density label / S\u2014Standard density convention / blank\u2014No label\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 52 / printed p. 44). The package can therefore only be planted by the program's own ATTACH list \u2014 for COMTRAN, by compiler-generated code. *(Errata for the \u00a745/open-question-45 entry, which glosses item 3 as \"item 3 of the list of what happens when standard label images are assumed\": the list is the **non-zero-decrement** case, i.e. what happens when a non-standard package **is** supplied. The 14-word figure it cites is unaffected; only the framing needs correcting.)*\n\n*Register and parameter conventions.* Two rules precede the vector: \"5. At each TSX, index register 2 will contain the 2's complement of the location of the first word of the File Control Block for the file being processed.\" and \"6. The contents of all index registers used must be saved and restored.\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 33 / printed p. 25). There are **no parameter words**: the label image itself is the parameter and it is passed in storage \u2014 the 14-word area \"LAREA | IOCS + 43 to IOCS + 56 | Label area (14 words)\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 47 / printed p. 39), which is the area COMTRAN's LABEL type code redefines (J 02.05.03) and which J calls IOC)29 (J 90.02.08). Everything else the routine might need is reachable off index register 2: file serial number (FCB word 7), reel sequence number (word 8), retention days (word 9), file name (words 10\u201312), the block-sequence counter (word 4 bits 3\u201317) and the label/multi-reel/density flags (word 2) (external: C28-6100-2, PDF pp. 74\u201376 / printed pp. 66\u201368).\n\n*The vector, verbatim.* \"Assuming that the non-standard label package begins at symbolic location MYLBLS, then the following actions will occur as indicated:\n 1. `TSX MYLBLS, 1` \u2014 To check header label input file. The routine must return by means of: `TRA 1, 1` if label is correct / `TRA 2, 1` if the input label is invalid. Bit 35 of the 7th word of the label area must be 1 upon either return if a checkpoint record is to be skipped.\n 2. `TSX MYLBLS + 1, 1` \u2014 To check trailer label, input file. Return must always be: `TRA 1, 1`. Upon return, the sign of the MQ must be: + if EOR; tape switching will occur / \u2212 if EOF; an EOF exit will be taken.\n 3. `TSX MYLBLS + 2, 1` \u2014 To check label on tapes to be used for output. Return must be: `TRA 1, 1` if the reel may be used / `TRA 2, 1` if (creation date + retention days) has *not* been reached / `TRA 3, 1` if it is not possible to use the reel.\n 4. `TSX MYLBLS + 3, 1` \u2014 To prepare output trailer label. Return must always be: `TRA 1, 1`. Upon return, bits P, 1-35 of the AC must contain: `1EORbb` if an EOR trailer is to be prepared / `1EOFbb` if an EOF trailer is to be prepared.\n 5. `TSX MYLBLS + 4, 1` \u2014 To prepare output header label. Return is: `TRA 1, 1`.\"\n(external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 33 / printed p. 25; transcribed from a 300-dpi crop, the manual setting each alternative return on its own line.)\n\n*How the routine learns which event applies:* **by entry point \u2014 there is no event code and no parameter.** The five cells are one word each and IOCS enters the one appropriate to the event, so both the event and the direction (input vs output) are implicit in the entry chosen. Mapping onto J's three COMTRAN events (\"when the file is opened or closed, or when a reel switch occurs\", J 02.05.03): opening an input file \u2192 entry 1; opening an output file \u2192 entry 3, vetting the reel already mounted, then entry 5, writing the new header; reel switch on input \u2192 entry 2 for the exhausted reel's trailer, then entry 1 for the new reel's header; reel switch on output \u2192 entry 4 with AC = `1EORbb`, then entries 3 and 5 for the new reel; closing an output file \u2192 entry 4 with AC = `1EOFbb`; closing an *input* file \u2192 no call at all, the trailer having been checked when it was read. That mapping is *inference* \u2014 the manual nowhere tabulates events against entries \u2014 drawn from the entry descriptions plus IOCS's own standard-label timing, \"During writing, an EOR label is prepared by IOCS when the end of a reel is encountered. An EOF label is prepared when the file is closed\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 31 / printed p. 23), and the glossary's definition of CLOSE as the routine used \"to terminate file usage, prepare end of file trailer labels, and rewind the file\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 83 / printed p. 75).\n\n*Two returns are decisions, not merely status.* At entry 2 the **user routine, not IOCS**, decides whether the reel just finished is the last: the MQ sign it leaves determines whether IOCS switches reels or raises end of file \u2014 exactly the discrimination the standard trailer's `1EORbb`/`1EOFbb` identifier makes for standard labels (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 31 / printed p. 23). At entry 4 the routine likewise announces in the AC which trailer kind it has built. How it is expected to *know* which is wanted at entry 4 is not stated; the natural sources are FCB word 2 bit 15 (\"End-of-tape: 1 \u2013 No / 0 \u2013 Yes\") or bit 16 (\"File permanently closed\"), both reachable off index register 2 (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 75 / printed p. 67) \u2014 *inference*, supported by no text.\n\n*What the routine may do:* only inspect and fill the 14-word image. All tape motion stays with IOCS (\"All labels, both headers and trailers, will be read and written by the system\"; \"All EOF marks associated with labels will also be read and written by the system\"), and the system's own reactions are unchanged (\"All the system actions and messages will apply\") \u2014 so entry 1's `TRA 2, 1` still produces the standard `UNIT XXXXX (file name) LABEL ERROR` printout with its operator halt and Label Control Sense Switch handling, and entry 3's `TRA 2, 1` / `TRA 3, 1` still produce `LABEL ERROR, RETENTION NOT EXPIRED` / `LABEL ERROR, OUTPUT REEL INVALID` (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 32 / printed p. 24; PDF p. 68 / printed p. 60; PDF p. 63 / printed p. 55). The mechanism reaches only labels that \"adhere to the general IOCS labeling scheme\"; \"If non-standard labeling procedures are desired, the IOCS labeling routines must be entirely replaced\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 32 / printed p. 24) \u2014 beyond anything COMTRAN can express, and consistent with J's own 14-word ceiling on LABELN. Note also that \"Labeling is not available for files processed on any on-line card equipment\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 29 / printed p. 21), so FOR LABEL is meaningless on a card file.\n\n*How control returns:* by **skip return through index register 1** \u2014 `TRA n, 1` reaching the (n\u22121)th word after the TSX. This settles J's otherwise unmotivated CRYPT requirement twice over: no COMTRAN statement can execute `TRA 2, 1`, set the MQ sign, or load the AC with a BCD identifier; and IOCS enters with IR1 as the linkage register and IR2 loaded, whereas COMTRAN object code uses IR1 as a record base register (`CLA 1)DATE,1`) and IR4 as its TSX linkage and data base register (J 90.02.03\u201306; J 90.05 listing) \u2014 so a COMTRAN procedure entered this way would have its base registers destroyed unless CRYPT code honours item 6 and saves them (*inference*).\n\n*Still open \u2014 the COMTRAN surface.* `FOR LABEL statement.name` names **one** procedure while IOCS wants **five** entry points, and neither manual says how the compiler bridges the two: whether it emits a five-word vector all of whose entries funnel into the named procedure after setting a discriminator, and if so what cell the programmer is to test. No such cell is named anywhere in J (02.05.03, 02.06.05, 02.06.12, 90.02, 90.04), and Appendix 90.07, which would have shown it, is marked \"(Not Currently Available)\". Recommendation for the compiler: generate the five-word vector, plant its address in the ATTACH file-list decrement (FCB word 3 bits 3\u201317), and define a documented event code the FOR LABEL procedure can test \u2014 a design choice, not a recovered fact. **Confidence: certain** on the IOCS calling convention (printed in full, verified at 300 dpi); **tentative** on the COMTRAN-to-IOCS glue. \u00a78.5.6's \"FOR LABEL / LABELN linkage documented only by a missing appendix\" should accordingly be *narrowed* \u2014 the runtime linkage is now fully specified \u2014 rather than closed.\n\n=== Q50 ===\n\n**Resolved (external source, 2026-08-02): no count check of any kind is performed outside standard labels**, and F p. 41's record-count comparison has no IOCS counterpart even inside them.\n\n*What IOCS checks on a standard-labeled input file.* Word 2 of the trailer is a **block** count, and it is verified for every reel, not only the last: \"BLOCK COUNT is the number of data blocks on this reel. This is checked for every input reel. If it differs from the number of blocks actually read in, a sequence error is given. In this case it should be noted that a sequence error for the reel can occur even though each block is not being checked for sequence\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 31 / printed p. 23), restated under Input File Trailer Labels as \"The Block Count which appears in every trailer written by the system is checked and a sequence error exit is taken if it does not agree with the number of blocks 'read.'\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 32 / printed p. 24; verified on a 300-dpi crop). The running total is File Control Block word 4 bits 3\u201317, \"Counter for block sequence checking\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 75 / printed p. 67): \"Every File Control Block carries a block sequence number for the current reel of the file. On a labeled file, this sequence number is always written in a trailer label at the end of each reel and checked when it is read\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 41 / printed p. 33).\n\n*What a mismatch does \u2014 and where.* Not a message: a program exit. The condition is a \"sequence error\", one of the three ERR conditions of the READ routine \u2014 \"ERR is the location to which transfer is made when any of three types of error conditions occur: (a) a redundancy which cannot be corrected; (b) check sum error (binary file); and (c) sequence error. The condition is recognized at the first reference to a buffer in which it occurs. The error encountered may be ignored by continuing to 'read' the file\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 24 / printed p. 16). The abnormal-conditions table gives \"READ | Block sequence error | ERROR exit MQ(S-2) = 1\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 79 / printed p. 71), matching the history-record convention \"1\u2014if a sequence error occurred\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 25 / printed p. 17) and the glossary's ERR (\"an exit \u2026 upon recognition of an uncorrectable redundancy, or a block sequence or check sum error\", external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 84 / printed p. 76). **No block-count or sequence-error text appears anywhere in the IOCS System Messages and Halts list** (external: C28-6100-2, PDF pp. 66\u201370 / printed pp. 58\u201362). In COMTRAN terms the mismatch therefore surfaces at the file's ON ERROR exit, defaulting to SYS)283 (J 90.02.32) \u2014 not as a close-time display.\n\n*And it never fires at CLOSE.* The check is inside READ, at the moment the trailer is read. CLOSE's label work is output-side only: \"For a regular output file, a close other than the MON type, causes writing of (1) an EOF mark, a trailer label, and another EOF mark, if the file is labeled, or (2) an EOF mark only, if the file is unlabeled\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 27 / printed p. 19), and the glossary defines CLOSE as \"The IOCS routine used to terminate file usage, prepare end of file trailer labels, and rewind the file\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 83 / printed p. 75). Nothing at close compares anything on an input file.\n\n*Unlabeled files: nothing at all.* With no labels there is no trailer and hence no count, and IOCS says so by making end-of-data the programmer's problem. Single-reel: \"It is the programmer's responsibility, in this case, to determine which EOF mark signifies the actual end of the file. No reel switching is possible for input files of this type.\" Multi-reel: \"An EOF exit is given once per reel for each EOF encountered and if the file is again referenced by a READ operation, reel switching will occur and buffering will be resumed. For this type of file, the user must have a recognizable data record to indicate that the end of the file has been reached.\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 34 / printed p. 26; verified on a 300-dpi crop.) *This also settles open question 53:* nothing substitutes for the end-of-reel label check on an unlabeled MULTI input file \u2014 a sentinel data record is the only recourse, which is exactly the HIGH.VALUE idiom the J sample uses.\n\n*The one check independent of labeling \u2014 and its unstated restriction.* \"In addition, for a binary file, a block sequence word can be appended to the end of each block, regardless of whether the file is labeled or unlabeled. This word contains the sequence number of the block in its address and may also contain an 18-bit folded check sum of the block in the left half of the word.\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 41 / printed p. 33; verified on a 300-dpi crop). These are the object-time counterparts of COMTRAN's SPECIF SEQ and CKSUMS, which reach the \\*FILE card as column 33 \"Block sequence numbering flag (**significant for binary files only**): S\u2014Sequencing words provided\" and column 34 \"Check sum flag (**available for binary sequence numbered files only**): C\u2014Check sums included\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 52 / printed p. 44), and the FCB as word 2 bits 10\u201311 (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 74 / printed p. 66). J states only \"Block checksums are not checked unless the Environment SPECIF card option CKSUMS is selected\" (J 02.07.06) and never mentions the binary restriction \u2014 so *inference*: on a BCD COMTRAN file (the normal case, and every file in the J sample) SEQ and CKSUMS are inoperative, and CKSUMS additionally requires SEQ. Worth recording in \u00a77 alongside the option table.\n\n*LABELN / non-standard labels.* IOCS still reads and writes the labels and their EOF marks and still maintains the block counter, but the **content** check passes to the user: entry `MYLBLS + 1` \"To check trailer label, input file\" is the routine that sees the trailer image (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 33 / printed p. 25). *Inference:* IOCS cannot itself perform the block-count comparison on a non-standard trailer, because it does not know where in the image the count lies; the manual scopes the check to \"every trailer **written by the system**\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 32 / printed p. 24), and checking is exactly the half of the labeling routines the package replaces (\"using different routines to check and prepare label images\", same page). What IOCS still supplies is the running counter in FCB word 4 bits 3\u201317, reachable off index register 2 at every entry, so a FOR LABEL routine can do the comparison itself. Countervailing evidence, which the manual never disambiguates: item 4 of the same list, \"All the system actions and messages will apply\", could be read as retaining it. **Confidence on this sub-point: probable.**\n\n*Can F p. 41 be mapped onto IOCS at all?* F says \"The record count is compared with the count in the end-of-file label if label records are present and if end of file has been reached. If the count does not agree, notification is given through external display. If the tape is not at end of file the record count is ignored\" (F p. 41), the count having been started at OPEN (\"Checking is performed, and a record count is initiated\", F p. 39, for input and output alike) and maintained at FILE (\"A count of the number of records written is maintained\", F p. 41). Four of its five elements fail against IOCS, and the failure of the first is definitional. **Unit:** IOCS's glossary fixes \"record\" in the physical sense \u2014 \"BLOCK \u2014 A physical record; that is, a tape record, a card, or a line of print\"; \"BLOCK COUNT \u2014 The number of blocks processed on the current reel of a file, kept in the corresponding File Control Block\"; against \"LOGICAL RECORD \u2014 Data which, as a group, has some logical significance to the user\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF pp. 83, 85 / printed pp. 75, 77). Every count IOCS keeps is of blocks, and **no record count appears in any standard label**. The only two other places IOCS counts \"records\" are tape-repositioning data \u2014 \"If the Unit Control Block is for a tape unit, it also contains counts which specify the current position of the tape (file count, and record count within the file)\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 49 / printed p. 41) \u2014 and the reel-removal printout \"UNIT XXXXX (file name) REEL XXXX \u2013 XXXXX RECORDS\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 67 / printed p. 59); neither is a label field and neither is compared with anything. *Inference:* F's record count coincides with IOCS's block count only on an unblocked file, one logical record per block; for any COMTRAN file with a BLOCKSIZE holding more than one record, F's quantity denotes nothing IOCS keeps. **Timing:** F at CLOSE, IOCS inside READ when the trailer is read. **Reporting:** F \"notification \u2026 through external display\", IOCS a sequence-error ERR exit with MQ prefix 1 and no printed message at all. **Scope:** F only at end of file, IOCS every input reel. What *does* survive is F's conditional \"if label records are present\" \u2014 precisely right, since outside standard labels there is nothing to compare \u2014 and F's output-side clause \"If specified by the programmer, an end-of-file label containing the record count is written\", which is structurally right: IOCS writes the `1EOFbb` trailer at close with a count in word 2 at exactly that point, only it counts blocks.\n\n*Resolution for the language definition.* Answer to the question as put: **no** \u2014 under LABELN the check is whatever the FOR LABEL routine performs, and unlabeled files get none. F p. 41's input-side close-time record-count comparison should be recorded as an **F/J divergence resolved against F** and dropped from the implemented semantics; what a COMTRAN compiler must actually document is that a labeled input file's per-reel block count is verified automatically by IOCS and that a mismatch is delivered to the file's ON ERROR exit, resumable, with no message. **Confidence: certain** on the main claim and on the block-vs-record finding; **probable** on the LABELN delegation; the record \u2194 block identification is *inference*."}, "verdict": {"verdict": "CORRECTED", "problems": ["WRONG RETURN ARITHMETIC (mechanical error, load-bearing). The draft says control returns \"by skip return through index register 1 \u2014 `TRA n, 1` reaching the (n\u22121)th word after the TSX\". That is off by one and would mis-specify any generated vector. TSX places in the index register the 2's complement of the location of the TSX *itself*: \"TSX \u2014 Transfer and Set Index ... This instruction places the 2's complement of the core address of the TSX(IC) in the specified index register (T). The computer takes its next instruction from location Y.\" (external: 22-6528-4, PDF p. 39 / printed p. 39), and \"Effective addresses are always formed in the computer by the addition of the 2's complement of the contents of the index register\" (external: 22-6528-4, PDF p. 10 / printed p. 10). Hence TRA 1,1 \u2192 TSX+1 (the word immediately after the TSX), TRA 2,1 \u2192 TSX+2, TRA 3,1 \u2192 TSX+3 \u2014 the *n*th word after the TSX, and the caller must reserve up to two words after each TSX for the alternate returns.", "UNATTESTED INSTRUCTION QUOTED AS EVIDENCE. The draft cites COMTRAN's use of IR1 as a record base register with \"`CLA 1)DATE,1`\". No such instruction exists in J. The listing has `CAL 1)DATE,1` (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 211) and, for the CLA form, `LAC BL)2,1` / `CLA 1)FICA,1` (J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 210). Corrected in the replacement text, which now uses only attested instructions.", "INTERNAL CONTRADICTION IN THE Q50 HEADLINE. The draft says \"no count check of any kind exists outside standard labels\" and then, two sentences later, that the per-block sequence word is checked \"regardless of whether the file is labeled or unlabeled\". A block sequence number *is* a count check. The claim must be scoped to reel/file-level counts: no per-reel or per-file count comparison exists outside standard labels; the per-block sequence number is the one labeling-independent sequence check, and it is binary-files-only.", "OVERSTATED \"SOLE INTEGRITY CHECK\". The draft calls the sequence word + check sum \"the sole labeling-independent integrity check\". Uncorrectable-redundancy detection is also labeling-independent and is one of the three ERR conditions (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 24 / printed p. 16). Narrowed to \"the only labeling-independent *sequence/count* check\".", "TWO INFERENCES PRESENTED AS FACT. (a) The draft asserts that entry 1's `TRA 2,1` \"still produces\" the LABEL ERROR printout and entry 3's `TRA 2,1`/`TRA 3,1` \"still produce\" RETENTION NOT EXPIRED / OUTPUT REEL INVALID. The manual says only \"All the system actions and messages will apply\"; the routing of specific user-routine returns to specific messages is unstated \u2014 now labeled *inference*. (b) \"it never fires at CLOSE\" is an argument from the two descriptions (check described inside READ trailer processing; CLOSE's label work described as output-side only), not a stated negative \u2014 now labeled *inference*. Also the LAREA = IOC)29 equation is stated flatly in Q46 while open question 45 correctly flags it as *inference*; made consistent.", "MISSED DECISIVE J EVIDENCE FOR THE \"STILL OPEN\" PART OF Q46. Appendix 90.08 tabulates every field of the Loader *FILE card the compiler generates from Environment FILE/SPECIF options \u2014 cols 17, 18-21, 22-25, 27, 28, 29, 30, 31, 32, 33, 34, 35, 38-41, 44-48, 51-53, 54-72 \u2014 and there is **no field for a label routine entry point** (J 90.08.01). Combined with the FCB convention \"Items marked with an asterisk are generated in the File Control Block by the Preprocessor. The remaining positions are initially zero\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 73 / printed p. 65), under which word 3 bits 3-17 carries no asterisk, this converts the draft's assertion into positive evidence: the address cannot arrive by control card and must be planted at object time, either through the ATTACH list (IOC)5, \"The entry point to the IOCS ATTACH subroutine\", J 90.02.08) or by storing into FCB word 3 via IOC)2.", "MISSED J CORROBORATION THAT RAISES THE LABELN SUB-POINT. The draft rates the LABELN delegation \"probable\" on IOCS text alone, missing J 02.06.12: SERIAL/REEL/RETAIN \"is used by IOCS in checking and preparing standard labels (but not non-standard labels) and may be used by the programmer in checking and preparing non-standard labels\" \u2014 a stated J sentence that IOCS's own checking stops at standard labels and that the programmer's routine does the checking.", "MISSED THAT J STATES THE ON ERROR MAPPING OUTRIGHT, AND MISSED THE GAP IN J IT EXPOSES. The draft infers that a block-count mismatch surfaces at the ON ERROR exit. J says it: \"IOCS cannot continue processing after discovering an unrecoverable redundancy error, a block checksum error or a block sequence error without direction from the programmer. The ON ERROR option of the Environment FILE card provides for communication between IOCS and the programmer in these three error situations\" (J 02.07.07) \u2014 the same triple as IOCS's ERR conditions, and the two calling sequences correspond word for word (IOCS `TSX READ,4` / `PZE FILE,,EOB` / `PZE EOF,,ERR`, external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 23 / printed p. 15, against J's `TSX IOC)8,4` / `PZE FILENAME,,SYS)260` / `PZE END-OF-FILE-PROCEDURE,,ERROR-PROCEDURE`, J 90.02.04). The finding this unlocks and the draft misses: J 02.07.06 says \"If block sequence checking is desired the Environment SPECIF card option SEQ must be selected\", but IOCS checks the trailer block count on every reel of any labeled file irrespective of SEQ \u2014 \"a sequence error for the reel can occur even though each block is not being checked for sequence\". J's account is therefore incomplete, and a LABELS file without SEQ can still take the ON ERROR exit with a sequence error.", "MISSED DIRECT EVIDENCE, SETTLING TWO INFERENCES THE DRAFT LEFT SOFT. (a) \"CKSUMS additionally requires SEQ\" is not merely inferred from the card legend: FCB word 2 bits 10-11 encode \"00 \u2013 No block sequence words / 10 \u2013 Block sequence word is present, no check sums. / 11 \u2013 Block sequence word is present, check sums are present (input) or are to be computed (output)\" \u2014 there is no state for check sums without a sequence word (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 74 / printed p. 66). (b) The compiler passes SEQ/CKSUMS straight through to those very columns \u2014 col 33 \"block sequence numbers to be checked | SPECIF - SEQ\", col 34 \"block checksums to be checked | SPECIF - CKSUMS\" (J 90.08.01) \u2014 so it never enforces the binary restriction; and \"If no specification is made the file is assumed to be BCD\" (J 02.06.04) establishes the default the draft asserted without citation.", "Q53 SETTLEMENT LEFT WITHOUT ITS J\u2192IOCS BRIDGE. The draft asserts the unlabeled-MULTI case is settled but never shows that a COMTRAN MULTI-without-labels file *is* IOCS's case. J 90.08.01 col 29: \"M - multi-reel unlabeled file | MULTI, neither LABELS nor LABELN\", which is exactly IOCS's \"Multi-Reel Unlabeled Files\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 34 / printed p. 26). Added.", "F/J FRAMING TOO BLUNT. The draft would record F p. 41 as \"an F/J divergence resolved against F\". J does not diverge \u2014 it explicitly defers: \"A file is referenced by the commands OPEN and CLOSE to initiate and complete file activity. (See Commercial Translator General Information manual).\" (J 02.07.01). So F's text remains J's only prose on CLOSE while the object-time system provides no mechanism to honour it. The replacement says this, and adds the positive evidence the draft lacked: the compiled sample maintains no record count at all \u2014 the GET sequence is four words with no counter increment (`00200 TSX IOC)8,4` / `PZE INPUTMASTER,,SYS)260` / `PZE GN)058,,SYS)283` / `IOCTN* BL)2,,15`) and `CLOSE ALL FILES` compiles to `TSX SYS)177,4` / `PZE IOC)1` with no comparison (J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 201, 204).", "CITATION SLIPPAGE (minor). \"J 90.02.03\u201306\" for the base-register convention should be J 90.02.04\u201305 (`LAC BL)2,4` / `CAL DATANAME,4` and the accompanying sentence fall between the 90.02.04 and 90.02.05 markers). The abnormal-conditions row prints MQ with a subscript, \"ERROR exit MQ(S-2) = 1\" is better rendered MQ_{S-2}. Both annotations also lacked the trailing consolidated citation list that every neighbouring entry in the file carries (cf. open questions 40, 41, 47).", "VERIFIED AND UNCHANGED (recorded so the parent knows what was checked): every C28-6100-2 verbatim quote in the draft matches the page image character for character, including the full five-entry vector on printed p. 25, the four-item non-standard-package list on printed p. 24, the BLOCK COUNT paragraph on printed p. 23, both unlabeled-file paragraphs on printed p. 26, the ERR paragraph on printed p. 16, the CLOSE paragraph on printed p. 19 and the Section 9 opening on printed p. 33. Every PDF/printed pair is correct (printed = PDF \u2212 8 throughout). The claim that no block-count or sequence-error message appears in the message list is confirmed \u2014 I read printed pp. 58\u201362 in full plus the restart messages on pp. 63\u201364. The Q45 errata is confirmed: the manual's list is the non-zero-decrement case, so the existing entry's gloss \"the list of what happens when standard label images are assumed\" is indeed wrong."], "corrected_annotation": "=== Q46 (append to open question 46) ===\n\n**Resolved (external source, 2026-08-02):** the IOCS manual prints the linkage in full, and it is not a single exit but a **five-entry transfer vector** \u2014 which is precisely why one COMTRAN `FOR LABEL statement.name` cannot express it and why \"portions of the FOR LABEL coding must be done in CRYPT\" (J 02.05.03).\n\n*Where the routine's address lives.* \"If the non-standard labels to be processed adhere to the general IOCS labeling scheme, non-standard label images may be easily handled by IOCS, using different routines to check and prepare label images. For this purpose, IOCS allows a non-standard label image package to be specified for each file. The location of the first cell of this package can be specified in the decrement of each entry in the list of files used for the ATTACH routine. If the decrement is zero, standard images are assumed; if it is non-zero, the following procedures will be executed: 1. All labels, both headers and trailers, will be read and written by the system. 2. All EOF marks associated with labels will also be read and written by the system. 3. The standard label area at symbolic locations IOCS + 43 (see page 39) will be used; its length is 14 words. 4. All the system actions and messages will apply.\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 32 / printed p. 24). The address is then held in the File Control Block as word 3 bits 3\u201317, \"Location of entry point for standard or non-standard label image routines\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 75 / printed p. 67), the FCB diagram labelling the same field \"Entry point for non-standard label image routines\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 73 / printed p. 65). That field carries **no asterisk**, and the appendix's key reads \"Items marked with an asterisk are generated in the File Control Block by the Preprocessor. The remaining positions are initially zero.\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 73 / printed p. 65) \u2014 so the Preprocessor never fills it, and the zero it is left holding is exactly the \"decrement is zero, standard images are assumed\" default (*inference*, from the two statements together). *(Errata for the open-question-45 entry, which glosses the 14-word sentence as \"item 3 of the list of what happens when standard label images are assumed\": the list is the **non-zero-decrement** case \u2014 what happens when a non-standard package **is** supplied. The 14-word figure it cites is unaffected; only the framing needs correcting.)*\n\n*There is no control-card route to it, in either system.* The IOCS Preprocessor's \\*FILE card carries only column 32, \"Labeling conventions: H\u2014High density label / L\u2014Low density label / S\u2014Standard density convention / blank\u2014No label\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 52 / printed p. 44). Decisively for COMTRAN, J's own Appendix 90.08 tabulates every field of the Loader \\*FILE card **as generated by the Compiler** from Environment FILE and SPECIF options \u2014 columns 17, 18-21, 22-25, 27, 28, 29, 30, 31, 32, 33, 34, 35, 38-41, 44-48, 51-53 and 54-72 \u2014 and none of them is a label-routine entry point (J 90.08.01). The address can therefore only be planted at object time by generated code, by one of two routes J's runtime already has: the ATTACH file-list decrement, IOC)5 being \"The entry point to the IOCS ATTACH subroutine\", or a direct store into FCB word 3 through IOC)2, \"A cell in the CT Monitor communication area which locates (L) a number (N) of IOCS file blocks. I/O information is kept in each of the 12 word blocks for every file in the CT program\" (J 90.02.08). *Inference*: neither manual says which the field-test compiler used.\n\n*Register and parameter conventions.* Two rules precede the vector: \"5. At each TSX, index register 2 will contain the 2's complement of the location of the first word of the File Control Block for the file being processed.\" and \"6. The contents of all index registers used must be saved and restored.\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 33 / printed p. 25). There are **no parameter words**: the label image is the parameter and it is passed in storage \u2014 the 14-word area \"LAREA | IOCS + 43 to IOCS + 56 | Label area (14 words)\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 47 / printed p. 39), which is the area COMTRAN's LABEL type code redefines (J 02.05.03) and which is almost certainly J's IOC)29, \"a 14 word area within IOCS which is used to process all labels\" (J 90.02.08) \u2014 *inference* from matching size and role, as flagged at open question 45; neither manual states the equation. Everything else the routine needs is reachable off index register 2: file serial number (FCB word 7), reel sequence number (word 8), retention days (word 9), file name (words 10\u201312), the block-sequence counter (word 4 bits 3\u201317), and the label / multi-reel / density / end-of-tape flags (word 2) (external: C28-6100-2, PDF pp. 74\u201376 / printed pp. 66\u201368). J confirms the programmer is expected to work from exactly those cells: the SERIAL, REEL and RETAIN information \"is kept in the IOCS file block ... It is used by IOCS in checking and preparing standard labels (but not non-standard labels) and may be used by the programmer in checking and preparing non-standard labels\" (J 02.06.12).\n\n*The vector, verbatim.* \"Assuming that the non-standard label package begins at symbolic location MYLBLS, then the following actions will occur as indicated:\n 1. `TSX MYLBLS, 1` \u2014 To check header label input file. The routine must return by means of: `TRA 1, 1` if label is correct / `TRA 2, 1` if the input label is invalid. Bit 35 of the 7th word of the label area must be 1 upon either return if a checkpoint record is to be skipped.\n 2. `TSX MYLBLS + 1, 1` \u2014 To check trailer label, input file. Return must always be: `TRA 1, 1`. Upon return, the sign of the MQ must be: + if EOR; tape switching will occur / \u2212 if EOF; an EOF exit will be taken.\n 3. `TSX MYLBLS + 2, 1` \u2014 To check label on tapes to be used for output. Return must be: `TRA 1, 1` if the reel may be used / `TRA 2, 1` if (creation date + retention days) has *not* been reached / `TRA 3, 1` if it is not possible to use the reel.\n 4. `TSX MYLBLS + 3, 1` \u2014 To prepare output trailer label. Return must always be: `TRA 1, 1`. Upon return, bits P, 1-35 of the AC must contain: `1EORbb` if an EOR trailer is to be prepared / `1EOFbb` if an EOF trailer is to be prepared.\n 5. `TSX MYLBLS + 4, 1` \u2014 To prepare output header label. Return is: `TRA 1, 1`.\"\n(external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 33 / printed p. 25, the manual setting each alternative return on its own line.) Item 1's checkpoint bit is consistent with the standard header layout, where BCD position 42 \u2014 the last character of word 7, i.e. bit 35 \u2014 is the \"Checkpoint record indicator\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 30 / printed p. 22).\n\n*How the routine learns which event applies:* **by entry point \u2014 there is no event code and no parameter.** The five cells are one word each and IOCS enters the one appropriate to the event, so both the event and its direction are implicit in the entry chosen. Mapping onto J's three COMTRAN events (\"when the file is opened or closed, or when a reel switch occurs\", J 02.05.03): opening an input file \u2192 entry 1; opening an output file \u2192 entry 3, vetting the reel already mounted, then entry 5, writing the new header; reel switch on input \u2192 entry 2 for the exhausted reel's trailer, then entry 1 for the new reel's header; reel switch on output \u2192 entry 4 with AC = `1EORbb`, then entries 3 and 5 for the new reel; closing an output file \u2192 entry 4 with AC = `1EOFbb`; closing an *input* file \u2192 no call at all, the trailer having been checked when it was read. That mapping is *inference* \u2014 the manual nowhere tabulates events against entries \u2014 drawn from the entry descriptions plus IOCS's standard-label timing, \"Note: During writing, an EOR label is prepared by IOCS when the end of a reel is encountered. An EOF label is prepared when the file is closed.\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 31 / printed p. 23), and the glossary's CLOSE, \"The IOCS routine used to terminate file usage, prepare end of file trailer labels, and rewind the file\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 83 / printed p. 75).\n\n*Two returns are decisions, not merely status.* At entry 2 the **user routine, not IOCS**, decides whether the reel just finished is the last: the MQ sign it leaves determines whether IOCS switches reels or raises end of file \u2014 the same discrimination the standard trailer's `1EORbb`/`1EOFbb` identifier makes for standard labels (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 31 / printed p. 23). At entry 4 the routine announces in the AC which trailer kind it has built. How it is expected to *know* which is wanted is not stated; the natural sources are FCB word 2 bit 15 (\"End-of-tape: 1 \u2013 No / 0 \u2013 Yes\") or bit 16 (\"File permanently closed\"), both reachable off index register 2 (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 75 / printed p. 67) \u2014 *inference*, supported by no text.\n\n*What the routine may do:* only inspect and fill the 14-word image. All tape motion stays with IOCS (\"All labels, both headers and trailers, will be read and written by the system\"; \"All EOF marks associated with labels will also be read and written by the system\"), and the system's own reactions are unchanged (\"All the system actions and messages will apply\") \u2014 so the standard label-error apparatus remains in force: `UNIT XXXXX (file name) LABEL ERROR` with its cause line, `IF SSWX DOWN IGNORED`, `OPERATOR ACTION PAUSE`, and Label Control Sense Switch handling (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 68 / printed p. 60; PDF p. 63 / printed p. 55). *Inference*, and unstated: that entry 1's `TRA 2, 1` is what raises `INPUT REEL INVALID` and entry 3's `TRA 2, 1` / `TRA 3, 1` what raise `RETENTION NOT EXPIRED` / `OUTPUT REEL INVALID`; the manual says only that the system messages apply, never which return triggers which. A hard length constraint also survives into the non-standard case: IOCS rejects any label record that is not exactly fourteen words, \"(file name) NO LABEL, BLANK CREATED \u2014 The record read as the label of an input file was not fourteen words in length\" and \"(file name) NO TRAILER \u2014 The record read as the trailer label of an input file was not fourteen words in length. The end of file condition is assumed. There is no stop associated with this error.\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 69 / printed p. 61) \u2014 so J's \"a label of 14 words or less\" (J 02.06.11) must mean the programmer may *use* fewer than 14 words of the area, not write a shorter record (*inference*). Finally, the mechanism reaches only labels that \"adhere to the general IOCS labeling scheme\"; \"Non-standard labels may be either a mere arrangement of the standard format or an entirely different labeling procedure. If non-standard labeling procedures are desired, the IOCS labeling routines must be entirely replaced. However, if only changes in label formats are desired, the IOCS label handling routines will still apply.\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 32 / printed p. 24) \u2014 the second case being beyond anything COMTRAN can express. Note also \"Labeling is not available for files processed on any on-line card equipment.\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 29 / printed p. 21), so FOR LABEL is inoperative on a card file.\n\n*How control returns:* by **skip return through index register 1**. TSX leaves in the index register the 2's complement of its own location \u2014 \"TSX \u2014 Transfer and Set Index ... This instruction places the 2's complement of the core address of the TSX(IC) in the specified index register (T). The computer takes its next instruction from location Y.\" (external: 22-6528-4, PDF p. 39 / printed p. 39) \u2014 and \"Effective addresses are always formed in the computer by the addition of the 2's complement of the contents of the index register.\" (external: 22-6528-4, PDF p. 10 / printed p. 10). So `TRA 1, 1` returns to the word immediately following the TSX, `TRA 2, 1` to the second word after it and `TRA 3, 1` to the third: at entries 1 and 3 the caller must reserve one and two words respectively after the TSX for the alternate returns. This settles J's otherwise unmotivated CRYPT requirement three times over. (i) No COMTRAN statement can execute `TRA 2, 1`, set the MQ sign, or load the AC with a BCD identifier. (ii) The machine has only three index registers \u2014 \"Three index registers (XR) are used in the computer. These registers are called A, B, and C or 1, 2, and 4.\" (external: 22-6528-4, PDF p. 9 / printed p. 9) \u2014 and IOCS commandeers two of them at every entry, IR1 as the return link and IR2 as the FCB pointer, while COMTRAN object code uses IR1 and IR2 freely as record base registers (`LAC BL)2,1` / `CLA 1)FICA,1`, J 90.05 listing, PDF p. 210; `CAL 1)DATE,1`, PDF p. 211; `SLW 1)DATE,2`, PDF p. 205) and IR4 as its TSX linkage and data base register (`LAC BL)2,4` / `CAL DATANAME,4`, J 90.02.04\u201305). A COMTRAN procedure entered at a label exit would destroy the return address in IR1 at its first based data reference (*inference*). (iii) Item 6 obliges the routine to save and restore whatever it uses, which no COMTRAN-level construct does.\n\n*Still open \u2014 the COMTRAN surface.* `FOR LABEL statement.name` names **one** procedure while IOCS wants **five** entry points, and neither manual says how the compiler bridges the two: whether it emits a five-word vector all of whose entries funnel into the named procedure after setting a discriminator, and if so what cell the programmer is to test. No such cell is named anywhere in J (02.05.03, 02.06.05, 02.06.12, 90.02, 90.04, 90.08), and Appendix 90.07, \"SAMPLE NON STANDARD LABEL PROCESSING (Not Currently Available)\", which would have shown it, does not exist in this edition. Recommendation for the compiler: generate the five-word vector, plant its address in the ATTACH file-list decrement (equivalently FCB word 3 bits 3\u201317), reserve the alternate-return words after each TSX, and define a documented event code the FOR LABEL procedure can test \u2014 a design choice, not a recovered fact. **Confidence: certain** on the IOCS calling convention (printed in full, verified against the page images) and on the return arithmetic (7090 principles of operation); **tentative** on the COMTRAN-to-IOCS glue. \u00a78.5.6's \"FOR LABEL / LABELN linkage documented only by a missing appendix\" should accordingly be **narrowed** \u2014 the runtime linkage is now fully specified, and only the compiler's surface-to-vector mapping is unrecovered \u2014 rather than closed. (J 02.05.03; J 02.06.05, 02.06.11\u201312; J 00.00 contents; J 90.02.04\u201305, 90.02.08; J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 205, 210\u2013211; J 90.08.01; external: C28-6100-2, PDF pp. 29\u201334 / printed pp. 21\u201326, PDF p. 47 / printed p. 39, PDF p. 52 / printed p. 44, PDF p. 63 / printed p. 55, PDF pp. 68\u201369 / printed pp. 60\u201361, PDF pp. 73\u201376 / printed pp. 65\u201368, PDF p. 83 / printed p. 75; external: 22-6528-4, PDF/printed pp. 9, 10, 39)\n\n=== Q50 (append to open question 50) ===\n\n**Resolved (external source, 2026-08-02): no reel- or file-level count check exists outside standard labels**, and F p. 41's record-count comparison has no IOCS counterpart even inside them.\n\n*What IOCS checks on a standard-labeled input file.* Word 2 of the trailer is a **block** count, verified for every reel, not only the last: \"BLOCK COUNT is the number of data blocks on this reel. This is checked for every input reel. If it differs from the number of blocks actually read in, a sequence error is given. In this case it should be noted that a sequence error for the reel can occur even though each block is not being checked for sequence\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 31 / printed p. 23), restated under Input File Trailer Labels as \"The Block Count which appears in every trailer written by the system is checked and a sequence error exit is taken if it does not agree with the number of blocks 'read.'\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 32 / printed p. 24). The running total is File Control Block word 4 bits 3\u201317, \"Counter for block sequence checking\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 75 / printed p. 67): \"Every File Control Block carries a block sequence number for the current reel of the file. On a labeled file, this sequence number is always written in a trailer label at the end of each reel and checked when it is read\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 41 / printed p. 33). **Note the consequence for J:** J 02.07.06 says \"If block sequence checking is desired the Environment SPECIF card option SEQ must be selected\", but the trailer check is automatic on any labeled file whatever SEQ says \u2014 the italicised IOCS sentence above exists precisely to make that point. A COMTRAN file with LABELS and no SEQ can therefore still take a block sequence error.\n\n*What a mismatch does \u2014 and where.* Not a message: a program exit. The condition is a \"sequence error\", one of the three ERR conditions of the READ routine \u2014 \"ERR is the location to which transfer is made when any of three types of error conditions occur: (a) a redundancy which cannot be corrected; (b) check sum error (binary file); and (c) sequence error. The condition is recognized at the first reference to a buffer in which it occurs. The error encountered may be ignored by continuing to 'read' the file\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 24 / printed p. 16). The abnormal-conditions table gives \"READ | Block sequence error | ERROR exit MQ(S-2) = 1\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 79 / printed p. 71, MQ subscripted S-2 as printed), matching the history-record convention \"1\u2014if a sequence error occurred\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 25 / printed p. 17) and the glossary's ERR, \"An address, specified in the calling sequence of the READ routine, which is used as an exit upon recognition of an uncorrectable redundancy, or a block sequence or check sum error\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 84 / printed p. 76). **No block-count or sequence-error text appears anywhere in the IOCS System Messages and Halts list** (external: C28-6100-2, PDF pp. 66\u201370 / printed pp. 58\u201362) or in the restart messages (PDF pp. 71\u201372 / printed pp. 63\u201364). In COMTRAN this is the file's ON ERROR exit, and J says so in its own words: \"IOCS cannot continue processing after discovering an unrecoverable redundancy error, a block checksum error or a block sequence error without direction from the programmer. The ON ERROR option of the Environment FILE card provides for communication between IOCS and the programmer in these three error situations\" (J 02.07.07) \u2014 the identical triple, reached through the identical calling-sequence slot, IOCS's READ being \"`TSX READ,4` / `PZE FILE,,EOB` / `PZE EOF,,ERR` / `IOXY A,,m`\" where \"FILE is the file designation, EOB is the end of buffer switch, EOF is the end of file exit, and ERR is the error exit\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 23 / printed p. 15) against J's `TSX IOC)8,4` / `PZE FILENAME,,SYS)260` / `PZE END-OF-FILE-PROCEDURE,,ERROR-PROCEDURE` / `IOCDN* BL)2,,14` (J 90.02.04). The correspondence is word for word, so SYS)260 is the EOB switch and the COMTRAN ON ERROR procedure is IOCS's ERR exit, defaulting to SYS)283 when no ON ERROR is coded (J 90.02.32) \u2014 *inference* from the two templates, corroborated by J's own remark that record length errors are \"referred to as EOB errors in the IOCS manual\" (J 02.07.06). The error is resumable: \"The error encountered may be ignored by continuing to 'read' the file.\"\n\n*And it does not fire at CLOSE.* The check is described inside READ, at the moment the trailer is read; CLOSE's label work is output-side only \u2014 \"For a regular output file, a close other than the MON type, causes writing of (1) an EOF mark, a trailer label, and another EOF mark, if the file is labeled, or (2) an EOF mark only, if the file is unlabeled\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 27 / printed p. 19) \u2014 and the glossary defines CLOSE as \"The IOCS routine used to terminate file usage, prepare end of file trailer labels, and rewind the file\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 83 / printed p. 75). *Inference* (the manual states no negative): nothing at close compares anything on an input file.\n\n*Unlabeled files: nothing at all.* With no labels there is no trailer and hence no count, and IOCS makes end-of-data the programmer's problem. Single-reel: \"It is the programmer's responsibility, in this case, to determine which EOF mark signifies the actual end of the file. No reel switching is possible for input files of this type.\" Multi-reel: \"An EOF exit is given once per reel for each EOF encountered and if the file is again referenced by a READ operation, reel switching will occur and buffering will be resumed. For this type of file, the user must have a recognizable data record to indicate that the end of the file has been reached.\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 34 / printed p. 26.) *This also settles open question 53*, and the J-to-IOCS bridge is explicit: the compiler generates Loader \\*FILE column 29 = \"M - multi-reel unlabeled file\" from \"MULTI, neither LABELS nor LABELN\" (J 90.08.01), which is exactly IOCS's Multi-Reel Unlabeled Files case. Nothing substitutes for the end-of-reel label check \u2014 a sentinel data record is the only recourse, which is the HIGH.VALUE idiom the J sample uses.\n\n*The one check independent of labeling \u2014 and its unstated restriction.* \"In addition, for a binary file, a block sequence word can be appended to the end of each block, regardless of whether the file is labeled or unlabeled. This word contains the sequence number of the block in its address and may also contain an 18-bit folded check sum of the block in the left half of the word.\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 41 / printed p. 33). This is a per-block sequence check, not a file- or reel-level count, and it is the *only* labeling-independent sequence check IOCS has; uncorrectable-redundancy detection, the other labeling-independent integrity mechanism, checks no counts. These are the object-time counterparts of COMTRAN's SPECIF SEQ and CKSUMS: the compiler generates \\*FILE column 33 \"block sequence numbers to be checked | SPECIF - SEQ\" and column 34 \"block checksums to be checked | SPECIF - CKSUMS\" (J 90.08.01), and IOCS's own definition of those columns restricts them \u2014 column 33 \"Block sequence numbering flag (**significant for binary files only**): S\u2014Sequencing words provided\", column 34 \"Check sum flag (**available for binary sequence numbered files only**): C\u2014Check sums included\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 52 / printed p. 44). The FCB encoding admits no other combination: word 2 bits 10\u201311 are \"00 \u2013 No block sequence words / 10 \u2013 Block sequence word is present, no check sums. / 11 \u2013 Block sequence word is present, check sums are present (input) or are to be computed (output)\" \u2014 there is no state for check sums without a sequence word (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 74 / printed p. 66). J mentions neither restriction, saying only \"Block checksums are not checked unless the Environment SPECIF card option CKSUMS is selected\" (J 02.07.06). Since \"If no specification is made the file is assumed to be BCD\" (J 02.06.04), and every file in the J sample is BCD, the practical conclusion is that on a normal COMTRAN file SEQ and CKSUMS are inoperative and the compiler passes them through unchecked \u2014 *inference*, from the card mapping plus the IOCS column definitions. Worth recording in \u00a77 alongside the option table.\n\n*LABELN / non-standard labels.* IOCS still reads and writes the labels and their EOF marks and still maintains the block counter, but the **content** check passes to the user: entry `MYLBLS + 1` \"To check trailer label, input file\" is the routine that sees the trailer image (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 33 / printed p. 25; see open question 46). *Inference:* IOCS cannot itself perform the block-count comparison on a non-standard trailer, because it does not know where in the image the count lies; the manual scopes the check to \"every trailer **written by the system**\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 32 / printed p. 24), and checking is exactly the half of the labeling routines the package replaces (\"using different routines to check and prepare label images\", same page). J corroborates the general delegation: the SERIAL/REEL/RETAIN information \"is used by IOCS in checking and preparing standard labels (but not non-standard labels) and may be used by the programmer in checking and preparing non-standard labels\" (J 02.06.12) \u2014 a statement about those three fields rather than the block count, but the same division of labour. What IOCS still supplies is the running counter in FCB word 4 bits 3\u201317, reachable off index register 2 at every entry, so a FOR LABEL routine can do the comparison itself. Countervailing evidence, which the manual never disambiguates: item 4 of the non-standard-package list, \"All the system actions and messages will apply\", could be read as retaining it. **Confidence on this sub-point: probable.**\n\n*Can F p. 41 be mapped onto IOCS at all?* F says \"The record count is compared with the count in the end-of-file label if label records are present and if end of file has been reached. If the count does not agree, notification is given through external display. If the tape is not at end of file the record count is ignored\" (F p. 41), the count having been started at OPEN (\"Checking is performed, and a record count is initiated\", F p. 39, for input and output alike) and maintained at FILE (\"A count of the number of records written is maintained\", F p. 41). Four of its five elements fail against IOCS, and the failure of the first is definitional. **Unit:** IOCS's glossary fixes \"record\" in the physical sense \u2014 \"BLOCK \u2014 A physical record; that is, a tape record, a card, or a line of print\"; \"BLOCK COUNT \u2014 The number of blocks processed on the current reel of a file, kept in the corresponding File Control Block\"; against \"LOGICAL RECORD \u2014 Data which, as a group, has some logical significance to the user\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF pp. 83, 85 / printed pp. 75, 77). Every count IOCS keeps is of physical records, and **no logical-record count appears in any standard label**. The only two other places IOCS counts \"records\" are tape-repositioning data \u2014 \"If the Unit Control Block is for a tape unit, it also contains counts which specify the current position of the tape (file count, and record count within the file)\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 49 / printed p. 41) \u2014 and the reel-removal printout \"UNIT XXXXX (file name) REEL XXXX \u2013 XXXXX RECORDS\" (external: C28-6100-2, PDF p. 67 / printed p. 59); neither is a label field and neither is compared with anything. *Inference:* F's record count coincides with IOCS's block count only on an unblocked file, one logical record per block; for any COMTRAN file whose BLOCKSIZE holds more than one record, F's quantity denotes nothing IOCS keeps. **Timing:** F at CLOSE, IOCS inside READ when the trailer is read. **Reporting:** F \"notification \u2026 through external display\", IOCS a sequence-error ERR exit with MQ prefix 1 and no printed message at all. **Scope:** F only at end of file, IOCS every input reel. What *does* survive is F's conditional \"if label records are present\" \u2014 precisely right, since outside standard labels there is nothing to compare \u2014 and F's output-side clause \"If specified by the programmer, an end-of-file label containing the record count is written\", which is structurally right: IOCS writes the `1EOFbb` trailer at close with a count in word 2 at exactly that point, only it counts blocks.\n\n*Resolution for the language definition.* Answer to the question as put: **no** \u2014 under LABELN the check is whatever the FOR LABEL routine performs, and unlabeled files get none. F p. 41's input-side close-time record-count comparison should be recorded not as an F/J divergence proper \u2014 J diverges from nothing, it defers, \"A file is referenced by the commands OPEN and CLOSE to initiate and complete file activity. (See Commercial Translator General Information manual).\" (J 02.07.01), leaving F's text as J's only prose on the verb \u2014 but as **an F description the object-time system cannot honour**, and dropped from the implemented semantics. The compiled sample bears that out: the GET calling sequence is four words with no counter increment (`00200 TSX IOC)8,4` / `PZE INPUTMASTER,,SYS)260` / `PZE GN)058,,SYS)283` / `IOCTN* BL)2,,15`) and `CLOSE ALL FILES` compiles to `TSX SYS)177,4` / `PZE IOC)1` with no comparison anywhere (J 90.05 listing, PDF pp. 201, 204) \u2014 *inference*: the field-test compiler maintained no record count at all. What a COMTRAN compiler must document instead is that a labeled input file's per-reel **block** count is verified automatically by IOCS, whatever SEQ says, and that a mismatch is delivered to the file's ON ERROR exit, resumable, with no message. **Confidence: certain** on the main claim, on the ON ERROR routing and on the block-versus-record finding; **probable** on the LABELN delegation; the record \u2194 block identification and the non-implementation of F's count are *inference*. Found and downloaded three PDFs to the scratchpad `external/` folder:\n\n1. IBM 705 Reference Manual, Form A22-6506-0 (May 1959) \u2014 bitsavers.org/pdf/ibm/705/A22-6506-0_705_Reference_Man_May59.pdf. Figure 2, \"IBM 705 Character Code Chart,\" page 8, is a clean typeset table that explicitly prints the labels \"Plus Zero\" (12-0), \"Minus Zero\" (11-0), \"Record Mark\" (0-8-2, i.e. zone-0/digit-10), and \"Group Mark\" (12-7-8) \u2014 an exact match to COMTRAN's four inferred card codes for \u27e8+0\u27e9/\u27e8\u22120\u27e9/\u27e8rm\u27e9/\u27e8gm\u27e9. This manual is especially apt because COMTRAN's own \"Commercial (705)\" collating sequence explicitly names the 705. The chart's only other special character (card 12-4-8) is left unnamed, tagged merely \"SPECIAL CHAR.\"\n\n2. IBM 1401 Reference Manual, Form A24-1403-5 (April 1962) \u2014 bitsavers.org/pdf/ibm/1401/A24-1403-5_1401_Reference_Apr62.pdf. Figure 267, \"IBM 1401 Character Code Chart in Collating Sequence,\" printed page 170 (PDF p. 184), independently confirms the same four names with matching or directly-corresponding card codes (12-0, 11-0, 0-2-8, 12-7-8) on a different, later IBM machine \u2014 strong independent corroboration. Its own 12-4-8 special is likewise printed with no English name, just a bare hollow-square glyph.\n\n3. IBM \"Type 705 EDPM Code Chart,\" pocket reference card, form number printed on the card as 22-6642-0 (undated; archive.org catalogs it under the title \"22-6042-0\" \u2014 likely a cataloging variant/typo, not a different edition) \u2014 archive.org/download/bitsavers_ibm7052260art_981314/22-6042-0_IBM_705_EDPM_-_Code_Chart.pdf. Its back panel typesets \"Group mark prints \u25a1\" and \"Record mark prints \u2021/Blank\" for the 717 printer and Typewriter, and its printed COLLATING SEQUENCE line reproduces the exact structural skeleton of COMTRAN's own COM-sequence line (loz+gm cluster right after the period, +0 before A\u2013I, \u22120 between A\u2013I and J\u2013R, rm between J\u2013R and S\u2013Z) \u2014 strong positional corroboration that the unnamed 12-4-8 glyph occupies COMTRAN's \"lozenge\" slot, even though no name is printed for it here either. A prior owner's pen annotations on the front panel write \"Record Storage Mark\" and \"GROUP\" next to the relevant rows, but these are handwritten, not IBM print.\n\nNet result: four of the five names (Plus Zero, Minus Zero, Record Mark, Group Mark) are now confirmed by two-to-three independent, dated, typeset IBM primary sources with matching card/BCD codes. The fifth \u2014 COMTRAN's \"lozenge\" \u2014 remains unnamed in every period IBM document checked (both dedicated code charts and the pocket reference card); modern hobbyist pages that do call it \"lozenge\" cite no IBM source for the term. Both charts \u2014 and an undated IBM 705 EDPM pocket \"Code Chart\" (Form 22-6642-0) whose printed COLLATING SEQUENCE line structurally mirrors COMTRAN's own COM sequence slot-for-slot \u2014 leave the fifth character (card code 12-4-8, printed only as an unlabeled \"SPECIAL CHAR\" box/bowtie glyph) without any English name in any period source found, so \"lozenge\" remains *inference*, corroborated by matching card code and sequence position but still unconfirmed by name.", "confidence": "probable"}, "verdict": {"verdict": "CORRECTED", "problems": ["FACTUAL ERROR (load-bearing): claim #4 and the proposed annotation give the 705 group mark's card code as 12-7-8.
